# Agents

Agents running in Clubmate terminals (Claude Code, Codex, scripts) can drive Clubmate through one registry of commands: core primitives (workspaces, sessions, processes, focus) and actions that plugins declare (open a file, switch a worktree, close a terminal, ...).

## What agents can do

`clubmate command list --agent` shows everything an agent may run, and `clubmate command describe <id>` shows one command's arguments.

- Workspaces: list, open folders, open the home workspace.
- Sessions: list, start a login shell, type into one (`session.send`), read its recent output (`session.read`), kill one.
- Processes: the process tree of every session and of processes plugins register (agent processes).
- UI: show a section, focus one of its slots or a node in it (`ui.focus`).
- Notifications: `notify.post` shows a macOS notification (needs input, permission, question, done, stuck, info), skipped while the user looks at the session; see [notifications](notifications.md).
- Backlog: `backlog_list`, `backlog_claim`, `backlog_update`, `backlog_done` and friends share a per-workspace task list with the user; see [backlog](backlog.md).
- Plugin actions marked for agents. Some are generic capabilities, so an agent need not know which plugin provides them: `editor.open`, `editor.quickfix`, `editor.reveal`, `terminal.new`, `terminal.close`.
- Git worktrees: `git.worktrees` lists every git folder's worktrees, `git.worktree-switch` switches a folder to one of them, `git.worktree-new` creates `.worktrees/<branch>` and switches to it (unless `switch` is false), and `git.worktree-remove` removes a linked worktree (never the main checkout or one with uncommitted changes). Both run the repo's [worktree hooks](worktree-hooks.md). Create and remove return `{id}` at once; `git.worktree-op-status id=…` waits up to 1 s for the outcome (the app instead gets the `git.outcome` event).
- Agent sessions: `agents.sessions` lists the workspace's agent sessions with their status, `parentSession`, `childStatus` (a done or idle orchestrator whose child sessions still run or wait; a lander's reviewer counts as its child while its review round runs, see [Gates](#gates)), `needsYou` and `needsYouSince` (see [Needs you](#needs-you)), `activity` (`{title, at}` only for a `done` session waiting for its own reminder, see below; a running session's tool calls are never named outside its transcript), `turns` (the turns it has started so far, kept on the stored record, like `modeLabel` and what `canAside` reads, so a row does not change when its transcript is loaded or dropped from memory; Backlog's Owner row says "running · turn 3" from it), `repo` (the short name of a repo child's repo, its full path in `repoPath`), `task` and `taskSummary` (see [Needs you](#needs-you) § Landing), `connectRequests` (§ Connect requests) and notification policy (`notify`, `notifyEffective`); `agents.sessions session=…` lists only that session (empty when the workspace has none), with `childStatus` and `notifyEffective` still worked out from the whole workspace, and is what the Mac and the phone fetch to check a notification's policy and what the phone fetches to check a permission is still pending before it answers it, so neither client pulls the whole list for those; `agents.sessions since=…` returns `{sessions, removed, cursor, delta}` instead of the list: `since=""` gives every session and a `cursor`, and an earlier `cursor` gives only the sessions whose row changed since then, any field, `childStatus` and `notifyEffective` included (`delta: true`), with the ids `removed` since then; the counter, its epoch and one entry per session live in the plugin's storage (`delta/sessions/<workspace>/…`, the shared `plugins/internal/delta` tracker that hashes each row when the list is read, so no field of a row may depend on what the plugin holds in memory), so a cursor survives a restart; with `since`, a workspace the plugin cannot read is an error rather than a list grouped without its home; a cursor from another epoch (the counter was wiped or could not be saved), or one older than the oldest removal the plugin still remembers (it keeps the last 256 removed ids per workspace), gives every session again without `delta`. The reply also carries `runs` and `dock` (see Run rows and Dock below); with `lean: true` a `delta` reply leaves out each of them unless it changed since the cursor (the tracker keeps them as two more entries, `#runs` and `#dock`), so a caller keeps the copy it holds when a key is missing; a reply that is not a delta, or one to a call without `lean`, always has both. The phone sends `lean` and keeps runs and dock with its saved list and cursor; the Mac does not. With `session=…` the cursor covers only that session: it moves past changes to the sessions the reply left out, so it is never reused for the whole list. The phone's session list and the Mac's agent counts and notifications load the whole list once per workspace and then only what changed on each `agents.changed`; the phone's Agents and Backlog lists load again when they come back on screen (a pop, a tab switch) only when their last load is 10 s old or older or failed, since the events and a reconnect keep them current, so a pop does not re-render the list during its animation; `agents.notify session=… policy=all|needs-input|muted` sets the policy of the caller's own session or one it started (see [notifications](notifications.md#per-session-policy)), and `agents.transcript session=…` reads one session's transcript from Clubmate's own log (`last=true` keeps only the last turn and leaves out the modes, config options and commands catalogs; `compact=true` drops the images in tool calls' content, the image data in user prompts and the commands catalog; tool calls never carry `rawOutput` or `_meta`, which stay only in the log; `since=<cursor>` returns only what changed after an earlier result's `cursor`).
- Child agent sessions (no approval): `agents.new` starts a session, optionally in a new worktree `.worktrees/<branch>` made from the caller's worktree without switching the workspace, and sends it a first `prompt` (`notify` sets its notification policy); `agents.prompt` sends any session a follow-up (added to the running turn when the agent supports steering, else queued) and returns `{turn}` when it started a turn, `null` otherwise; `agents.cancel` stops a running turn (with `turn`, the turn number the caller saw from `turns`, it is refused with reason `stale` and stops nothing unless that turn is still running or the agent woke itself after it (a background task's follow-up): a late Stop from the phone never stops the next turn, nor a plain background wait left after the turn it meant ended; `agents.stop-subagent` takes the same `turn` and is refused unless the subagent was started in that turn and still works, so it reaches a subagent that runs on after its turn ended; the phone sends `turn` with both, fixed when Stop is tapped, see [remote § Phone outbox](remote.md#phone-outbox)); `agents.permission` answers a permission request and `agents.elicitation` a question (AskUserQuestion, an MCP form or sign-in link), but only of sessions the calling agent session started (directly or through its children); the paired phone (through the `remote` plugin) and the voice plugin answer any session's permission requests and questions as the user; `agents.wait` waits without polling until one of the listed sessions stops running (done, waiting for permission, asking a question, error or closed), up to 300 s per call, and returns their status with the pending permission's options; it also returns early with the user's queued messages to the calling session, which then leave the queue and show in its transcript as your message in the running turn.
- Failed turns: a turn the agent ends with an internal error (`-32603`, e.g. Claude Code's "ended the turn without returning results for tool calls" when the advisor tool is rate-limited) is retried once, 3 s later, within the same turn: the transcript shows a notice and a Clubmate message asking the agent to continue. When every tool call that error lists was still open when a message was steered into the turn (yours or Clubmate's; it interrupts a server tool such as the advisor, which then never returns), the turn is not failed and not retried: it ends `done`. A second failure leaves the session in error, as any other error does. Network errors ("No response from API", "Connection refused", `ECONNREFUSED`) are not retried within the turn, since Claude Code has already waited and retried; see the network error paragraph under orchestration.
- Handoffs: `agents.handoff session=… note=…` continues a session that is not running in a fresh one (same worktree, agent, parent, advisor, title, graph run, notification policy, AFK mode, Handoff option and starting cron job or trigger), seeded with a brief from Clubmate's log (the task — on a chain of handoffs the first session's task, found by following `handoffFrom` back — the last prompt and reply, the last turn's stop reason and checkpoint, and how to read the full transcript) followed by `note`; the old session is closed but can be reopened, and each links to the other. The sessions the old one started move to the new one (`parentSession`), so their wake-ups, permissions and questions reach it. Agents may hand off only sessions they started. In the app, a session's menu has "Hand Off to New Session", and a session whose agent cannot resume offers "Hand off to a new session". A closed session that was not handed off carries `reopen` in `agents.sessions`, the action its closed footer offers as `{id, label, icon, command, args}`: "Reopen" (`agents.resume`) for that one session, "Restore" (`agents.restore`) on a task's parent, or "Hand off to a new session" (`agents.handoff`) when its agent cannot resume; the Mac builds its button from the same action and the phone shows it in the closed session's footer, so neither client decides which one applies.
- Automatic handoff (off by default, to try out): the Handoff option in a top-level session's composer options, or `cm command run agents.config session=<id> id=handoff value=<off|landed|context|either>` (which also takes a child session), lets Clubmate hand the session off by itself when a turn ends. `landed` fires once the session has landed into its repo's default branch through `git_land` (git reports it with `agents.land-done`) and none of its children is running or waiting; `context` fires when the agent's last `usage_update` put the context above 250k tokens (an agent that sends no usage never fires it); `either` takes whichever comes first. A turn that leaves something a handoff would drop or strand is skipped and checked again at the next turn end: queued prompts, open items, a composer draft, asides, a held turn or tool call, a limit wait, a pending land review, a restricted start (system prompt, disallowed tools, start mode), a missing worktree, a gate reviewer, night and aside sessions. Clubmate first prompts the session for handoff notes (`[Clubmate handoff notes]`: where its state lives, open children, unwritten rulings, what comes next); when that turn ends, or the first turn end after it that nothing blocks, it hands off with the brief built from the transcript without the notes turn, the notes in full and an instruction to start no new work until a prompt, a child or a verdict asks for it. The notes turn ends like any turn (notification, parent wake-up). Both transcripts get a notice ("Handed off to a new session: context 412k"); the option moves to the new session, which starts with no landing counted.
- Attribution: `agents.blame path=…` (or `commit=…`) tells which agent session, turn and prompt made a file's lines or a commit; `agents.trace rev=…` exports a commit's or a range's changed lines as [Agent Trace](https://agent-trace.dev/) records; see [History and attribution](#history-and-attribution).
- Moving a session: `agents.export session=… dir=…` writes a session bundle, `agents.import dir=…` continues it in another worktree or workspace; see [Resuming elsewhere](#resuming-elsewhere).
- Reminders and schedules: `cron.add` calls a command later or repeatedly, e.g. `agents.prompt` to your own session tomorrow morning; the job is deleted with your session unless `owner: "none"`. Watching your children needs no cron job: Clubmate wakes you when their turns end (see below). See [cron](cron.md).
- Reacting to events: `triggers.add` calls a command when a bus event arrives, e.g. `agents.prompt` to a reviewer session when another session's `agents.turn` ends; `triggers.emit` publishes your own `event.<name>`, which `cron.add` can schedule. See [triggers](triggers.md).
- Selection: a top-level session you start on the Mac (⌘N, New, Backlog's Start agent, `cm`) becomes the selected session there. One started from the phone, a cron job or a trigger, or with `background: true` (Backlog passes it for a Start from the phone), is not selected: the Mac stays on the session you work in, and the new one shows as a row in the tree. The same holds for `agents.import`, and for `agents.handoff` from the phone, a cron job or a trigger unless the Mac shows the session handed off: then the Mac follows to its successor.
- Unattended runs: a session a cron job or trigger starts with `agents.new` remembers it (`cronJob: cj_…` or `trigger: tr_…` in `agents.sessions`, set by those plugins; anyone else passing them gets `invalid_params`), and each `agents.turn` of it carries the id. `agents.turn` says `finished: true` when the session is done with its work: the turn ended `done` and no child session (any depth) is running, waiting for permission or asking its parent; a land reviewer whose round runs counts as a child of its lander here, so a turn that ends on `review pending` is not finished. A parent waiting for its children ends many turns with `finished: false`; Clubmate wakes it when the last child ends, and the turn after that is `finished: true`. So a trigger on `agents.turn` with match `{"cronJob":"cj_…","finished":true}` follows every run of a recurring job without knowing its session id. `agents.turn` doesn't say what the run changed; the triggered session checks that itself (e.g. commits since the last tag).
- Agent graphs: `agents.graph-run graph=… input=…` starts a team of child sessions from `.clubmate/graphs/<graph>.json` (one session per role, one trigger per edge) and the Graphs section shows the run; `agents.graph-list`, `agents.graph-show`, `agents.graph-stop`, `agents.graph-delete`. See [agent graphs](agent-graphs.md).
- Tasks across repos: `agents.connect-repo` asks the user for repos, `agents.task` declares a change across them in landing order, `agents.new` with `repo` and `slug` starts a child per repo (from an agent session only for a repo its task lists, so every repo child has a task to report to) and `agents.task-report` reports how its repo landed; see [Needs you](#needs-you), [Task tree](#task-tree) and [Repos card](#repos-card). Answering a connect or Land request, leaving a repo unlanded, asking its child to fix it and removing a folder stay the user's own.
- Open items: `agents.open-items` leaves the decisions and offers still open at the end of a turn for the user, as a quiet card that needs no answer; see [Open items](#open-items).
- Problem reports: `report.send` sends a report about Clubmate itself to its developers, after the user checked it in a sheet; see [problem reports](reports.md).
- Answers on your behalf: `agents.answer session=… text=…` relays your own answer to a session the agent started (its child or their descendants) as your message, without asking you again; any other session fails with "the session was not started by this agent".
- Web research: `browser.open`, `browser.snapshot`, `browser.click` and the other `browser.*` tools drive a hidden WebKit browser in the app, one set of tabs per agent session, and `browser.handoff` asks the user to solve a captcha or sign in; see [agent browser](browser.md).
- Showing a doc: after writing a spec, plan or report the user should read, `docs.open {path}` (MCP `docs_open`) shows it rendered in a popup with mermaid diagrams drawn; see [docs viewer](docs-viewer.md).
- Testing the app: `testapp.list` lists running test instances and `testapp.call instance=… method=…` calls one of them (see below).

Hidden helpers and commands not marked for agents stay out of reach.

## Setup check

The plugin checks what agents need on this Mac in the background: Node 22 or later, the Claude login, git. It runs at plugin start, when the Mac app becomes active (the app sends `agents.setup` with `check`), after a setup fix's terminal exits and after an in-session sign-in exits. Checks run one at a time and only the first failing one shows, in this order: Node, sign-in, git. After Node passes, the plugin installs claude-agent-acp in the background (the same install a first session would do), so the first session starts without the npm wait. A failed background install is not tried again before a stored time (`claude-acp.retry` in the plugin's state dir): 1 minute after the first failure, then 4 times longer each time, at most 6 hours; a success clears it. Only the first try shows the "installing" note in sessions; a retry is silent. A check that falls in the wait skips the sign-in check.

- **Node**: `node` on the PATH, else Homebrew's keg-only `node@22` (`/opt/homebrew/opt/node@22/bin/node`, `/usr/local/opt/…`). A session also starts with that keg's Node and puts its folder first on the agent's PATH. Missing: `node_missing`; older than 22: `node_old` (detail "found 18.19"). The fix is `brew install node@22` in a terminal tab when Homebrew is present, else a link to nodejs.org.
- **Sign-in**: the plugin runs the installed agent's own CLI, `node <claude-agent-acp> --cli auth status --json`, with the agent's environment (without `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, which sessions do not get either). `loggedIn: false` is `signed_out`, with the fix `claude auth login` (the agent's `--cli auth login`) in a terminal tab. Any other answer (no output, not JSON, a timeout, a failed install) is unknown: the plugin shows nothing and sign-in stays inside the session (the Sign in terminal).
- **git**: `git --version`; when `git` is the `/usr/bin/git` shim, only after `xcode-select -p` succeeds, so the check never opens the Command Line Tools dialog. Missing: `git_missing`, fix `xcode-select --install`.

The result is one field, `agents.setup` → `{state, found: {node, git}, fix: {label, run | url}, title, detail}`; every change publishes `agents.setup.changed`. state is `checking` until the first check ends, then `ok`, `node_missing`, `node_old`, `signed_out` or `git_missing`. title and detail are set only for a failing state. Clients render it and never re-derive it:

- Welcome (Mac) shows one warning row under Open folder…: title · detail and the fix button, hidden when title is empty. When the fix fails (no Home workspace, the workspace does not open, `terminals.new` fails), the error shows in place of the detail until the state changes.
- The Agents empty state (the main slot with no session open, Mac and phone) swaps its title, text, button and the caption under it for a failing state. With `git_missing` agents still run, so New session ⌘N stays next to the fix. With no failure, a workspace with no sessions yet shows "Start your first agent" with New session ⌘N and a caption: "Getting Claude ready…" while checking, "Claude Code · Node 22.11 · git 2.47" when ok.
- The fix button (`setupFix` in the empty state, `agents.setup-fix` from Welcome) opens the URL, or runs the command in a Terminals tab (`terminals.new` with `command`, which refuses an agent caller) of the workspace, or of Home from Welcome. The check runs again when the terminal starts and when it exits; the tab stays open so its output can be read. The ids of running fix terminals are stored (`setup/fixes`), so an exit after a plugin or core restart still runs the check.

`CLUBMATE_AGENTS_SETUP=<state>` (or `@<file>` holding the state, read at every check) forces a state with fake versions (tests, test-app shots); its fix runs `/usr/bin/true`, or `/bin/sh -c "$CLUBMATE_AGENTS_SETUP_FIX"`, never an install. With `CLUBMATE_AGENTS_COMMAND` set, only git is checked.

## First run hints

The first agent on a Mac gets three hints and one suggested prompt, each a line inside something that is already on the screen, each shown once per Mac (onboarding rules: `design/features/onboarding/rules.md`). The agents plugin decides them and stores what it decided (plugin storage key `firstRun`), so they survive a plugin or core restart; clients render the fields and never re-derive them.

- `firstRun` is `{emptyFirst, session, needsYou, phone}`. `emptyFirst` is true once a first session exists. `session` is the id of the first session (not an aside, a service session, a child or a run step). `needsYou` is `<session>/<request>` of the first permission request of any session. `phone` is `<session>/<turn>` of the first turn of the first session that ended without an error, was not stopped and after which `remote.offer` succeeded; when `remote.offer` fails (the remote plugin is not running), nothing is stored and the next finished turn of that session tries again. Each is set once and never changes. An install that has sessions but no stored `firstRun` (it had agents before the hints) stores all four as spent, so it gets no hints.
- **Empty Agents** (H1): "Start your first agent" with the first-run text shows while there are no sessions and `emptyFirst` is false; after that an empty Agents section shows the short text.
- **Suggested prompt**: while the first session has no turn yet, its composer has `suggestion` "Find one small bug, fix it and run the tests" and its row in `agents.sessions` has the same `suggestion` field. The Mac shows 'Try "…" · ⇥ to use' in the empty field and ⇥ puts the prompt in the field; the phone shows 'Try "…"' under the field and a tap puts it in.
- **Needs you** (H2): the permission card of the `needsYou` request carries the note "The agent waits until you answer. Anything waiting also shows under Needs you and in notifications." (`note` on the Mac's `permissionCard` node and on the phone's `card`) until it is answered. Later requests have no note.
- **Phone** (H3): when a turn of the first session ends and `phone` is not set, the plugin runs `remote.offer`, which moves `companion.state` from `unpaired` to `offer` (a paired phone or an earlier Not now keeps its state; see `docs/remote.md` § Companion state). While `companion.state` is `offer`, the Mac transcript of that session draws one dashed `offer` row under that turn: "Answer Claude from your iPhone when you step away." · Pair iPhone… (opens the pairing sheet, client action `pairDevice`) · Not now (the `phoneNotNow` event; the plugin runs `remote.dismiss-offer` with the event's caller, so an agent session cannot dismiss it). The plugin reads the state with `remote.status` at start, on `remote.changed` and when the remote plugin starts. The phone does not draw the row: it is about pairing the phone.
- **Notifications**: the Mac asks macOS for notification permission when the first agent session exists, not at launch and not at the first notification; a notification before that goes only to the inbox. When notifications are off, Settings › General shows "Notifications · Off · Open System Settings…".

The Agents section has `opensFirst` in its manifest, so a workspace opens on Agents the first time and on its last section after that (`docs/sdui-protocol.md`).

## Import your nvim config

**Import ~/.config/nvim…** in the command palette (or Import… in Settings › General › Editors) starts an agent session in the Agents section that reads `~/.config/nvim` and moves your own keymaps, options, autocmds and plugins into Clubmate's Neovim user layer (`~/.config/clubmate`, or `~/.config/clubmate-dev` for development builds), following the guide bundled at `nvim-distro/skill/SKILL.md`. It skips what Clubmate's defaults already cover and asks you before adding destructive or machine-specific mappings. It needs an open folder workspace and never changes `~/.config/nvim`. Neovim offers the same import once, the first time Code opens to edit while `~/.config/nvim` exists ([nvim integration](nvim-integration.md#import-your-nvim-config)).

## Testing the app

Agents test Clubmate in their own invisible instances instead of taking over the user's screen. `scripts/test-app.sh start [name]` (name defaults to the branch) launches the Debug build with scratch state, runtime and `XDG_*` dirs under `/tmp/clubmate-test-<name>`, its own core and its own defaults suite, in test mode (`CLUBMATE_TEST_MODE=1`): it never activates, its windows are transparent and ignore the mouse, app-modal dialogs are skipped and every approval is denied (with the launch arg `--test-approvals` the real approval alert shows instead; it can still only be denied, by ⎋ or its timeout, and the pointer is refused while it is up). Inside the app it still acts as the active app with a key window, so focus, focus rings, menu keys and SwiftUI's focused values work as on screen: `NSApp.isActive` answers true, `NSApp.keyWindow` is the workspace window, its attached sheet or a popover or panel that asked for key, `activate` does nothing, and the first key or pointer input to a window announces it as key again (SwiftUI misses the first announcement while its window is still opening). `relaunch [name] [app] [-- app args]` restarts only its app with new launch args (core, state and seed data stay; e.g. after writing `theme = …` to its `state/ghostty.conf`), `stop [name]` quits it and removes the dirs, `list` shows running ones, `cm [name] -- <args>` runs the CLI against one, with that instance's workspace as `CLUBMATE_WORKSPACE_ID`. `start [name] -- --sdui-gallery` shows the SDUI gallery with a core but no workspace. `make gallery-smoke` starts the gallery, takes one snapshot into `build/gallery-smoke` and stops it; it fails when any fixture node crashes the app.

- `ui.snapshot {out, png?, width?, height?}` (CLI `cm ui snapshot --out <dir> [--size 640x800] [--no-png]`): the app draws each of its windows itself (main window with titlebar, popovers, sheets, settings) to `w<window id>.png`, terminals included, and writes `w<window id>.json`: every rendered SDUI node with its frame in window points, its parent, its menu entries, plus every terminal's visible text and native menus listed as text. Rows of SDUI lists and trees and the option rows of a `choice` are listed too (source `row`, id `<list id>#<item id>`, role `row` plus `selected`, `focused`, `keycap` (the row draws a key cap, e.g. the chat picker's ↩ while the list has focus), `hovered`), and never flagged. The result names the key window (`key`), its first responder's class (`responder`) and the smallest node around it (`focus`), e.g. `<session>/composer/0` when the caret is in a prompt. Flags: `outside-window`, `wider-than-parent`, `overlaps-sibling`, `zero-size`, `truncated` (text nodes, measured), `text-overflow` (an inline `NSTextView` such as transcript prose whose laid-out text is taller than its frame, so it draws over what follows; source `text`, label its first line). The result's `summary` lists only flagged elements, or `0 problems`. `--size` resizes the main window first.
- `ui.present {target, shown}` (CLI `cm ui present <target> [--hide]`): opens `inbox`, `worktree`, `switcher`, `settings`, `palette`, the problem report sheet fixtures `report` and `report-over`, or the `approval` and `approval-paths` (long hyphenated paths, a Session line and a From line) fixture alerts without a click; an alert lists `alert.detail`, `alert.facts` (role `text clipped` when the arguments need more lines than the alert gives them) and `alert.accessory`, the problem report sheet `report.text` and `report.summary`; menus are never opened, they appear as text in the layout. `card:<node id>` expands a transcript tool card (`--hide` collapses it), and with it the summary row of its group; card ids (`<session>/tool/<tool call id>`) are listed by `cm ui dump` and not checked, so a wrong id changes nothing. `message:<node id>` expands a long message the same way, and `image:<message node id>#<path>` opens the image viewer.
- `ui.key {keys, focus?, caller?}` (CLI `cm ui key [--focus <node id>] <combo>...`): `focus` first makes the view under that SDUI node (an id from the snapshot layout; with duplicate ids any match, so prefer a unique one) first responder, e.g. a text field. Each combo then goes, in the key window, to the active key monitors (sidebar trees, the composer, a sheet's ⎋), else, when it has ⌘, to the window's key equivalents (view shortcuts), else, with ⌘ or ⌃, to the app menu item whose key and modifiers match exactly (after letting SwiftUI fill its items, as opening the menu bar does; AppKit's own matching of a synthetic event can ignore ⇧ and pick a ⇧⌘ item for a plain ⌘ key), else as a key down and up to the first responder; the result lists the route each combo took (`voice` when the voice key took it, `monitor`, `monitor over terminal`, `shortcut`, `menu <item>`, `menu <item> over terminal`, `menu <item> (disabled, not sent)`, `responder <class>`, or `alert`: an alert's keys go through the app's event queue, so its own ⎋ monitor sees them). A combo is `[cmd+][ctrl+][opt+][shift+]<key>`, `<key>` a US-layout letter, digit or punctuation, `return`, `tab`, `space`, `delete`, `esc`, `up`, `down`, `left` or `right`; `cm ui key --focus description o n e opt+return t w o` types two lines. ⌘N and ⌃⌘↓ reach the menu like a real key press; with an SDUI `sheet`, a form sheet or a popover up, keys go to it. Only ⎋ is sent while an alert (approvals), the problem report sheet or any app-modal window is up, so keys can't answer an approval or send a report. While a terminal has focus a combo goes only to the key monitors that pass terminals (a button's ⌘↩) and, with ⌘ or ⌃, to app menu items with their own target (the app's commands, not Edit's Paste or Select All, which act on the first responder), and is refused when none takes it, so keys never run shell commands. Tab moves AppKit focus, but SwiftUI's `.focusable` controls (buttons, choice rows) never join the Tab loop in the test app, even with `-NSTreatUnknownArgumentsAsOpen NO -AppleKeyboardUIMode 2`.
- `ui.pointer {action, node, at?, count?, to?, keys?, context?, item?, file?, dy?, caller?}` (CLI `cm ui click|hover|drag|menu|scroll|drop`): pointer input at a node of the snapshot layout (its centre, or `--at x,y` points from its top left; refused when the id is on screen more than once, or the point is outside its window or under an alert). `cm ui click <node> [--count 2]` posts mouse down and up into the app's event queue, so local event monitors and tracking loops see them as real clicks; `cm ui hover <node>` sends mouse entered, moved and exited to the tracking areas under and no longer under the point (posted mouse moves never reach SwiftUI's `onHover`; SwiftUI also drops hover while the system cursor is hidden, e.g. while you type or while Universal Control has the pointer on another device, so in test mode `NSCursor` answers that it is never hidden); `cm ui drag <from> <to>` presses, moves in ten steps and releases inside one window (no test uses `--count 2` yet); `--keys cmd` (or `shift+opt`…) holds modifiers. `cm ui scroll <node> --dy N` turns the wheel N lines (negative: up) with `--keys` held: the event is built from a CGEvent tagged in its `eventSourceUserData` and posted into the queue, and while it is pending `NSEvent.window` and `locationInWindow` answer the node's window and point for that tag (the queue rebuilds the NSEvent from its CGEvent, so nothing attached to the NSEvent survives), so local monitors (the graph's ⌘/⌃+wheel zoom) and the view under the point see it like a real wheel; no momentum or precise deltas. A menu a click opens (a SwiftUI `Menu`, a `Picker`, a context menu with `cm ui menu <node> --context`) is read when it starts tracking, picked when `cm ui menu <node> '<item> > <sub item>'` names an enabled item, and closed at once, so no menu shows; the result's `menu` lists its items with `enabled`, `checked` (✓) and `children`. `cm ui drop <node> <file>` hands the file to the drop view under the point (SwiftUI's sits beside the hit-test chain) as a drag from Finder would, through a private pasteboard; a real drag session, the menu's look, Quick Look, trackpad scrolling phases and gestures (pinch) are not covered. Clicks carry `caller` like `ui.key`.
- Over MCP: `testapp.call` proxies `ui.snapshot`, `ui.present`, `ui.key`, `ui.pointer`, `ui.get`, `ui.event` and `command.run` (both always as an agent call), `command.list`, `workspace.list` and `workspace.open` to `/tmp/clubmate-test-<instance>`; `ui.snapshot`'s `out` defaults to and must stay inside `/tmp/clubmate-test-<instance>/snapshots`, and a `ui.pointer` drop's `file` must be inside `/tmp/clubmate-test-<instance>`.
- `make mac-ui-test [RUN=<regexp>]` builds the fake agent and runs `internal/macui` against the Debug app (`CLUBMATE_TEST_APP`; without it the tests skip): each test starts its own instance on a scratch repo under `build/mac-ui/<name>`, drives it with `ui key` and `ui pointer` and asserts on the snapshot (focused node, row state, windows, menus) and on plugin commands. It covers menu keys in Agents, Triggers and Cron, the graph Trust sheet's keys, question card clicks and ⌘↩ (in Agents and in the Code chat with a review open), the Open items card and queue, an image link in a transcript, the inbox's graph pin arriving while the inbox is open, the Start agent popover, a file drop and the tag chips in Backlog, the title picker, worktree and folder sheets, the Connect card, Archive task, the approval alert (with its From line) over the problem report sheet, the drawn views (trigger flow edges, lanes and runstrip keys, the Runs fold per cron job, a graph History move, ⌘-wheel zoom), row hover, a drag on the sidebar divider (`<slot>/resize`) and the Settings font menus. `RUN` goes through make, so a `$` in it is eaten: write `RUN='TestA|TestB'`, not `TestA$|TestB`. It runs under `caffeinate -d -u`, but the wheel tests don't need an awake display: with the display asleep (an idle Mac at night, or the lid closed with no external display) a wheel event still reaches the scroll view under the pointer but AppKit doesn't move it, so while every online display is asleep (or none is online) and no scroll view the test wheel reached has moved after it settles, `ui scroll` scrolls the first one that can move by the wheel's lines. Which view gets the wheel, and ⌘/ctrl wheels going to the zoom monitor, still go through AppKit's own routing, and with a display awake the wheel tests see only AppKit's scrolling.

Multi-repo landing check: `scripts/mr-landing-check.sh run` builds two scratch repos under `build/mr-landing/repos`, `pr-flow` (its CLAUDE.md: land through a pull request, push to its bare `origin`, never merge locally) and `plain` (no instructions), starts the test instance `mr-landing` with a workspace of both, starts an orchestrator with finish mode merge and asks it to add a README line in both repos as a task across repos. It waits until both repos settle and the orchestrator's turn ends, so a merge the orchestrator makes after its children landed counts, and checks: `pr-flow` reads `prOpen` or `onBranch` with its rule corrected to `pr`, its `main` has no merge and `origin` has `feat/mrcheck` when a pull request is claimed; `plain` reads `onBranch` with `main` untouched; both `feat/mrcheck` branches hold the line. It writes `task.json`, `sessions.jsonl` and a snapshot of the orchestrator (`shots/`) next to the repos and exits non-zero on a failed check. `MR_AGENT=fake` (the default) drives every session with fakeagent's `run` steps, which run a shell line in the session's folder with its `CLUBMATE_AGENT_SESSION` so `clubmate command run` calls come from that session; `MR_AGENT=haiku` (or another model name) runs real Claude on that model, picked with `agents.config` before the first prompt. `MR_KEEP=1` leaves the instance running, `MR_APP` names another `Clubmate Dev.app`, `MR_TIMEOUT` the seconds to wait (120 fake, 1500 real), `stop` stops it. Each scratch repo's `.claude/settings.json` lists every CLAUDE.md above `build/` in `claudeMdExcludes`, so a real agent reads only the repo's own rules and not Clubmate's "squash into main".

A test instance stays offline: in test mode (outside release builds) the plugins never start `claude -p`, so graph step and turn summaries and the voice cleanup are skipped as when the CLI is missing (`plugins/internal/claudecli`). `CLUBMATE_TEST_CLAUDE=1` (read only in test mode) lets them start the `claude` found on `PATH` again; `scripts/companion-e2e/run.sh` sets it for its fake `claude`.

The user's own app never answers `ui.snapshot`, `ui.present`, `ui.key` or `ui.pointer`: only a test-mode app registers them, and test mode needs a dev build with non-default state and runtime dirs.

## Approvals

Destructive actions (closing or killing sessions, typing into a session, answering an agent session as you, restarting the editor, killing orphan processes, removing a worktree) ask you first. Clubmate shows an alert naming the action, the workspace, the targeted session, the agent session that asked (a "From:" line with its title and id, e.g. `git.worktree-remove` from a child session; just the id when Clubmate can't find it in that workspace) and every argument in full, with Allow and Deny. Deny is the default. If nobody answers within 60 seconds, or the app isn't open, the action is refused.

An agent session calling these tools is the parent of the sessions it starts with `agents.new` (Clubmate knows the caller from `CLUBMATE_AGENT_SESSION`, which every agent session has). A child starts with the parent's mode, model and effort, not the worktree's remembered ones (the model is the one `model` on `agents.new` names when given, the effort the one `effort` names), and at most 8 children of one parent run or wait for permission at once (a child whose finished turn a Done gate holds counts only while it works). `model: "auto"` lets Clubmate pick the model for the experiment in [Outcomes](#outcomes); `kind` and `size` label what the child is for. A model or effort level the agent doesn't offer leaves a notice in the transcript with the offered values and the same text in the session's `startNotice` (`agents.sessions`), so the parent sees it; the real Claude adapter offers `default`, `opus`, `fable` (Fable 5.2), `sonnet`, `haiku` and full ids such as `claude-fable-5-1`, and effort `default`, `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh`, `max` for models that have it. A session an agent starts where it is not the parent (in another workspace, or through a cron job or trigger with `owner: "none"` that agent made) is top-level but not yours: it records that agent in `startedBy`, takes no AFK default, and one agent keeps at most 8 such sessions open at once (closing one frees its place). Agents without an agent session (Claude in a plain terminal, `cm --agent`) count as one such agent, `startedBy: "unknown"`; a graph run or another plugin starting a session is not one. A prompt an agent sends through `agents.prompt` or `agents.new` is logged as coming from that agent (`agent:<session id>`) and reaches the target with `[From Clubmate agent session "<title>" (<id>), not from the user]` in front, so it never passes for your approval; from an agent without an agent session it is logged as `agent:unknown` and starts with `[From another agent session, not from the user]`. Prompting an idle child also counts against the limit of 8. Agents without an agent session (Claude in a terminal, `clubmate command run --agent`) start top-level sessions that aren't yours (above) and cannot answer permission requests.

Sessions an agent starts run lean, to save tokens: a child (`parentSession`, asides excepted), a graph run's node and a gate's reviewer (`gateFor`) start without the advisor and with a light profile. The advisor is off through `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_ADVISOR_TOOL=1` in the session's env: leaving `advisorModel` out falls back to the user's `~/.claude/settings.json`, and an empty one overrides it but still sends a deferred standby advisor; its Advisor picker reads Default, which for these sessions means off, and picking a model there turns it on for that session from its next start or resume. The profile goes as Claude Code flag settings over the user's own: every plugin enabled in the user's `settings.json` (`$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` honoured) is set to `false` in `enabledPlugins`, `disableClaudeAiConnectors` is on, and `strictMcpConfig` leaves only Clubmate's own MCP server, passed in `mcpServers` even where Clubmate is registered with `claude mcp`, and Claude in Chrome where the session has it; a repo's `.mcp.json` servers are dropped too. The setting sources stay, so the user's and the project's CLAUDE.md, auto memory, permission rules and default mode, hooks, user and repo skills, and Clubmate's own plugin are kept; plugins a project's settings enable are left on. Measured 18 Nov through the ACP adapter on this repo: a child's first call went from 56.8k to 46.2k tokens with Claude in Chrome, 55.1k to 44.3k without. Top-level sessions (yours, a cron job's, a trigger's) and asides keep the advisor and the full profile, and a child made before this keeps the advisor it inherited but gets the light profile on its next resume.

A child in its own worktree merges its finished, tested work into its parent's branch in the parent's worktree, never into `main`; a child sharing its parent's worktree, or whose parent is on the default branch, only commits and the parent merges. Children never remove worktrees or branches: once a child's turn ends with its branch merged into the parent's branch and its worktree clean and unused, Clubmate closes the child (it stays listed as Closed) and removes its worktree and branch itself, without asking. Clubmate never closes a session on its own (merged, worktree gone, a reviewer's round over) while a session below it at any depth runs, waits or asks its parent: a closed session would drop that session's report, so its parent would never be woken and never post `done`. It doesn't when removing would lose something git can't bring back: an ignored secret file no other worktree holds identically, a commit on no branch, or an unsaved buffer of it in any Neovim of the workspace (or a Neovim that doesn't answer). Then the worktree stays, the child shows `Couldn't remove the merged worktree: …` with the reason, nothing retries it, and you remove it from the title picker, which lists what would go. A session that has children is told to write to you only the children's questions that need your answer, to answer their permission requests itself with `agents.permission`, and to pass your answers on with `agents.answer` rather than paraphrasing them.

Clubmate wakes an orchestrator when a child's turn ends: once a child is done, ends in an error or waits for permission, and the parent is not in a turn of its own, the parent gets `[Clubmate] Child "<title>" (<id>) is <status>.` (the title's first line, quoted), one line per child in one message when several finish together (within a second, `CLUBMATE_AGENTS_WAKE_DELAY` overrides it, or while the parent's turn was still running). A child the parent already saw through `agents.wait` is not reported again, so an orchestrator that waits on its children is not woken twice. A suspended parent is resumed for the message like for any prompt. Pending wake-ups live in memory and are lost if the plugin restarts. A hand-off passes the old session's pending wake-ups to the new one, including a child's report that lands while the hand-off runs or after it (the closed session forwards it to the session it handed off to), and an `agents.wait` the old session still has open counts as the new session's wait.

When Clubmate restarts (an update, a core handover) while a turn is running, the turn is cut off. On start, the agents plugin sends `[Clubmate] Your turn was cut off by a restart; continue from where you stopped.` to every cut-off session that is not closed, including one that was waiting for a permission or an answer (it asks again), so every session picks up its work without you. The plugin saves session records in the background; on a normal stop (the core's `plugin.shutdown`, sent on a plugin restart, a disable or a core stop) it first writes the saves that are still waiting, for at most 1.5 s, inside the 2 s the core gives that call, so a turn that ended or was held just before the stop is not cut off when Clubmate starts again. A crash or a kill in that window still loses the last save. `CLUBMATE_AGENTS_SAVE_HOLD` delays each save in tests. Messages you queued while the turn ran are stored with the session and survive the restart (a queued image is kept as a file in the plugin's state, under `uploads/<session>`, and read back when it is sent; only a file inside that folder and up to 10 MiB is read, any other image path is sent empty): a cut-off session gets them in the same continue prompt, after the restart line, and any other session keeps them queued as before. A queue too long to store with the session (over 64 KiB as JSON) is written to `uploads/<session>/queue.json` instead and read back on start; a file that cannot be read back is kept as `queue.json.bad`. A prompt taken off the queue stays stored with the session until it goes to the agent; the transcript line of a prompt is written right before it is sent and marks it sent. A turn cut off before that point gets its prompt back: an agent that exits or a cancel puts it back at the front of the queue instead of sending it (a close drops it with the queue), a turn that reaches the send while the plugin shuts down does not send, and after a restart the session sends it without the restart line, since nothing was cut. A prompt that comes back is sent like any queued message: together with the messages queued behind it from the same sender (and the same voice flag) as one turn, their texts joined by a blank line, so with A in flight and B queued the agent gets "A" and "B" in one prompt; a review prompt always goes on its own. A prompt whose transcript line was written counts as sent, so a restart never sends it twice; the line also names the queued messages merged into it, so a restart drops those from the queue even when the session's record was not saved before the send; a restart in the instant between that line and the send loses it. `CLUBMATE_AGENTS_SEND_HOLD` holds a turn before that point in tests.

When a turn ends with a network error ("No response from API", "Connection refused", `ECONNREFUSED`, "Can't reach the API server", "No internet route", "Unable to connect to API ("), an overload or a rate limit in a session that is in AFK mode or belongs to a live orchestration (never a night session), Clubmate sends it `[Clubmate] Your turn ended with an error (<reason>). Continue the task from where you stopped.` 1 min later (`CLUBMATE_AGENTS_NET_RETRY_AFTER` overrides the minute), then after 2, 4 and 8 min and every 10 min after that, up to 8 times; the transcript shows a notice with the attempt ("Network error; retrying in …", "Claude is temporarily overloaded; retrying in …"). A retry that falls due while a follow-up cycle runs (a background command's follow-up, also one that began as the failed turn ended) waits for the cycle to end and is sent then. A turn that ends without an error resets the count. While a retry is pending, the parent is not woken for the child's error; once the retries run out, the session stays in error with a "…; stopped retrying" notice and the parent is woken as for any error. Any other session stays in error; send it a message to continue. Pending retries live in memory and are lost if the plugin restarts.

When a turn ends on the subscription's usage limit ("You've hit your …" or "You've reached your …" — not out of credits, a seat or an org without usage, which never reset), every session except a night session waits for the reset, whatever its mode. The reset time comes from the error text ("resets 3pm (Europe/Warsaw)", "resets Nov 16 at 11am (Europe/Warsaw)"), else from a usage window that is full (`agents.usage`); with neither, the session just stays in error. The wait is kept on the session's record (`limitWait {at, job, attempts, fired}` in `agents.sessions`, `at` = reset + 90 s, `CLUBMATE_AGENTS_LIMIT_MARGIN` overrides the margin), and the agents plugin adds one one-shot cron job per workspace and reset time, "Continue sessions after the usage limit" (`agents.limit-resume`, `createdBy plugin:agents`, no owner, a missed run runs once on wake). When it fires, every session of the workspace whose wait is due and that is still in error, not closed and not running gets `[Clubmate] Your usage limit has reset. Continue the task from where you stopped.`; the cron job and the record both survive a restart, and the plugin continues waits that fell due while it was down when it starts. A continue that hits the limit again waits again, up to 3 continues; then the session stays in error with "Stopped by the usage limit again; stopped waiting". Any other turn start (a prompt by hand, a parent's wake) drops the wait, and so does "Don't continue" (`agents.limit-cancel`), which also deletes the job when no other session waits on it. While a session waits, its parent is not woken for its error; the user's own error notification is still posted. A waiting session reads "Stopped by the usage limit · resumes 19:04" (`failure.line`, the word "resumes 19:04"), with the weekday instead ("resumes Wed") when the continue is a day or more away. It does not need you: it is in the `paused` group (§ Groups) with `paused: true` and `pausedUntil`, and leaves it when its wait goes (a continue starts its turn, "Don't continue", the last of the 3 continues failing, a prompt by hand).

When a turn ends because the agent needs a sign-in (ACP `auth_required`, or an internal error with `errorKind` `authentication_failed` or `oauth_org_not_allowed`, e.g. "Failed to authenticate: OAuth session expired and could not be refreshed"), and also when a follow-up cycle the agent starts by itself (a background task's notification) ends with such a message (its last agent text starts with "Failed to authenticate"; the adapter sends no text for the "run /login" ones), the session goes to error with "Sign in" (or "Sign in to continue using Claude."), the parent is woken as for any error, and the record keeps `authWait: true` in `agents.sessions` (not for a night session). All sessions share one Claude login, so once it works again they all continue: when Clubmate's "Sign in" terminal exits, and when a turn (not cancelled or superseded) or a follow-up cycle of any session ends without an error (for example after `/login` in a terminal and one message typed by hand), every session with `authWait` that is still in error, not closed and not running gets `[Clubmate] Your turn ended with an error (<reason>). Continue the task from where you stopped.`, as "Retry" sends it. `authWait` survives a restart; the next turn end of the session sets or clears it. The cycle's failure is logged as a second `turn_end` of that turn, with its error; the turn keeps its stop reason. Why the login stops working is decided inside Claude Code, not by Clubmate: all sessions fail within a few minutes of each other and none works again until `/login`.

AFK mode is for when you step away. Turn it on with the AFK item in the composer options menu of a top-level session, or tell the agent you are away ("я афк") and it turns it on itself with `agents.afk on=true`; "я вернулся" turns it off. The record keeps who turned it on in `afkBy`: empty when you did (the composer menu, the phone, `cm` outside an agent session, `agents.afk-default`, `agents.new afk:true` from you, a cron job or a trigger), the session id when an agent did (`agents.afk` or `agents.new afk:true` from a session); you turning it on clears it, an agent turning it on again leaves yours. Both read the same everywhere except [connect requests](#needs-you): only AFK you turned on connects a path without asking. While it is on, each prompt of that session and of all its child sessions tells the agent to work without asking you, make and note its own decisions, hand large work to child sessions and subagents, and finish with one short report of commits, decisions and open questions. Turning it on while a turn runs also adds the instruction to that turn and to the running turns of the session's child sessions (steering), so they don't wait for the next prompt. The instruction is not shown in the transcript. `agents.afk-default on=true|false` sets whether new top-level sessions you start start in AFK mode: yours from the app, the phone, `cm`, another plugin, a graph run, or a cron job or trigger you made (or a plugin made), not one an agent starts outside its own tree (`startedBy`, above), which starts without AFK unless its `agents.new` says `afk: true`, and then `afkBy` names that agent. `agents.new` takes `afk: true|false` (from any caller, e.g. a cron job's or trigger's args) to start that session in or out of AFK mode from its first prompt, so an unattended session doesn't depend on the agent turning AFK mode on for itself; `finish: ask|merge` sets a top-level session's finish mode instead of the default, but only from you (the app, the phone, the CLI without `--agent` outside an agent session): agents, a plain `cm` from an agent's shell, cron jobs, triggers and other plugins get `invalid_params`, since merging into main without asking stays your call.

From the Agents sidebar you can run a task in its own worktree end to end: press "New in worktree…", type a branch name and press Start (or Return) to start a session in a new `.worktrees/<branch>` made from the workspace's worktree (as `agents.new` with `branch`). When its turn is done, "Review changes" in the session header opens the diff; with Finish set to "Merge to main" the agent squashes its branch into `main` itself once the tests pass.

Claude in Chrome: Claude Code started through the SDK, as Clubmate starts it, ignores its "enabled by default" setting, so a Claude session gets the `claude-in-chrome` tools only because Clubmate passes `--chrome` (`_meta.claudeCode.options.extraArgs` on `session/new` and `session/resume`). It does so while `claudeInChromeDefaultEnabled` is true in `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR/.claude.json` (or `~/.claude.json`), the setting `/chrome` → "Enabled by default" writes, and only for a session you started yourself or a child of one: never for a service session, nor for one a cron job, a trigger, a graph run or another agent (`startedBy`) started, nor their children, since those run unattended in your signed-in browser.

"Context" (⌘I) in a Claude session's header shows what the session sees instead of its transcript, to find out why it did not do something you expected: the CLAUDE.md files it loaded (user, project, memory and nested ones, with their current text), the skills, MCP servers (and the ones that need a sign-in or failed), hook context, subagent types, tools and deferred tools it was offered, each with how often it used it; unused ones are muted. It reads the session's own Claude log (`$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` or `~/.claude`, `projects/*/<agent session id>.jsonl`) and follows it as it grows; it shows the current state, not its history. "Transcript" (⌘I) goes back.

The status bar shows how much of the Claude subscription is used: `5h 42% ↻19:03 · 7d 81% ↻Wed`, the 5-hour and weekly limits with their reset times, in the warning colour once Claude warns about a limit and in the danger colour once it is reached. The numbers come from the rate-limit reports Claude attaches to agent replies, so they appear after the first reply of an agent session, are kept across restarts, and miss usage from Claude outside Clubmate until the next reply; a limit disappears once its reset time passes. The tooltip names when they were last updated; `agents.usage` returns them.

A session's header on the Mac also estimates its share of those limits: `≈4% of 5h · ≈1% of 7d` in its meta, the rows "5h window ≈4%" and "7d window ≈1%" in its details popover (`<1%` below half a percent). The share counts the session and everything it started, its child sessions at any depth, its asides and the land gate's reviewers of its landings (`gateFor.lander`), so an orchestrator that spent little itself shows what its task cost; each child still shows its own subtree. The spend is added up before the percent is worked out, and a deleted child or aside (an aside is deleted when its parent closes) hands its spend to its parent, so the parent's number does not drop. Claude reports only the account's total, in whole percents, so the plugin splits it by cost: for each limit window it remembers the utilization at the first report it saw in that window and adds up what every session spent since (the growth of each session's `usage_update.cost`, which Claude Code reports as a running total), and a session's share is the window's growth times its part of that spend. Cost already weighs the model, so an Opus turn counts more than a Haiku one. It is an estimate: use of Claude outside Clubmate grows the window too and is spread over Clubmate's sessions, and a window seen for the first time mid-way counts only from then. The numbers are kept across restarts and vanish when their window resets; `agents.usage` returns them under `sessions` (session id to its subtree's `{five_hour, seven_day}` in percent, one decimal). A window in which the session has no share, or whose utilization has not grown since the plugin first saw it, is left out rather than shown as `<1%`. Each row of `agents.sessions` carries the same numbers as `limitShare` (a window whose share rounds to 0.0 is left out) and, for the phone, the words: `shareLabel`, the 5-hour share as `≈6% of 5h`, only from 1%, for the session subtitle; `shareDetail`, every window as the Mac header writes it (`≈6% of 5h · <1% of 7d`), for the iPad session header; and `shareWords`, each window's amount alone (`{five_hour: "≈6%", seven_day: "<1%"}`), for Session info (the rules live in the plugin; the phone does not round or pick the window). Whenever a session's shares change as shown (a different whole percent, `<1%` or gone), its row gets an `agents.changed` of kind `usage`, which re-renders the Mac header and makes the phone refetch, also when it was another session's spend (a descendant's included) or a new rate report that moved it.

`agents.usage` is also `remote`, and `remote` forwards its bus topic, so the phone gets the account's windows as well: `account` is an opaque id of the Claude account the Mac is signed in to (a hash of the account and organization ids in Claude Code's `.claude.json`, under `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` when set), so a phone paired with Macs on different accounts never mixes their numbers; when it changes on a Mac, the windows and shares start over. `accountEmail` is that account's email (`oauthAccount.emailAddress` in the same file), which the phone uses to name the account; it reaches the phone only over the Tailscale link (`/v1/command`, `/v1/events`), never the push relay, and `remote.log` doesn't record payloads. A window whose reset time has passed is left out. `alert` names the window to call out, `{window, status, used, resetsAt}`: a `rejected` window before an `allowed_warning` one, the 5-hour before the weekly; it is absent while both are allowed. Times are unix seconds, so each client writes them in its own time zone.

Agent mode is a cooperative guard for well-behaved agents, not a security boundary: any program running as your user can reach Clubmate directly.

Inside an agent session `cm` sends `CLUBMATE_AGENT_SESSION` as the `caller` of every `command.run`, `ui.event` and `ui.key`, with or without `--agent`, so a plain `cm` from the agent's shell reads as that session and not as you. A test-mode app sends a key press's caller on the `ui.event` and `command.run` its buttons send while it handles that press, so `cm ui key opt+1` on a Connect or Land card is refused like the button. A caller counts only when it is a session of the core it reached; Backlog and Graphs treat a caller they cannot check, because the session list failed, as a session. Then `agents.connect-answer`, `agents.land-answer`, `agents.land-commits`, `agents.repo-leave`, `agents.repo-fix`, `agents.folder-remove`, `agents.folder-sheet`, `agents.repos-sheet`, archiving and cleaning up (`agents.archive-plan`, `agents.archive`, `agents.archive-sheet`, `agents.restore`, `agents.cleanup-plan`, `agents.cleanup-sheet`), `agents.mode`, `agents.config`, setting `agents.finish-default` or `agents.afk-default`, answering open items (`agents.open-answer`, `agents.open-no`, `agents.open-later`, `agents.open-backlog`, `agents.open-convert`) and `agents.graph-approve`, `agents.graph-continue` and `agents.graph-trust` refuse it, and so do the buttons that are yours: in Agents sending a message, answering a permission or a question, the open items card, mode, config, delete and settings; adding or changing a backlog task; approving or trusting in Graphs. `agents.afk`, `agents.notify`, `agents.cancel` and `agents.handoff` reach only the session itself and the ones it started, as with `--agent`. Backlog changes are the session's and a prompt carries its "not from the user" line. It turns on no agent gating: hidden commands such as `agents.close` still run, and so does any other button. Test instances stay open: `scripts/test-app.sh` runs `cm` with the variable unset, and a `cm` aimed at an instance with its state dir sends a session id that core doesn't know, so both are you there, while an agent session of that instance is refused like any other. Pairing and companion access (`remote.pair`, `remote.cancel-pair`, `remote.enable`, `remote.set-port`, `remote.revoke`, `remote.set-push-relay`, `remote.set-tunnel`, `remote.set-report-inbox`, `remote.report-upload`) have no workspace to check a session in, so they refuse any caller and `--agent`, also the unknown id of a `cm` aimed at a test instance: run them there through `scripts/test-app.sh`. This is a guard against an agent answering in your place by accident, not a boundary, for `cm ui key` as for the other two: a process with your uid can unset the variable, make up an id or talk to the socket itself.

## Status

One map for both clients: the glyph (SF Symbol), the word and the tone of each state a session shows. The agents plugin decides the state once: `status` in `agents.sessions` and in the session header (`header.status`), with the parent states `childrenRunning`/`childrenWaiting` (`childStatus` in `agents.sessions`) and `asks` (a top-level `done` session with `needsYou`), `asksParent` (a `waiting` child with `asksParent` in `agents.sessions`), `review` (a `done` session with `review` in `agents.sessions`), and `open` (a `done` session with `openCount`, see [Open items](#open-items)). The Mac (sidebar, header, Graphs) and the phone copy this table and never pick a glyph or tone on their own.

| State | Glyph | Word | Tone |
|---|---|---|---|
| `running` (a turn runs, a command the agent sent to the background still runs after its turn, the agent works on its own when that command ends, or a `done` session waits for its own reminder) | `play.circle` | elapsed ("4 min"), for a reminder "next check 19:12", else "Running" | accent |
| `waiting` (permission or question pending) | `exclamationmark.circle` | "waiting" ("asking" for a question) | warning |
| `asks` (done, its last message asks you) | `exclamationmark.circle` | "asking" | warning |
| `asksParent` (a child's question waits for the session that started it, see [Needs you](#needs-you)) | `arrow.up.circle` (the phone draws `arrow.up` in its glyph circle) | "asking parent" (the phone "Asks parent: <question>", header "Asking parent") | accent |
| `review` (a top-level session whose Finish is not "Merge to main" ended its turn `done` with changes, not marked reviewed yet) | `eye.circle` (the phone draws `eye` in its glyph circle) | "review" (the phone "Review") | accent |
| `open` (done, with open items left; `status` stays `done` and `needsYou` false) | `checkmark.circle` | outlined "3 open" | none, the count in warning ink |
| `done` | `checkmark.circle` | age ("2 h ago") | none (secondary ink) |
| `idle` (no turn yet) | `circle` | "idle" | none |
| `error` (the turn failed) | `xmark.circle` | "exit 1" when the error carries the agent's exit status, else "Stopped"; the session header badge reads "Stopped · exit 1" or "Stopped · error" | danger |
| `closed` | `archivebox`; on the Mac row the glyph of the status it closed from when that was `done` (`checkmark.circle`) or `error` (`xmark.circle`), kept as `closedStatus` (sessions closed before it was kept have none) | "Closed"; when Clubmate closed it (`autoClosed`) "auto" on the Mac row, "Auto-closed" on the phone | none |
| `childrenRunning` (done or idle, a child runs) | `play.circle` | "Waiting for child sessions" | accent |
| `childrenWaiting` (done or idle, a child waits) | `exclamationmark.circle` | "Child session waiting" | warning |

One agent process serves every session of a worktree. When it exits, only the sessions with a turn, a background task or a request open turn `error`; the others keep their status, question and review mark, get no second `agents.turn`, and start a new process with their next prompt.

A failed session's header shows the error in words under the title: "Claude exited with status 1." for an exit status, "Claude stopped: <message>" for an untyped agent error (the `acp error <code>` prefix dropped), a typed failure's own sentence ("Claude is temporarily overloaded."); `error` in `agents.sessions` keeps the raw text. The plugin builds these words once: the Mac gets them in its sidebar and header nodes, the phone as `failure` `{word, badge, line}` in `agents.sessions` (row "exit 1" or "Stopped", header "Stopped · exit 1" or "Stopped · error", the sentence above, shown under the transcript), present only on an `error` session; a phone talking to a Mac that sends no `failure` shows "Stopped" and "Stopped · error".

Under the error both clients offer the same way out, built once by the plugin (`failureActions`): "Sign in" when the agent needs a login, else "Hand off to a new session" when the agent cannot resume the session, else "Continue now" and "Don't continue" (`agents.limit-cancel`) while the session waits for a usage-limit reset (the error line reads "Stopped by the usage limit · resumes 15:02", the sidebar word "resumes 15:02"), else "Retry". Retry, Continue now and a finished Sign in (`agents.resume`) send a session whose last turn failed `[Clubmate] Your turn ended with an error (<reason>). Continue the task from where you stopped.`; a session that failed to start or reattach, or a closed one, is only reattached. The Mac draws them as buttons; the phone gets them as `failure.actions` `[{id, label, icon, command?, args?}]` and shows a button for each action with a `command` the Mac lets it run (Retry → `agents.resume`, Hand off → `agents.handoff`, which opens the new session). An action without a `command` happens only on the Mac: the phone shows it as a line, "Sign in on Mac Studio". While the Mac is offline the buttons are disabled with the same reason as a permission ("Can't answer — Mac Studio is offline").

Clubmate closes a top-level session an hour after its turn ended `done` (`CLUBMATE_AGENTS_CLOSE_AFTER` overrides the hour), together with its child sessions, when nothing in that tree runs, waits or has queued messages, the session itself doesn't ask a question, nothing has open items, a waiting connect request or an open task, or stopped with an error, owns a cron job or trigger (they would be deleted with it), no session in it has an entry in a landing queue (any state; see [Landing queue](#landing-queue)), no child in it holds a branch of its own with a change that is not in its parent's branch (landed means the branch tip is an ancestor of the parent's branch, or `git merge-tree` of the two gives the parent's tree, which covers a squash; when the parent's branch is the default branch, landing in `origin/<default>` counts too, so a merged pull request counts before the local branch is updated; a branch with no net change from its fork holds nothing). The parent's branch is the branch of the parent session's worktree; when that worktree is gone it is the branch the parent held when it closed (`closedBranch`), else the branch the parent's own worktree was made on (its `forkBranch`), else the child's recorded base (`git.worktree-base`). When the child's own worktree is gone, its branch is `forkBranch` (the branch its worktree was made on, kept on the record and in `agents.sessions`), checked in the repo; when that branch is gone too, or the child runs in a folder outside git, it holds nothing. A session made before `forkBranch` was kept has none, so once its worktree is gone its branch holds nothing either. A check git can't finish (the worktree can't be read, git is killed or times out) and a parent's branch that can't be resolved keep the tree open. The verdict is kept per pair of tips (the child's branch and the parent's branches), so git checks a kept tree again only after one of them moved, and nothing was logged in any of them for that hour. So an integration orchestrator and its task children stay open while their work is not landed, also when its parent sits on the default branch. A node session of a graph run that is top-level (the run was not started by an agent session: cron, a trigger, the Graphs section, `cm`) closes this way only once its run is `stopped` in `agents.graph-list` or gone from it (`agents.graph-delete`), since a running run's edges may prompt it again, and then also when it is still `idle` because the run never reached it; the hour still counts from the node's own last activity, and while the agents plugin's graph engine can't be asked the node stays open. A node of a run started by an agent session sits under that session and closes with its tree. A child whose parent is already closed counts as top-level here and closes on its own. Sessions started with their own system prompt or tool limits (the voice session, a night session) are never closed this way. Nor is a session in `review`: when a top-level session whose Finish is not "Merge to main" ends a turn `done` and its worktree differs from its first checkpoint (what "Review changes" shows), the plugin sets `review` on it (in `agents.sessions` and the record) and keeps it until you press "Reviewed" in its header on the Mac or the phone (`agents.reviewed session=…`, remote); the hour then counts from the press. Its next turn, a close or an error clears `review`, and the turn after decides again. A top-level session whose last message asks stays `asks`; a child's `asks` holds nothing back, since it was for the session that started it (see [Needs you](#needs-you)), which got it when the child's turn ended and has since finished. A session with open items stays too, since an hour would send them all to the backlog; once they are answered or aged out the hour counts as usual. `review` is not Needs you and sends no push. A child Clubmate closes after its branch was merged is marked the same way. Such a session carries `autoClosed` in `agents.sessions` until it is reopened; the Mac sidebar row reads "auto" and its header "Closed automatically an hour after it finished", the phone "Auto-closed" and "Closed automatically". The transcript logs the close with actor `clubmate`.

Clubmate deletes a closed top-level session 30 days after anything last happened in it (`CLUBMATE_AGENTS_DELETE_AFTER` overrides the 30 days; `0` turns it off), together with its child sessions, exactly as Delete does: transcripts, checkpoint refs, uploaded files and the search index entries go. It waits while any session of that tree is open, any of them was active in those 30 days, or one holds a task that hasn't landed; night sessions go with their next night instead. A session whose parent is still kept goes with that parent.

A turn of Claude Code can end while a command it sent to the background runs on (`run_in_background`, e.g. `gh run watch`); Claude Code wakes the agent when the command ends, without a prompt. The plugin keeps such a session `running` from the end of its turn through the agent's own follow-up: a background task starts with the `backgroundTaskId` in a tool result's `_meta.claudeCode.toolResponse`, agent output (a message, thought or new tool call) with no turn running starts the follow-up, and the follow-up ends at its result (the `usage_update` with a `cost`). The follow-up is for the commands that ended: at its start the plugin keeps tracking each command whose text (quotes and extra spaces ignored, as whole words: `sleep 5` is not in `sleep 50`) is still in the arguments of a shell under the session's `claude` process, one command per shell (of two commands with the same text and one such shell, the newer one), and drops the others; when it can't read the processes it drops them all. So with two overlapping commands the first one's follow-up leaves the second tracked: the session stays `running` and the second gets its own follow-up (or, see [Gates](#gates), its own Clubmate prompt). At a turn's end the plugin checks the session's `claude` process: when no shell runs under it any more, the task ended during the turn and the session is `done`. Stop (`agents.cancel`) on such a session stops waiting: the session turns `done`, and a follow-up in progress is cancelled. The phone and the Mac show it as any running session; nothing on the clients changes.

A session whose turn ended `done` but that owns an enabled one-shot cron job (`in`, `at`) prompting itself (`agents.prompt` with its own id, "check the CI again in 5 minutes") is shown as `running` too: in `agents.sessions` (`status`, `group`, `active`, no `needsYou` even when its last message ends with `?`), in its header and on its sidebar row ("next check 19:12", the earliest such job; `activity` in `agents.sessions` carries the same title with `at` when its turn ended, and there is no `turnStartedAt`). `reminding: true` in `agents.sessions` marks it: no turn runs, so cron and triggers do not count it as busy and its reminder fires. Its turn still ended `done` for the agent (`agents.turn`, `agents.wait`), so a prompt starts a new turn rather than steering one; but its `done` or `asks` notification is not posted, and a parent counts it as a running child. The cron plugin sends the workspace's reminders whenever its jobs change, and again after the agents plugin restarts (`agents.reminders {reminders: [{session, at}]}`, only from the cron plugin); when the job fires, is deleted or paused the session shows `done` again. A recurring job (`every`, `cron`) never makes a session look running.

The session header's meta repeats the sidebar row's detail, which the row often cuts: AFK, the notify policy ("Input only", "Muted"), and the activity (`15 min` while running, the parent states' words, the last change's age otherwise), leaving out a state the header already shows as a badge (waiting for you, asking, Stopped, a review's progress), then the context fill. The plugin builds both from one `sessionRow`. The Mac draws the session header as one 28 pt row (`design/features/compact-session-header/rules.md`): the meta is not a line there, but labelled rows (`details`: Activity, Context, 5h window, 7d window, AFK, Notify, Review) in a popover behind a context ring (`context`: the fill, amber from 80 %), and the meta string is the ring's accessibility label. The plugin orders the badges by urgency (danger, warning, accent, then the rest) and the action buttons by what stays longest in the row: Context, Review changes, Reviewed, Open review, then from ‹parent›, forked from, asides, Earlier session. The phone's session header shows the same activity after the state from `agents.sessions` (`Running · 15 min` from `turnStartedAt`, or a reminder's `activity` title).

Waiting is amber (warning) everywhere: sidebar, Graphs, header badge ("waiting for you"), phone. A failed turn is `error`, never `done`. An internal error (`-32603`: untyped, or typed `incomplete_tool_call` or `no_result`; never in a night session) is retried once in the same turn ("The turn failed; retrying once"); only when the retry fails too does the session end in `error`, so a turn that recovers on the retry is `done`.

## Older sessions

Rule and wire. A closed session tree leaves the default list when it is old. The plugin decides this once and sends `older: true` on each row of such a tree in `agents.sessions`. The unit is a root session, all its descendants by `parentSession` and the gate reviewers of these rows (a reviewer goes with its lander). A unit is older when every row in it is closed, has no open task and no open items, is not a session of a graph run that still has its run row (run rows stay 7 days), and has `updatedAt` more than 3 days ago (`CLUBMATE_AGENTS_OLDER_AFTER` overrides the 3 days; `0` turns Older off). A unit with a night session is never older, as the delete does not touch it. The 30-day delete above uses the same clock, but a narrower unit and fewer checks: it groups only by `parentSession` and checks only that the record has expired. Older also links gate reviewers to their lander and stays out for a row with open items or a live run row. So the Older list holds closed trees from 3 days old until the delete removes them. A parent of a row in the default list is always in that list. An action that changes `updatedAt` on a row (resume, Restore, a status change, a new entry in its transcript such as a presented doc or a change to its open items) takes the whole unit out of Older. The plugin checks the line at each call; there is no timer. `agents.sessions` with `since` leaves out the older rows. A delta puts the id of each changed row that is now older in `removed`, so a row that crosses the line leaves the client's list, and a row that comes back arrives as a changed row. Each reply has `olderCount`: the number of older root trees the caller can see that are not `hidden` (omitted at 0). `older: true` gives the older rows too (a cursor is for one mode: do not use a cursor from a call with `older: true` in a call without it, or the opposite), and `session` gives that session also when it is older (search hits, notifications, links). Without `since` (plugins, `cm`, MCP) the list has every row, each older row with `older: true`. `agents.sessions-older {before?, limit?}` (hidden, remote, not for agents) gives `{sessions, next?, count}`: `limit` whole units (default 30, at most 100) with all their rows, the unit with the newest `updatedAt` first, the root id as tie-break. `next` is a cursor `<unix nanoseconds>.<root id>`, so a unit that enters or leaves Older between two calls does not move the next page. `agents.search` marks an older hit with `older: true`. The UI word is "Older".

On the Mac. The sidebar puts the older top-level sessions in an "Older" group (`clock.arrow.circlepath`) directly after Closed, under the All filter only. Its count is `olderCount` (the older trees of all sessions, not only of the newest 1000), and the Closed count no longer includes them.
