The group is folded by default and opens when you select a session in it (from a search hit, a notification or a link); you can fold it again. Its rows look and act like Closed rows: the 3 trees with the newest `updatedAt` in the tree (the order of `agents.sessions-older`), plus the tree of the selected session, then "Show 25 more · N left", which shows the next 25 until the group is folded. j and k step into the open group and skip a folded one. The plugin builds the sidebar from all sessions, so the Mac does not call `agents.sessions-older`. A session moves to Older at the next render of the sidebar.

On the phone. The list shows a folded group "Older · N" directly after Closed, only in the All filter, and only when the Mac sends `olderCount` above 0 and offers `agents.sessions-older`. N is `olderCount`, not the number of rows the phone has. The first unfold gets the first page of 30 units; the last row of the open group is a spinner while a page loads, "Show 30 more · N left" ("Show N more" for the last page) while `next` is set, or the error with Retry. The phone sends one page request at a time and keeps the pages in memory only; the saved list keeps `olderCount`, not the rows. Older rows look and act like Closed rows, and the list is the tree of [Session order](#session-order) also when only closed and older sessions are left. The pages are a cache: the phone drops them when `olderCount` changes, when an id in `removed` is a row it has (a row that crossed the line, or an older row that changed), and on a full reply; an open group then loads its first page again. A row that comes back stays in the Older group until the held list shows it. A row the phone fetches with `session` (after an action, a search hit, a notification) never enters the default list when it has `older: true`; the phone updates its copy in the Older group instead. An older session opened from a search hit shows its header from its transcript and has no list row. The workspace row's "N closed" counts the default list only. A Mac without `olderCount` gives no Older group and the full list as before.

## Repos card

An orchestrator with a task shows the Repos card as the last thing in its transcript, under its latest report: a `repos` node ([SDUI](sdui-protocol.md#node-types)) the plugin renders from `task` on every change, not written by the agent. It isn't in the Code chat panel. The plugin decides everything in it and the client draws the fields.

- Header: "3 repos", the counts ("1 merged · 1 failed · 1 waits"; several open pull requests read "2 PRs open, in order"), `feat/<slug>` "in each" while open. "1 failed" is danger, "1 ready to land" warning while a Land request is out, the rest muted.
- A row: its place in the order (equal `order` shares a number), repo name, how it lands with its `source` as written by the agent, its state. An AFK-connected repo reads "pull request · connected while you were away", whatever its rule. Words: `merged · PR #87`, `on its branch`, `PR #412 open`, `ready to land`, `failed`, `landing`, `running`, `asking`, `left unlanded`, `waits`, and `waits for 2` (a place in the order), `waits for its report` or `waits for task pause, landing there`; a repo that finished and waits reads `on its branch · waits for 2`. PR numbers come from the child's report (git is the only source of pull request state), so "PR #412 open" can be stale. Amber only for what needs you: `ready to land` with a Land request, and `asking` when the child itself needs you.
- A failed row opens by itself: the failing `tail`, Ask it to fix ↩ (`agents.repo-fix`), Open its session, Open in Code, Leave it unlanded… (`agents.repo-leave`, after a confirmation), and "Leaving it unlanded holds ads-management too." when repos after it haven't landed. Any other row with something to show opens on a click, with the actions that apply. A closed orchestrator offers none. Open in Code runs `git.worktree-switch` on the child's worktree, moves Code to its folder with `editor.code-root` and goes to Code; the title pill follows.
- The card is folded to its header unless a repo failed or a Land request waits. Folding or opening it holds until those change: a new failure or a new request opens the card again, even one folded by hand. Folding or opening a row holds until its state or the time it entered that state changes. The plugin decides this once and sends `collapsed`, `open` and the keys that hold a choice (`foldKey` on the card and on each row), so the Mac and the phone keep the same rule; the Mac's choice is kept per session until the plugin restarts, the phone's while the app runs.
- The header pill "3 repos · 1 failed ▾" opens the card, scrolls to it and gives it the keys, from anywhere in the transcript. The card's keys are in [shortcuts](shortcuts.md#repos-card-agents-section).
- Footer: "Lands in order 1 → 2 → 3 · a repo waits until the ones before it landed", with no order line while every repo shares one place. Once every child is done it reads "All children done · 3 worktrees left behind" with Archive task… at its end.

## Open items

A decision or an offer an agent leaves at the end of a turn is an open item, not a question: the turn ends `done`, nothing waits, and the items sit under the report until you get to them. A question the agent cannot go on without stays AskUserQuestion, amber and `waiting`. An agent always ends a turn with some text, often after the tool call, so the Mac draws the card after the turn's last message, not where the call was; the phone docks it above the composer.

- Posting. An agent posts with `agents_open_items` (`agents.open-items`) for its own session; a user or a test instance may name any `session` with `cm command run agents.open-items --args '…'`. Each item is `{kind: "decision", header, question, quote, multiSelect?, options: [labels], descriptions: [lines]}` or `{kind: "offer", label, description?, quote}`: `header` of up to 12 characters, 2 to 4 options, a description for each (Other is always offered), and `quote`, the report's sentence. The manifest schema allows no objects inside array items, so the tool takes labels and descriptions as two parallel lists; stored and sent to clients an option is `{label, description}`. A bad item fails the whole call with a message that says what to fix.
- Limits. The open set holds 3 decisions and 4 offers across the session's turns. Anything beyond that is filed in the backlog at once, one task per item with the quote and the session's id, and its task id is in the entry's `overflow`, so the agent doesn't have to count.
- Record. Items live in the session log (`open-items`, with `open-items/changed` for what happens to them), so they survive restarts. Ids are `oi-N`, unique in the session. State is `open`, `answered`, `no`, `backlog` (with `task`, the backlog id) or `handed`. Clients get one transcript entry per turn: `{kind: "openItems", items: [{id, kind, header, question, label, description, quote, options, multiSelect, state, answer, task, number, at}], overflow: [taskIds], later}`.
- Report markers. In the text it sends (`agents.transcript` and the Mac's message nodes, not the log), the plugin puts a circled digit after the sentence that holds each item's `quote`, in the last agent message of that turn that has it: the card's item 1 is ①. An agent often writes a line after the tool call, so earlier messages of the turn count too. No match, no marker. The digit stays plain text; the Mac's prose draws ① to ⑳ over it as the digit in warning ink in a ring, in any message, keeping the line height and what a copy gives.
- `agents.sessions`: `openCount` (each unanswered item is 1, omitted at 0), `openDecisions` and `openOffers` (how many of them are decisions and offers, omitted at 0), `openSince` (the oldest one's time) and `group: "open"`. `status` stays `done` and `needsYou` false. With a pending permission or question the session is `waiting`; its items stay and show again afterwards. For edges and `agents.turn` an open session is `done`. A prompt you type leaves the items open. A posted item you already closed in a way the agent hasn't heard yet (its answer waits in the queue, or you said no and no turn has started since) is not posted again and its old id comes back, so an agent that re-offers something during the turn your queued answer waits on leaves no card behind; the same text is compared, ignoring case. Once the agent has the answer, the same item is a new one.
- Answering (user only, also from the phone). `agents.open-answer {session, answers: [{item, option?, options?, other?}], offers: [ids], note?}` closes those items and starts one turn, queued when a turn runs: `Answers: Lists → Add an SDUI prop; Grouping → Its own group; do: ⌘/ screen`, then the note. `agents.open-later {session}` keeps them open and sets `later` on the entry, which folds the card; ignoring the card is the same. `agents.open-backlog {session, items?}` files one task per item, all when `items` is empty. `agents.open-no {session, items?}` closes them and starts no turn; the session's next turn begins with `None of these: …`. `agents.open-queue {workspace?}` lists `[{session, title, worktree, updatedAt, items}]`, oldest first. `agents.open-convert {session}` prompts the session to post what it left in prose.
- Aging. An item still open 7 days after posting (`CLUBMATE_AGENTS_OPEN_AFTER` overrides the 7 days) or when its session closes, by hand or any other way, becomes a backlog task, with a line in the session. A deleted session takes its items along. While the backlog can't be reached the items stay open and the plugin tries again.
- Closed sessions. Closing normally empties the set, so this is for an item that could not be filed: an offer answered with `agents.open-answer` starts a new session in the closed session's worktree (the main checkout when that is gone) and returns `{session}`, a decision goes to the backlog with its answer.
- Children. A child's items never reach you while its parent exists and is open: they close as `handed` and the parent is prompted with `[Clubmate] Child "…" (agt_…) left open items: …`, the items in the text. It answers the child with `agents_prompt` or carries them into its own `agents_open_items`. With the parent gone they stay on the child as yours. Graph nodes and cron or trigger sessions behave as top-level sessions.
- On the Mac. One card holds the open set, at the entry of the newest turn that has open items, under its report: `Open after this turn`, the outlined count (`openCount`), `2 decisions · 3 offers · answer any time; nothing waits`, each decision numbered with its header chip, quote line and radio or check rows, the offers as one `Next — tick what you want me to do` item, what was already answered or filed as a muted line, `+N more went to the backlog: #a, #b`, then Send answers, Later, To backlog and No to all, which closes the items with no turn, so a card nobody wants to act on goes away in one click. Older entries show one line once all their items are closed. Later folds the card to one line with Show; a waiting session hides it until it stops waiting. The `N open` badge in the session header is a button (`openReveal`, node `<session>/open/badge`): a click leaves the Context view, unfolds a Later card, widens the transcript window to its turn, scrolls to it and gives it the keys; while the session waits the badge has no action. `agents.open-later` folds the card again from any client, also after Show or a badge click. In a form of at most 560 pt the items are an accordion, as in the question card. The plugin keeps the picks, the keyboard cursor and the folded state per session in memory (`sessionUI.open`), the form autosaves into them, so the card, the queue and the composer see the same picks: a message typed while something is picked goes out as `open-answer` with the text as its note, and the composer's placeholder says so (`Message Claude… — your answers go with it`). Keys are in [shortcuts](shortcuts.md#open-items-agents-section-card-or-open-queue).
- Report numbers. The card's numbers and the report's circled digits come from one function (`openNumbers`): decisions in posting order across the entries that still have an open item, every offer the one number after them, since the offers are one item on the card. An entry with nothing open has no digits. The same numbers go out as `number` on the items of the `openItems` entries and of `agents.open-queue`, omitted where there is none. They are worked out when sent, not stored, and an entry whose numbers changed counts as changed, so a phone that fetches only what changed gets the new ones. The phone draws the field on its card, its queue and the Next row; for a Mac that doesn't send it the phone numbers a turn's items itself, each by its place in the turn.
- Open filter. The chip `Open N` in Agents (⌥O; `N` sums `openCount` over the workspace's open sessions, children included) turns the sidebar into the sessions with open items or `mayAsk` and the main pane into the queue, oldest first: `Open · 7 items in 3 sessions`, Send N answers (⌘↩, answers are sent per session, one turn each), one card per session with Open session (↩), an accordion across all of them. A `mayAsk` session gets a card with Turn into items (`agents.open-convert`). Picking a row moves the cursor to that session. The row shows the outlined count in place of its age; `mayAsk` shows `may have left a question` in the faint ink. The status bar reads `· 5 open`, the sum of `openCount`.
- Phone. The Mac lists groups in `groupOrder`; the phone shows Open in its inbox under Needs you, in `inboxKey` order (an older Mac: an Open section above Running, oldest `openSince` first), and folds a card whose entry has `later` or whose session has a turn running. Its row reads "Done · 2 decisions, 3 offers" from `openDecisions` and `openOffers`, and it fetches `agents.open-queue` only while its Open screen is shown; and a message typed while picks are unsent goes as `note` of `agents.open-answer`. Its card's footer reads No to All, Later, Send answers; a tap on the transcript above an open card does what Later does, also one that opens a link or a tool row there (with the keyboard up it only drops the keyboard); No to All asks nothing: the card turns into "Closed 5 items · Claude hears “none of these” next turn" with Undo, and `agents.open-no` goes 5 s later, or at once when a message is sent first; To Backlog stays in the session header's •••.
- Safety net. A turn that posted no items, is not `waiting` or `asks`, and whose last paragraph matches `your call|up to you|should it|should I|if you want|let me know if|want me to|если хочешь|решать тебе|на твоё усмотрение|могу сделать|скажи, если` gets `mayAsk` in `agents.sessions` until its next turn. It will fire falsely, so it is only a faint hint.

## Needs you

A session needs you when a permission or a question is pending (status `waiting`), when a top-level session's turn is done and its last message asks a question (`asks`), when a connect request waits on a session whose turn has ended, or at any time while a `Land ‹repo›?` request or a failed repo of its task waits (see Connect requests and Landing below), or while a landing past its gate's rounds of changes, a turn a Done gate holds, or a tool call a Tool call gate holds, waits for your answer on its card ([Gates](#gates)), or while its approved landing waits for your yes on a landing queue's `Land into ‹branch›?` card ([Landing queue](#landing-queue)). A child session's `asks` is for the session that started it, not for you, so it never needs you on its own. So is a child's pending question (AskUserQuestion, a form `elicitation/create`; a URL one such as a sign-in stays yours) while the session that started it exists and is not closed: the child shows `asksParent` (`asksParent: true` in `agents.sessions`), sends no push and no Mac notification, counts as running for its parent's `childStatus`, and the parent is woken with `[Clubmate] Child "…" (agt_…) asks you, not the user: "<question>" (agents_elicitation request elicitation-N). Answer it yourself; ask the user only when the decision is theirs.` The parent answers with `agents_elicitation` or asks you itself. With the parent gone the question needs you. A child's pending permissions always need you. A session that stopped with an error does not need you unless a connect request or a landing waits on it; it shows as Stopped. A child that stopped with an error doesn't need you on its own either, but a repo child's failure makes its orchestrator need you. Nor do [open items](#open-items): they have their own count and group. An [aside](#asides) needs you only when it asks itself, never for its parent. Nor does a night session (`night` in `agents.sessions`, a service session of the nightly self-improvement run): its permission requests are rejected and its questions declined at once, and a last message ending with `?` doesn't count. Nor does a session that is `running` because its background task runs on, or because a [Done gate](#done-gates) holds its finished turn, even when its last message ends with `?` (see § Status). The ending `?` is a fallback: a top-level Claude session is told, through its system prompt (`_meta.systemPrompt.append` on `session/new` and `session/resume`), to ask questions that need your answer with AskUserQuestion, which arrives as a pending question (`elicitation/create`) and needs no guessing, and to post what stays open at the end of a turn as open items (§ Open items). Child sessions are told only the second part; their questions go to the session that started them. Nor is a session the voice plugin started with its own `systemPrompt`: it gets that text instead. The agents plugin computes this once and sends it as `needsYou` in `agents.sessions`; a top-level turn that ends with `asks` is also posted once as a `needs-input` notification (see [notifications](notifications.md#in-the-app)), and so is a gate's card when it opens ([Gates](#gates)). The Mac sidebar's "Needs you" filter and chip count each top-level row once when it or any session under it needs you (a child waiting for permission whatever its parent's status; a closed parent counts in Needs you only), and a graph run once on its `kind: "run"` entry when no listed row already counts for it. The Mac status bar's "N need you" and the dot on the rail's Agents icon show that same count (the sum over `listFilters`, see [Session order](#session-order)), so a parent and its waiting child count once; the phone (its "Needs you" list, waiting counts, session groups and glyphs) shows exactly these sessions. Neither client derives it on its own. The phone falls back to the same rule only for a Mac whose Clubmate does not send `needsYou` yet. A tool call a [Tool call gate](#tool-call-gates) holds is not a permission: the session reads `running` and needs nothing from you while its gate decides.

Connect requests. A session asks to connect repos to the workspace with `agents.connect-repo {repos: [{path | url, reason, landsBy, source}]}`. The call returns at once with `{request, status: "pending", next}`, since a tool call times out and a request has no limit; the session ends its turn and Clubmate prompts it with the answer. The request is stored on the session's record as `connectRequests` (`[{id, from?, fromTitle?, at, repos: [{path?, url?, name, reason, landsBy, source, facts, state, folder?, note?}]}]`, `facts` being what `git.repo-facts` returned), so it survives a restart and never times out; a pending one also keeps the session from auto-closing. A child's request lands on its top-level ancestor, the orchestrator, skipping closed ones, with the child in `from` and `raisedOn` in the child's result; the answer goes to the orchestrator only. The orchestrator needs you once its turn has ended (`done`, `idle` or `error`): its `pending` carries the request ids, `pendingSummary` has `kind: "connect"` ("Connect ads?", "Connect 3 repos?") and the turn end posts `needs-input`, as does a child's request landing on a session that is not running. The user answers with `agents.connect-answer {request, answers: [{path, action: connect | skip | other, otherPath?}]}`, `path` being the repo's path or, without one, its url. Only the user's answer counts: a call with `agent`, with a `caller` that is a session of this core, or through a plugin other than remote or voice, is refused, and no parent's word stands in for it, in `agents.permission`, in a prompt or by turning AFK on for a child. A repo left out of the answer is skipped, not pending, so one answer closes its request; an answer to a closed request is refused with `this connect request was already answered`. One connect answers every pending request for that repo in the workspace, whichever task asked: the repos reached this way read `connected`, and a request with nothing left to decide closes. A closed request prompts its session with one line per repo (`connected`, `skipped`, `failed` with why). A connected repo becomes a folder of the workspace with `addedBy` the orchestrator's title. A `url` connects by cloning (a remote helper url such as `ext::…` is refused when the request is raised; `git.clone`, next to the workspace's first folder under the repo's name, or into `otherPath`): the repo reads `cloning` meanwhile, no longer needs you, and the prompt comes when the clone ends; several urls clone one after another, without holding up the workspace's worktree work, and a clone in flight is followed again after a plugin restart. While the orchestrator itself is AFK (its own `afk`, not a child's) and you turned that on (`afkBy` empty), a path on disk connects at once with `addedAfk`, a notice in its transcript and `status: "connected"`, and needs no answer; a `url` still waits, since AFK never clones. AFK an agent turned on for itself connects nothing: "я афк" in a prompt reads the same as text another session wrote, so the request waits for you like any other. Turning AFK on for a session whose path requests wait connects them the same way, and the request's prompt goes out once nothing in it is left to decide. A session that stopped with an error while a request waits posts `needs-input`, not `stuck`. Otherwise a path that already is one of the workspace's folders reads `connected` at once; a path inside one still gets the card ([Repos in a folder](#repos-in-a-folder)).

On the Mac a waiting request is a card docked above the composer, in the permission family: `request.connectRepo` for the oldest open connect request, else `request.land` for the first repo with a Land request. It answers through the same code as `agents.connect-answer` and `agents.land-answer`. A connect card shows up to 3 repos and folds the rest; each row has name, path, remote, branch and clean state, the agent's reason as a quote, how it lands with its source, and what it brings, neutral. The card has the keys until the composer is clicked ([shortcuts](shortcuts.md#repo-requests-agents-section-or-code-chat-panel)); ticks and other folders are kept per request until the answer. Reason and source come from agents, so they render as text, never as links or controls. A repo not on disk reads "Clone … into …?" and the button "Clone and connect"; other folder there picks the parent of the clone. On a Land card Review… opens the child's review in Code, or its diff when it has none, and leaves the request open, Merge prompts the child, and Leave it unlanded… confirms first and says what it holds. The header badge reads "1 to land". The phone gets the same nodes as `request` and `repos` on `agents.transcript` (left out for agent callers, with no task or no request), and calls the same commands, so its words can't drift; see [Multi-repo tasks on the phone](#multi-repo-tasks-on-the-phone). `pendingSummary.onMac` stays true on `connect`, `land` and `failed` for a phone built before these cards, which shows the summary with "Answer on the Mac"; a phone that has the cards uses it only when the Mac sends no `request`. A question the orchestrator asks wins over a failed repo: `pendingSummary` is then the question, or nothing when its text is unknown, so the phone can answer it.

Landing. A task is declared with `agents.task` on the orchestrator and its `task.repos[]` are in landing order, each with `name`, `order`, `landsBy`, `source`, `state`, `for`, `session`, `pr`, `tail`, `request`, `capped`, `into`, its default branch, `fixAsked` and, once a Land request is out, `branch` and `commits`; the plugin decides all of it and clients render it. `state` is `waits` (not started or held, `for` says what for: a repo's name, "ads, which failed", "ads, left unlanded", "task pause, landing there", "its report"), `running`, `asking` (the child waits for an answer), `failed` (the child's session stopped with an error or it reported `failed`, with `tail`), `ready` (the work is done, and in ask mode a `Land ‹repo›?` request is out), `onBranch`, `prOpen`, `merged` or `left`. A child that is done reports with `agents.task-report {state, pr?, tail?}`: `ready`, `failed`, `onBranch`, `prOpen` or `merged`. Nothing lands before its `ready`, and a session of its that ends a turn without one reads `waits` for "its report". The landed states count only in their turn: `onBranch`, `prOpen` and `merged` are refused before Clubmate's land prompt, so `prOpen` can't clear a Land card, while a settled repo ignores any report, `ready` after `merged` included, and the result's `note` says so. A repo lands only after every repo with a lower `order` has landed, which means `merged` or `onBranch`; repos with the same `order` don't wait for each other, and a failure, `left` or an open pull request holds the rest. One task lands per folder at a time, across the app: a repo holds its folder from `ready` until it is `merged`, `onBranch`, `prOpen`, `failed` or `left`, and the holder is stored on the task, so it survives a restart, and `agents.task` declared again without a repo that holds its folder is refused, so the hold can't go with it; a second task reads `waits` for "task ‹slug›, landing there" and goes on by itself. A closed child reads `left` and gives the folder up, and so does a closed orchestrator. With `landsBy: branch` the repo goes to `onBranch` when its turn comes, with no prompt. Otherwise the orchestrator's finish mode decides. Merge: the plugin prompts the child to land by its repo's own rules, once; the prompt is stored as sent before it goes, so a restart never sends it twice, one lost in that moment shows as `waits` for "its report", and a landing cut off by a restart is continued like any turn. A queued land prompt is not stored with the session's queue: at plugin start a repo whose child hasn't begun its land turn gets it again, unless git shows it landed, which also goes for any prompt about to be sent. A land turn that ends with no report and nothing in git makes the repo `failed`, with a `tail` saying so, and it keeps the folder until the child reports or the user leaves the repo. A check git couldn't finish (git was killed or ran out of time) decides nothing: the repo stays as it is and is checked again at the next pull request check. Ask: a request with `kind: "land"` in `pendingSummary` ("Land ads?") and its id in `pending`, with no timeout, answered by `agents.land-answer {request, action: review | merge | leave}`, the user's word only, as for a connect request. `review` returns `{session, worktree}` for the client to open and changes nothing, `merge` prompts the child saying the user agreed, `leave` records `left`, which frees the folder and holds what depends on the repo. In any mode `agents.repo-leave {session, repo}`, the user's word only, does the same for a repo that failed or is held, and `agents.repo-fix {session, repo}`, also the user's word only, prompts the child of a failed repo with its `tail`, once per failure (`fixAsked`; a second press does nothing, and the row drops the button until the repo fails again); the repo leaves `failed` when that turn starts, also for a child that stopped with an error. The child is what lands, since only it has its repo's rules: if they say nothing it commits on the branch, stops and reports `onBranch`. Its first prompt names the rule the orchestrator declared for its repo and asks it to correct that rule with `agents.task` before it starts when the repo's instructions say otherwise or nothing (then `branch`), so the Land card says what it will do. After the orchestrator declares it, a repo's rule changes only through `agents.task` again: from the orchestrator, which declares the whole task anew, or from that repo's child, which names only its own repo and changes its `landsBy` and `source`, never the order; a child naming another repo, or any other session, is refused, and neither a land prompt nor a report rewrites the rule. The orchestrator's prompts carry the task's rules once it has a task: change no repo itself, and its finish mode is not for the task's repos, so it never merges, squashes, pushes or deletes a child's branch or worktree. Declaring the first task while a turn runs also adds those rules to that turn (steering), since an orchestrator usually declares and runs the whole task in its first turn, which started with only its finish mode. An agent session that starts a repo child for a repo its task doesn't list is refused with "declare it with agents_task first", since a repo child without a task has nothing to report its landing to. A repo connected by AFK for this orchestrator reads `capped`, and its land prompt asks for a pull request at most in this task, even if its rule is merge. The cap is advisory: it holds per orchestrator and lives in the prompt, so another orchestrator's task on that folder isn't capped, and a capped child's `merged` counts when git shows it. A folder with `addedAfk` that the user hasn't `seen` is never fetched, by `base: "default"` or by these checks: the user hasn't looked at its config yet, so a child starts from the local default branch with a notice, and a pull request there moves on once the folder was seen. `merged` holds only if git shows the change in the default branch, `origin/<default>` or the local one: the branch tip is an ancestor, or `git merge-tree --write-tree <default> <branch>` gives exactly the default branch's tree, which is exact to the byte and covers merges and squash merges, and the branch has a net change from its fork, so a commit and its revert or an empty commit prove nothing; a conflict or a failure is not proof, and no merge driver, merge attribute, merge setting or replace ref of the repo takes part; a branch with no commits of its own never counts, measured from the merge base recorded at `ready`, so a deleted child doesn't blank it. A claim git denies stays not merged and the result's `note` says so. Git is the only source of pull request state, no `gh`: a `prOpen` repo moves to `merged` when git sees it. Its repo is fetched every 5 minutes while a task has one (`CLUBMATE_AGENTS_PR_CHECK` overrides that) and stops when none is left, on any child's report for every `prOpen` repo of its task, and at plugin start. A closed orchestrator's task is neither fetched nor checked; if the session is reopened the next check picks it up. A Land request or a failed repo makes its orchestrator need you at once, running or not, since nothing in its turn answers it: a wake from a child doesn't take the card out of Needs you. The child shows its failure as usual, the parent's `pendingSummary` has `kind: "failed"` ("ads failed") and `taskSummary` reads as in [Task tree](#task-tree), which the sidebar shows instead of a child count. An orchestrator with an open task and its children aren't auto-closed until every repo is `merged`, `onBranch` or `left`, and a repo child is never cleaned up after a merge. A hand-off carries the task, its finish mode and its children along: after an orchestrator's, the old session holds neither the task nor a request and its children's `parentSession` names the new one, so the tree, `taskSummary`, wakes and the counts follow the live owner, and after a repo child's, the new child takes over the repo with its state, `ready` and folder. A folder holding a repo of an open task can't be removed.

Graph runs. A graph run needs you while one of its steps waits for your approval or one of its edges holds a firing past its round cap (see [agent graphs § Needs you](agent-graphs.md#needs-you)). The agents plugin's graph engine decides it and hands it to the session list in the same process; `agents.sessions` lists such a run as its own entry next to the sessions: `kind: "run"`, `id` the run id (`gr_…`), `title` the run title, `run: {id, title, number}` (`number` the run's place among the workspace's runs of its graph; the phone titles the row "Review gate · #4"), `status: "waiting"`, `needsYou`, `needsYouSince` (when the step began to wait or the firing was held), `group: "needsYou"`, `pending: ["step-<node>"]` with `pendingSummary {kind: permission, tool: execute, text: <command>, role: <the step's role>}` for a step (the phone's row reads "Squash into main wants to run `git merge…`"), or `pending: ["cap-<edge>"]` with `pendingSummary {kind: question, text: "<From> → <To> ran N of N rounds"}` for a cap. Its appearing publishes `agents.changed` (`status`), its going away (answered, continued, stopped, removed) `agents.changed` `resolved` with that request and `requestKind` `permission` (both post a `permission` notification, so the phone drops it), and it is in `removed` of the next delta. A run counts once: the Mac sidebar's run row reads the text ("Tests wants to run make test") in warning ink, opens and counts once under Needs you whether its node sessions are top-level or under the session that started the run; the status bar's "N need you" (which reads "N runs need you", "1 run needs you", while everything that needs you is a run), the bell's pinned items ("Permission · Run make test" or "Question · Tests → Coder ran 2 of 2 rounds", the run title under it; it opens the run in Graphs, as does its macOS notification, and the agents plugin's graph engine's own `notify.post` for the run is folded into it) and the phone's Needs you list (a run row opening the run) count the entry like a session; the status bar, like the Needs you chip, counts it only when none of the run's node rows (or its lander) already counts for it. A run's node session that waits for permission itself still counts on its own. The entry is answered with `agents.graph-approve` or `agents.graph-continue`, never `agents.permission`; the voice assistant leaves run entries out.

Gates waiting for trust. A gate whose graph changed since it was pinned needs you only while it holds something: while at least one held turn or held tool call waits behind it because its graph changed (`held.wait` or `calls[].wait` the changed-graph text; a paused gate counts what it still holds; not the "checking again" hold, and not a hold whose graph can't be read, which keeps its own wait words and no entry). `agents.sessions` then lists the gate as its own entry, one per gate however many wait: `kind: "gate"`, `id` the gate id (`gt_…`), `title` the gate's name ("Done · plans and specs"), `rowDetail` "Graph changed · N waits", `status: "waiting"`, `needsYou`, `group: "needsYou"`, no `pending`, and no `listGroupKey` or `listGroupLabel`: it is listed only in Needs you, never again under a held session's worktree (a graph run's entry keeps its place in its first node's worktree). It counts once in Needs you (`listFilters` `needs`, the Mac chip and status bar, the phone's chips); the held sessions stay under Running with "waits for trust" and don't count again. The same rule counts what waits everywhere (`waitsForTrust` in the plugin: a hold whose graph changed; `gateLanding.trust`, `gateRow.trustWaits`): the gate card's waiting line, the gate page header, the Gates screen's top row and this entry. A gate whose graph can't be read (`gateRow.unreadable`) counts nothing and its top row reads "Graph can't be read". On the Mac the entry is one line in the Needs you group like every tree row (`mirror:gate:‹id›`), and the same line heads the Needs you filter's list: the point's glyph in amber, the gate's match ("plans and specs"), then an amber "to trust" badge; the label truncates, the badge never does; the row words with the count are its tooltip (`help`). It opens the gate page. On the phone it is a row with the amber needs-you glyph (the circle with "!") whose line reads the row words with "Graph changed" in amber, and it opens the Gates section on that gate (`agents.gates`, picking the gate's row). When the entry appears the plugin posts one needs-input notification (title the gate's name, message the row words, `session` the gate id, so the Mac's source reads "‹workspace› · Gates"; not again after a restart while the gate still waits), and when it goes (trusted, or nothing waits any more) it publishes `agents.changed` `resolved` with `session` the gate id, `request` "trust:‹gate id›" and `requestKind: "gate"`, which withdraws that notification on the Mac and the phone; a tap on it opens the gate page. With nothing held, a gate waiting for trust shows only on the Gates dock row ("N to trust", amber) and the Gates screen's top row. An older phone draws the entry as a plain session row.

Run rows. `agents.sessions` with `since` also returns `runs`: every graph run of the workspace, the full list whenever it is sent (every reply, a delta too, unless the call passed `lean` and no run row changed since the cursor), as the plugin decides them; the Mac and the phone draw them and never re-derive a state. `state` is `asks` (the run itself needs you: a step waits for approval or an edge holds a firing at its round cap; `detail` "asks you", `needsYou`, `request` the pending request; it counts once in Needs you, the same entry as above), `running` (`detail` "turn N", or the working node's activity; while a node session needs you `detail` is "→ ‹Role›" and `pointer` that node's id, and the amber sits on the node's own row, the run doesn't count), `ended` (its reason is "‹node› reported ‹outcome›": `detail` the outcome, e.g. "approved"), `stopped`, `failed` (the start failed) and `emptied` (none of its node sessions is left, archived or deleted after 30 days: `detail` "no sessions"; the run page still opens, and it goes through Remove run…, or by itself like any ended run). A run that no longer runs leaves the list 7 days after it stopped, exactly as Remove run… removes it; its sessions stay (see [agent graphs § Stopping](agent-graphs.md#stopping)). `home` is `group` while the run is running or one of its sessions is open (`worktree` is the first open node session's), else `closed` (`worktree` its first session's or the folder its graph came from); `sessions` are the node sessions still listed; `group` (`needsYou`, `running`, `done`, `closed`) and `status` (`waiting`, `running`, `done`, `error`, `closed`) are where and how the phone lists the row, which shows `detail` as its state line. The phone lists land reviewers by `hidden` and `rowUnder` (see Reviewers below). The `kind: run` entries stay for an older phone. A change to a run (started, a turn, stopped, removed) publishes `agents.changed` with `session` the run id and `kind: status`, and a graph refused for want of trust one with `session: "graphs"`, so a client refetches `agents.sessions`.

Dock. The same reply has `dock`: three rows in this order, `service`, `gates` (Gates), `graphs`, each `{id, label, icon, detail, phoneDetail, state?, which?, attention?}` (rules: `design/features/dock-state/rules.md`). `detail` and `phoneDetail` are always the row's count (open service sessions, gates, graph definitions); what is happening is one `state`, the row's highest by priority, never in the count. Service: `state` "asks you" with `attention` while a service session needs you. Gates: "N to trust" with `attention` while N gates' graphs changed since they were pinned; else "N asking" while landings wait for Land anyway or Stop or a gate's review session waits on its own permission request (the lander row carries the amber); else "N landing" while N of this workspace's landings are under review; else "N held" while N of its turns and tool calls are held by Done and Tool call gates, counting calls, not holds: a call that two gates hold counts once, while each gate's own row counts the calls it holds. Graphs: "N to trust" with `attention` while N untrusted graphs were asked to run (by a cron job, a trigger, an agent or you: the refused `agents.graph-run` is remembered until the graph is trusted). `which` names the one gate (its dock label, "into main", "plans and specs") or graph (its title) behind a `state` when exactly one is, else it is left out. The Mac draws `state` after the label: an amber badge with `attention`, else "· ‹state›" in caption ink; the count stays in its column. The phone puts `state` in the row's subtitle followed by "· ‹which›", the word and the glyph in warning ink with `attention`, the count on the right. Under it each repo with a gate is a row (`gate-repo:‹main checkout›`, its folder name; selecting it opens its first gate) and its gates are rows under it, named by their match without the point ("into main", "into the parent's branch", "into release/*", "into any branch") with the Landing glyph, in the order default branch, base, branches like…, any; a gate's detail is decided once, by priority: "to trust" (warning) › "N asking" › "N landing" (both `plain`) › `paused` (faint) › the last verdict and its age (`approved 4m` in success, `changes 3h` in warning, `docs only 2d`, `you: land 1h` in accent) › `no landings yet` (faint; left out on the docked row). The phone's Gates screen uses the same detail. Search matches a gate under its repo. Docked rows may carry `children`, drawn one level deeper. Nothing else on a docked row is amber.

Pages. A run row (`run:‹id›`), a graph definition (`graph:‹name›`), a gate (`gate:‹id›`) and How graphs work / How gates work (`graph-help`, `gate-help`) open their page in the Agents main pane when selected (tree item `open`); the page carries a crumb and Back to the session ⌘[, which returns to the session selected before it. Selecting an ended run's page folds its row. The Graphs and Gates sections stay for the phone but leave the Mac rail (`rail: false`); ⌘K has New graph…, Run graph… and Open gates (which unfold their docked row), New gate…. A gate page's crumb is `Agents / Gates / ‹repo›`.

Reviewers. A land gate's review session (any session with `gateFor`: the reviewer, or each node session of a gate's review run) has no row: not in a worktree group, not in Closed, not in the All, Needs you, Open or Running counts; the plugin sends `hidden: true` on it in `agents.sessions`. The lander's own row carries the landing instead ([Gates § In Agents](#gates)). Only while such a session waits on its own permission request does it show, as a child row of its lander: the amber `shield`, "Round n review", detail "asks you" in warning; the lander unfolds, the request counts once in Needs you (the lander's row is the one counted), and the row goes once the request is answered. A gate run that needs you (a step's approval, a held edge) shows the same way, its row reading the need's text instead of "asks you" ([Gates](#gates)); a Done gate's run shows under the session it holds (matched through `held.run`), "Round n review" with the hold's round, the held session reading `asking`, and on the gate page's live card. An open one that stopped with an error, or that nothing links to any more (it isn't its landing's `landReviews[].reviewer` and not a node of a run), keeps a row under its lander too (the `shield`, "Round n review", or "Land review", "Done review" or "Tool call review" when no round is known, its status as detail), so it can still be seen, closed or deleted. Such a session has `hidden: false` and `rowUnder` (the lander's id); when the lander is closed or not listed the row shows at the top of its worktree instead, so nothing is out of reach. A new round that starts a fresh reviewer closes the one it replaces (done, idle or stopped with an error), and a landing closes every review session of that lander, target and source branch. Each one also carries `gateRound {round, label, verdict}` ("Round 2 review"; `round` counts an approval as its own round: reviewing is the landing's rounds so far + 1, `changes` closes the round it counted, `approved` the next one) and, while its lander still tracks the landing, `nestUnder`; the phone reads `gateRound.label` as the session's title ("Round n review" on its row and its screen), and `nestUnder` stays for phones that nested reviewers by it before they read `hidden` and `rowUnder`. `parentSession` doesn't change, so child limits, permission answers and finish modes stay where they were.

A [night session](#self-improvement) never needs you: its permission requests are rejected at once and its offers wait without asking.

Groups. The plugin also sends each session's `group` in `agents.sessions`, the section a phone on an older Mac lists it in (with the tree of [Session order](#session-order) it only splits the inbox into Needs you and Open and places Paused, Closed and Service): `closed` for a closed session (an older one is not in the default `since` reply, see [Older sessions](#older-sessions)), else `needsYou` when it needs you, else `service` for a service session, else `paused` while it is in error and waits for a usage limit reset (`limitWait` not yet fired, see § Status), else `running` while its status is `running`, else `open` while it is `done` with open items, else `done`. A parent is lifted to its most urgent child's group (any depth, after lifting the child; a land reviewer whose round runs counts as its lander's child): to `needsYou` always, to `running`, `open` or `paused` unless the parent is a service session; the order is `needsYou`, `running`, `open`, `paused`, `done`, `closed`, `service`, so a closed parent with a running child lists under Running, a done parent whose child waits for permission under Needs you, and a done or closed parent whose child is paused under Paused, while a running parent stays under Running. Every session in `paused` carries `pausedUntil` (unix seconds, to the minute): when its own wait continues it (the reset plus the margin, so "Paused until 23:01" sits under the usage line's "resets 23:00": the limit's reset and the session's continue are two events, and both times are shown as they are), or for a lifted parent the earliest of its paused children; `paused: true` marks only the sessions the limit stopped themselves (the phone draws their pause glyph, a lifted parent keeps its own). Sessions in `paused` never count toward Needs you, post nothing new and show no banner. The phone lists one section per `pausedUntil` right after Needs you, titled "Paused until ‹time›" in the phone's zone (the time; the weekday when it is a day or more away, as the usage line's "resets"), each row reading `failure.line`; the Mac sidebar puts the same sections, built by the plugin, at the top of the tree above the worktree groups (open roots only, like the phone: a closed root stays under Closed), folded together (one fold key). A phone built before `paused` reads the unknown group as Done (`SessionGroup` falls back), which is where these sessions listed before. The phone puts each top-level session (with its children nested under it) in its `group`'s section; it never works out the group itself, and falls back to the session's own group without lifting only for a Mac that does not send `group` yet. Each section's count and its workspace row ("N running · N closed") count these top-level sessions per `group` (children count with their parent, not on their own, as in the Mac sidebar's groups) and "N waiting" from every session that needs you. `active` in `agents.sessions` is true for a session that is `running` or `waiting` and not a service session; the Mac status bar's "N agents" counts these.

Oldest first. A session that needs you also carries `needsYouSince` in `agents.sessions`: when its pending request was made, or when its turn ended with the question. Every list of sessions that need you is ordered by it, oldest first, ties by session id: the Mac sidebar's "Needs you" filter (a top-level session counts with the oldest request in its subtree, and its worktree group, when shown, with its oldest session), the bell's pinned items and the phone's "Needs you" list across workspaces and Macs.

A session that needs you is never folded. In the Mac sidebar the worktree group, the Service group, the graph run and every parent above it are expanded while it needs you, whatever the user folded. The exception is a parent with repos, which folds and keeps that row pinned under it (see [Task tree](#task-tree)). On the phone a subagent or child that asks is a nested row under its parent, one step in (24pt) with no separator between them, never hidden in a folded group.

### Needs an answer

A question card whose fields are all AskUserQuestion's questions (`choice` or `multi`, each with its `<key>_custom` Other, none `required`) needs an answer before Send: a picked option, a ticked option, or text in an open Other row. Text in a closed Other row isn't sent, so it doesn't count. The plugin decides it and sends `needsAnswer` on the entry's `elicitation`; on the Mac the form's `needsAny` carries it. Any other form (`text`, `number` or `boolean` fields, an MCP form with its own `required`) sends as it did. Skip stays enabled on every card and means "none of these, go on"; on the Mac the footer says so next to it ("Skip answers “none of these” and ends the turn", "Skip ends the turn" when that doesn't fit). A single question a pick answers has no Send.

### Question card language

The plugin picks the language of a form question card's own words from the question: Russian when the message, the question texts and the options have more Cyrillic letters than Latin ones, else English. The text of a `<key>_custom` field doesn't count, since Claude sends it in English. It sends the words as `labels` on the entry's `elicitation` (`other`, `answer`, `send`, `skip`, `skipHint`, `skipHintShort`, `next`, `back`, `skipped`, `cancelled`, `answered`), and the Mac card and the phone card show them: "Other…" or "Другое…", "Your answer" or "Ваш ответ" as the Other field's placeholder (the phone no longer shows Claude's "Type your own answer, or add a note…"), Send and Skip, the skip hint, the phone's Next and Back, and the answered line's Skipped, Cancelled and Answered. An entry without `labels`, from an older Mac, shows English. A sign-in link card stays English.

### Quiz questions

`agents.quiz` (the `agents_quiz` MCP tool) lets an agent session ask the user a question about the code itself and wait for the graded answer, for guided code reading (the `code-tour` and `pr-walkthrough` graphs; design `design/features/guided-reading/rules.md`, § 1–3). Only an agent session calls it, and the question goes into its own session; the user answers on the Mac, the iPhone or the iPad.

- **Types:** `line` (pick a line in an excerpt), `range` (pick a range), `value` (type a value), `text` (answer in words), `choice` (pick one of 2–4 options, keys a–d).
- **Excerpt:** `file` (relative to the session's worktree, also when that is a folder inside a repo; an absolute path is resolved through symlinks first), `commit` (default `HEAD` there, stored as the full sha), `from`, `to` (at most 80 lines). The plugin reads the lines with `git show <commit>:<file>` and stores them with the question, so the card shows the file at the commit, never the work tree, also after a restart. `line` and `range` need an excerpt; the other types may show one.
- **Key:** `lines` for `line` (any of them is right) and `range` (`[first, last]`), `accept` for `value` (compared trimmed, case and spaces ignored, numbers as numbers) and `choice` (option values). `why` is the one line the reveal shows, `ref` its `path:line` (default `file:` the first key line).
- **Grading:** the plugin grades every type but `text` when the answer is sent: correct, close or wrong. A `line` answer is correct when it is one key line and close when it is a range that holds one; a `range` answer is correct when it is the key range and close when it overlaps it. A wrong answer rated sure gets `flag: sure_wrong` and the note "You were sure. The guide asks it again later." A `text` answer, and words typed in answer to any other type, stay `pending` until the agent grades them with `agents.quiz-grade {request, verdict, why, ref?}`; the card shows Checking until then.
- **Confidence:** guess, think so, sure (keys 1–3), required for every answer. For `line`, `range` and `choice` a rating after a pick sends (a rating before a pick only rates, for an answer in words): the plugin holds the answer for 2 s (`holdMs`), the card shows a bar, and Undo (⌘Z) goes back to the pick; another rating restarts the hold. For `text` and `value` a rating only rates, and the composer's send (⌘↩) sends the words.
- **Composer:** while a quiz is docked the composer is the question's Other (placeholder "Say it in words…", hint row "Typing answers in words; the guide grades it"): its send answers the quiz with the typed words (refused with "rate first: 1 guess · 2 think so · 3 sure" until rated, the Mac then moves the focus to the chips; refused while the composer has attachments, which stay).
- **Result:** `agents.quiz` returns `{request, status, answer?, lines?, confidence?, verdict?, correct?}` within `timeout` seconds (default and most 300). `status` is `waiting` (call `agents.quiz` again with `request` to keep waiting), `answered`, `skipped` or `cancelled`. `verdict` is `correct`, `close`, `wrong` or `pending`.
- **Stored:** the question, each pick and rating, the answer and the grade are records in the session log, so the card and the record come back after a plugin or core restart; a quiz that was still open when the plugin stopped shows Cancelled.
- **What the clients get:** the plugin decides every state and sends it as fields: the Mac gets the `request.quiz` node ([SDUI](sdui-protocol.md)), and `agents.transcript` gives the user's clients the same object as `quiz` on the elicitation entry. A pending quiz is a question for [Needs you](#needs-you) and `agents.wait`, like an AskUserQuestion card. The phone answers through `agents.quiz-answer`.
- **Answered:** the card leaves the dock and stays in the transcript as the record: ✓ and ✕ marks with labels on its lines (only the lines around the marks, the rest folded to "lines 23–27" rows), the verdict with the user's rating, one line of why and `path:line`.
- **Language:** the card's words follow the question's language as in [Question card language](#question-card-language).

### Guide steps

`agents.guide-step` (the `agents_guide_step` MCP tool) marks the start of a step of a guided code reading in the caller's own session (the `code-tour` guide; design `design/features/guided-reading/rules.md`, § 4). Args: `title`, `check` (true when the step asks a question), `concept` (the ledger concept id or title) and `ledger` (the absolute path of the run's `tour.json`; later steps keep it). The plugin stores the step in the session log and returns `{step, n, of?}`: `n` is the concept's place in the ledger, else the count of steps.

- **Transcript:** a step shows as "Step N · title" (node `guide.step` on the Mac, an entry of kind `step` with `step {step, label}` for the phone).
- **Controls:** under the latest step only, and only while no permission or question of the session is open: Continue (only when `check` is false; the only primary, ⌘↩ while the composer is empty), I know this · test me, Show me, Go deeper, Smaller steps. They are disabled while the agent runs; Go deeper also when the session can't have asides. There is no permanent Next: the guide goes on by itself after a graded answer.
- **Requests:** each control but Go deeper sends a fixed text to the session as the user's message: "Continue: go to the next step.", "I know this step: test me with one harder question.", "Show me: reveal the answer of this step.", "Smaller steps: split this step into smaller ones." (the Russian texts when the step's title is Russian). Go deeper opens an [aside](#asides) anchored to the step (`anchor` `step`, label "Aside · <title>", not graded, its own composer); a second press opens the same aside.
- **Fields:** the plugin sends `steps {session, step, actions [{id, label, enabled, primary?, shortcut}], pressed?, aside? {step, title, open, session}}`: as the `guide.steps` node at the end of the Mac transcript, and as `steps` in `agents.transcript` for the phone. `pressed` is the last control the user pressed on this step (Go deeper while its aside is open).
- **Stored:** the step and each press are records in the session log, so the controls and `pressed` come back after a plugin or core restart.
- **User only:** the Mac sends `stepAct {action}` ui events, the phone `agents.step-act {session, action}` (returns `{aside?}` for Go deeper); a call from an agent session gets "only the user presses a step control or a map offer", a control not offered now "this step control is not offered now".

### Concept map

A guide session whose steps name a `ledger` gets a concept map (design § 5). The plugin reads the ledger's `current` and `concepts [{id, title, status, parent, links, answers}]` (in tour order), combines them with the session's graded quizzes (a quiz's `concept` is a concept id or title; the latest one counts) and sends `tour {session, title, step, of, stepLabel, summary, current, counts, concepts [{id, label, state, stateLabel, step, stepLabel, depth, note?, links?, offers?}], edges, legend, shown, shortcut}`.

- **State rule:** a quiz correct and rated sure → known; correct and rated guess or think so → shaky; close → shaky; wrong → missed; sure and wrong → missed (the guide asks it again later). A concept without a graded quiz takes the ledger's status (`known`, `shaky`, `missed`; anything else is not yet). The current concept is `current`. `counts` count the rest.
- **Tree:** `depth` follows `parent`; a `links` entry is an edge to a concept on another branch, drawn as a ↗ chip (`edges` with `branch`).
- **Offers:** a known, shaky or missed concept offers Ask again (`ask_again`) and Back to step N (`go_back`); the current and the not yet concepts offer nothing. An offer sends "Ask me again about <concept> (<id>), in a new form." or "Go back to step N: <concept> (<id>), and teach it again." as the user's message. The Mac sends `tourOffer {concept, offer}`, the phone `agents.tour-offer {session, concept, offer}`; an offer the map does not show gets "this concept has no such offer now".
- **Where:** on the Mac the map is the `guide.tour` node in the aside column (`agents.aside` slot) while no aside is beside the session and the pane is shown; the Mac shows that column only when the main column is at least 960 pt wide. The header always has the `guide.strip` node: "step 5 of 8" while the pane shows, else the strip whose click opens the tree as a popover. ⇧⌘M (`tourPanel`) shows or hides the pane (kept in memory, as the Context toggle). The phone gets `tour` in `agents.transcript`: on the iPhone "step 5 of 8" under the title opens a sheet with the tree; on the iPad a header strip opens a popover, and the tree is a pane when the transcript column is at least 960 pt wide.

## Workspace activity

`agents.activity` (app scope, read-only) gives the activity of every workspace that has agent sessions: a map from workspace id to `{needsYou?, running?, failed?, lastActiveAt?}`. The phone's workspace picker shows it per workspace (`design/features/workspace-picker/rules.md`); the remote plugin adds it to its `workspaces` list ([remote § Wire contract](remote.md#wire-contract-v1)). The plugin decides each field here, once, and clients never count them again from sessions:

- `needsYou`: the entries of `agents.sessions` with `needsYou` true (sessions and graph runs), the rule of [Needs you](#needs-you).
- `running`: the session trees whose top row is in the `running` group (its own turn or a session under it runs; [Needs you](#needs-you) § Groups). A tree counts once, however many of its sessions run.
- `failed`: the sessions that stopped with an error (`status: error`), except one that waits for a usage limit reset (group `paused`) and a hidden land gate reviewer. There is no "seen" state, so a failed session counts until it runs again or is closed.
- `lastActiveAt`: the latest `updatedAt` of the workspace's sessions, closed ones too (RFC 3339, UTC).

[Service sessions](#service-sessions) do not count in any field. A count that is zero is left out, and so is `lastActiveAt` when the workspace has no session.

## Session order

Rows never move because something happened in a session (rules: `design/features/session-order/rules.md`). Inside a group, sessions go by start (`createdAt`), oldest first, the session id breaking ties; a new session goes last in its worktree group or under the session that started it, and children follow the same rule (graph run nodes and asides too). Activity (`updatedAt`) and state changes (running, waiting, done) never reorder anything; only a row's glyph, tint and detail change. Worktree groups: the workspace's home first, then the other worktrees by the start of their earliest session in that worktree, closed sessions included, so a group keeps its place when its first session closes. Closed (most recently updated first), Older (by the newest `updatedAt` in each tree, see [Older sessions](#older-sessions)), Service and the dock stay at the end; the "Needs you" filter keeps its oldest-need order (see [Needs you](#needs-you) § Oldest first); search results keep theirs. The Mac sidebar shows the newest 1000 sessions that are not older in this order, and every older tree through the Older group; the group keys come from every session.

`agents.sessions` sends the order as two stable sort keys per session, so one new session doesn't change any other row (and its delta hash): `listKey`, the start in UTC as `YYYYMMDDTHHMMSS.nnnnnnnnnZ`, a space and the session id, which sorts as a string like the rule above; `listGroupKey`, the session's worktree group: `0` for the home, else `1` followed by the start of the worktree's earliest session in the same layout, a space and the worktree path (no session id, so a session hidden from an agent caller doesn't leak through it). A client sorts by these strings and never re-derives the order. A Mac without `listKey` falls back to `createdAt`, then the id. A graph run's `kind: "run"` entry takes `listKey`, `listGroupKey` and its `worktree` from its earliest node session, and has none while the run has no node session listed.

Three more fields carry what the Mac sidebar shows, so the phone draws the same tree without its rules: `listGroupLabel`, the worktree group's header (its branch, a detached head as "detached · ‹sha›", else the folder name; on every session with a `listGroupKey`); `listFilters`, the chips (`all`, `needs`, `open`, `running`) this session counts in, and `listOpen`, the open items it adds to the Open chip. The plugin hands them out with the same tally that counts the Mac's filter tabs (`tallyFilters`), so the phone's chip count is the plain sum over the listed sessions (`listOpen` for Open) and equals the Mac's badge, as do the Mac status bar's "N need you" (the `needs` sum, reading "N runs need you" when every counted entry is a `kind: "run"` one) and "N open" (the `listOpen` sum): only a top-level row counts, in `needs` when it or anything under it needs you whatever its own status, a closed one only in `needs` and a hidden one in none, a service one only in `needs`, a lander counts in `needs` while its land gate's run waits for you, and a graph run's need goes on its `kind: "run"` entry when none of its node rows (or its lander) already counts for it. `inboxKey` decides the inbox (below).

On the phone. With `listFilters` in the list (and `inboxKey` on some session, or nothing that needs you or has open items), the Agents list is the Mac's tree (`AgentsTree` in CompanionKit): the inbox on top, a mirror row per session that carries `inboxKey` (the plugin's membership, see [Needs you inbox](#session-order) below; a graph step the phone draws under its run is copied as the step), in `inboxKey` order, under "Needs you" (keys starting `0`) and "Open" (`1`, with Answer all and the summed `openCount`); a mirror's state line ends with its `listGroupLabel` instead of an age and selects the same session as its tree row, which stays in its worktree; an entry (`kind`) with `inboxKey` and no `listGroupKey`, such as a gate waiting for trust, has no tree row: under All it shows only in the inbox, under a chip in a group of its own. Then "Paused until ‹time›" sections, then one section per worktree in `listGroupKey` order, rows and children in `listKey` order, headed by `listGroupLabel` and its row count (a lone home group has no header unless the inbox is shown above it, as on the Mac), then Closed (newest first) and Service, folded. Running and Done are not sections: a state change only restyles the row. Chips All / Needs you / Open / Running sit above it with the summed counts; a chip other than All hides the inbox and keeps the roots whose subtree has that filter, Needs you ordering rows and groups by `inboxKey`. Without these fields (an older Mac) the phone keeps its state sections by `group`, Needs you and Open longest waiting first, Running, Done and children by `listKey`.

Hold. While the list is on screen, a change that would move a row the user can see waits (`ListHold`): the list keeps its rows where they are and only their glyph, tint, detail and the chip counts change; a change that leaves every visible row in place (below the last one, no row of the list on screen, as on an empty chip, or the list is off screen: a session open on an iPhone, the app in the background) lands at once. The top bar shows a pill naming what waits, counted from the held diff: "1 needs you · 1 new · 2 moved" (new inbox rows, new sessions, other rows that change place). The queue lands on a pill tap, a pull to refresh, the list coming back, the app coming to the foreground, a chip switch, or when a session the user just started arrives.

**Needs you inbox.** Above the worktree groups the Mac tree shows an inbox: a "Needs you" section (glyph `tray`) with a copy (mirror row, `mirrorOf`) of every top-level session in the `needsYou` group, then, only when it has rows, an "Open" section (`tray.full`) with every top-level session in the `open` group (open items and its turn done), each longest waiting first; a section with no rows is gone, so the inbox is hidden when nothing waits. The session keeps its own row in its worktree, and only then is the home's group header shown even when it is the only worktree. A graph run's step session sits under its run row, so its copy is the run row; a run (its `kind: "run"` need or any of its steps) has one copy, in the first section it qualifies for. A copy's detail is where and how long, "chore · 3 min" (the branch's first segment, 8 characters at most, then the wait: now, N min, N h, N d), in the warning ink for Needs you; it has no children. Clicking or ↩ on a copy selects its session, both rows show the selection, and ↑↓ walk the rows as drawn; ⌃⌘↓/⌃⌘↑ skip the copies. The inbox shows under the All filter only; the Needs you filter keeps its own list. Membership and order are decided once, in the plugin (`inboxEntries` in `plugins/agents/inbox.go`): a top-level session (not closed, hidden, an aside, or a child under another row) is in Needs you when its `group` is `needsYou` (lifted from its children, asides and land reviews, service sessions included), else in Open when its `group` is `open`. Its wait starts at the earliest `needsYouSince` (Open: `openSince`) in its subtree when it enters a section and stays fixed while it is there, so unrelated updates don't move it; leaving the section clears it. `agents.sessions` sends it as `inboxKey`, present only while the session is in the inbox: `0` (Needs you) or `1` (Open), the wait's start in UTC as `YYYYMMDDTHHMMSS.nnnnnnnnnZ`, a space and the id. Sorted as strings, Needs you comes before Open, longest waiting first, the id breaking ties; a client tells the section from the first character and never re-derives the rule.

**Nothing moves under the pointer.** The sessions tree carries `hold`: on the Mac, while the pointer is over the sidebar, a change that would move a visible row (a new session, one closing, a copy entering or leaving the inbox) waits and the search field shows "N to show"; a changed glyph, tone or detail lands at once. The queue lands on a click on the pill, 0.5 s after the pointer leaves the sidebar, when the sidebar gets keyboard focus, when ⌃ goes down in the Agents section (so the session numbers you see are the ones the keys open), when you act in the sidebar, or when the selection moves to a row not yet shown (a session you just started). It holds only the tree it started on: another filter, search results or an empty list start over. There is no key for it: ⌘R opens a session's review. Details in [SDUI protocol](sdui-protocol.md).

## Session keys

Keys from the Claude Design ruling (`design/agents-keyboard-brief/README.md` § Round 2); the list is in `docs/shortcuts.md`. The plugin decides what each key goes to; the Mac and the iPad only send the command.

**Numbers.** ⌃⌘1…⌃⌘9 (the sidebar's g1…g9) open the N-th numbered row: the first nine top-level sessions and graph run rows the sidebar shows, in its order, with its filter, search and folds applied. Asides, graph steps, gate reviewer rows, inbox copies, group headers and service rows never get a number, so unfolding a row moves no number; a folded worktree group hides its sessions and their numbers. A run whose steps sit in two worktrees has two rows but one number, on its first row. The plugin writes the number on the tree row (`number`, see [SDUI protocol](sdui-protocol.md)), and `agents.jump {n}` opens the row with that number: a session gets the selection and its prompt the focus, a graph run opens its page. The number of the open session only moves the focus to the prompt. On the Mac, holding ⌃ for ½ s in the Agents section (or a pending g in the sidebar) shows the digits in place of the row details until ⌃ is released; any other key cancels the timer. A ⌃ press lands a held sidebar tree first (see [Session order](#session-order) § Nothing moves under the pointer), so the digits are always those of the tree the plugin numbers. `agents.sessions` marks the same rows with `numbered`, and each top-level run in `runs` too; the iPad numbers the first nine `numbered` rows of its list as shown (its own chip and folds), skipping its inbox copies.

**Open a session.** ↩ on a session row in the tree opens it and focuses the main pane; a click opens it too. A session with child sessions never folds or unfolds on ↩ or a click: l / h, → / ← and its chevron do that. The plugin sends `open: session` and `keepFolded` on every session row, and the Mac reads them (`docs/sdui-protocol.md` § tree item).

**Last session.** ⌃⇥ (the sidebar's gl) opens the session or page selected before the current one; a second ⌃⇥ comes back (`agents.last`). The plugin keeps one previous selection per workspace, stored, so it stays after a plugin or core restart. A closed or deleted previous session drops out: the key then says "no previous session". ⌃⇥ is caught only while an Agents pane has the focus, the prompt included; elsewhere (terminals, nvim, text fields in sheets) ⌃⇥ keeps its own meaning.

**Next that needs you.** ⌃⌘J and ⌃⇧⌘J (the sidebar's gn and gN) open the next or previous session that needs you, oldest first (`agents.needs {by}`). The plugin decides the order once and sends it as `needsRank` in `agents.sessions`: every session that needs you and is not hidden, asides, graph steps and graph runs (`kind: "run"`, which open their page) included, by `needsYouSince` (none last), the id breaking ties. The iPad steps through `needsRank` and never sorts. From a session not in that list, ⌃⌘J goes to the oldest and ⇧⌃⌘J to the newest; the list wraps around. With nothing waiting the key says "nothing needs you" and the focus stays.

Only the user runs these commands: a call from an agent session is refused.

## Asides

An aside is a read-only side conversation forked from a session to dig into one option of its pending question or a passage of its output, without touching the parent's context or transcript (spec `clubmate-notes/superpowers/specs/2026-11-16-side-threads-design.md`). The plugin decides everything below and sends it as fields; the Mac and the phone only render them.

The session. An aside is a session with `parentSession` (the session it came from) and `aside: {state, label, anchor}` in `agents.sessions`; that is the one shape clients read (the stored record, also in `agents.transcript`'s session, keeps `asideInfo {anchor, sent, kept}`). Its mode can't change (`agents.mode` is refused and its composer offers no modes), and it can't be handed off. The anchor is `{elicitation, field, option, question}` for an option of a pending question, `{open, option, question}` for an option of an open-items decision, or `{message, quote}` for a passage; it is display and seed text only. `agents.aside {session, anchor: [{key, value}]}` opens the aside or returns the one already open for that anchor, compared without `question`: `{session, reopened}`. `canAside` on a session says it can have asides: it is open, has been prompted, is neither an aside nor a service session, and its agent offers `session/fork` (assumed until an agent process has said otherwise). Otherwise `agents.aside` fails with `fork_unsupported`.

The fork. On attach the aside calls `session/fork` with the parent's agent session id instead of `session/new`, so it starts from the parent's latest point, then switches to `plan` mode; an agent without a plan mode closes the fork and the aside stops with an error. After a plugin restart it resumes the fork, in `plan` again. Its `_meta` comes from its own record (`CLUBMATE_AGENT_SESSION` is the aside's id) with `disallowedTools` `ExitPlanMode`, `mcp__clubmate` and `mcp__clubmate-dev` (a server name in `disallowedTools` matches that name exactly). The user's settings and other MCP servers stay. It gets no finish or AFK policy, no open-items text, no self-improvement plugin and no Chrome. The user's first message goes out after one framing text block, "You are an aside forked from this session to discuss …", which the transcript doesn't show.

| `aside.state` | When | Tree detail | Needs you |
|---|---|---|---|
| `open` | waiting for the user to write | open | no |
| `thinking` | its turn runs | thinking… | no |
| `asks` | its own permission or question is pending | asks you | yes |
| `sent` | something went back and it is still open | sent | no |

Needs you. An aside needs you only in `asks`, and it posts a notification only then. Its parent's status, `needsYou` and `childStatus` don't change with its asides: an aside is not a child for `childStatus`, the child limit, `agents.busy`, the parent's wakes or its prompt policies. Only an aside that asks lifts its parent's `group` to `needsYou` and lists the top-level session above it under the Mac sidebar's "Needs you" filter (at any depth, the same rule on both), so the aside can be reached; a running aside lifts nothing. Nor do asides change a parent's `taskSummary`, task rows or `archivable`. Agent sessions can't see or drive an aside: `agents.sessions` and `agents.transcript` from an agent leave it out, and `agents.aside*` take only the user's word.

Send back. `agents.aside-summary {session}` prompts the aside for a five-sentence summary meant for the parent; its next agent message is the text. `agents.aside-send {session, to, text, close}` sends nothing to an agent:
- `to: answer` prefills the parent's pending question for the anchored option. The card's choice shows the option `on` and, for a single-select question with an Other field, the text as Other `on` with its `value` (the AskUserQuestion note). A multi-select question gets the pick only, and the text goes to the composer draft. A card that answers on pick is refused with "this question can't be prefilled; send it as a draft". An open-items anchor writes the option and the Other text into that card's draft. A question that was answered meanwhile fails with "the question is no longer pending".
- `to: draft` sets the parent's `composerDraft {text, rev}` (in `agents.sessions` and the composer node's `draft`): the quote with `> ` on each line, a blank line, then the text. The user's next accepted prompt clears the draft. `rev` grows with each draft the session gets and never restarts, also after a clear (the record keeps `draftRev`), since clients apply a draft only when `rev` grows.

The aside is closed afterwards unless `close` is false, and is `sent` until then. While the parent's question is pending (and is not a card that answers on pick), the aside's own page shows it as a choice with `pinned: true` and action `asidePick`; a pick there, or `agents.aside-pick {session, value}`, prefills the option the same way without closing the aside.

Option marks. On the question card and the open-items card an option with an aside carries `aside: {id, state}`, and the choice has `asideAction: "asideAsk"` while `canAside`; that action with the option's value opens or reopens its aside.

Tree. Asides hang under their parent (a task's orchestrator included) after its agent-started children, oldest first (a new aside goes last, see [Session order](#session-order)), with the `bubble.left.and.bubble.right` glyph, the label "Aside · ‹option›" or "Aside · “‹passage, 40 characters›”" and the detail above; from 3 on they fold into one "Asides · N" row, which opens when one of them is revealed. They don't count in the parent's delete confirmation.

Keep. `agents.aside-keep {session}` makes the aside an ordinary session: it moves to the top level of its parent's worktree with `forkedFrom` set to the parent (clients show "forked from ‹parent›"), loses `aside` in `agents.sessions` and its disallowed tools, and takes the parent's current mode. The plugin closes the fork's agent session and resumes it with a normal session's `_meta`, so it has the Clubmate tools again, then sets the mode, since a resume brings back the mode the session last had. Its title drops the "Aside · " prefix. An aside whose fork never started can't be kept. It sends no turn; its next prompt goes out after one framing text block, "You are now a standalone session in ‹worktree›; you don't own the task of "‹parent›"; don't land it.". It owns none of the parent's tasks or finish mode, and closing it keeps it like any session. While its turn runs Keep is refused with "the aside is busy".

On the phone.
