The phone draws the cards natively from `agents.transcript`, so for a user caller the transcript carries what the Mac gets as SDUI props, at the top level and in full on every reply (a delta too): `prefills` `[{elicitation, field, value, note}]` (field is the question's key) and `[{open, value, note}]` (written only by Send back or a strip pick, never by the Mac's own picks), `optionAsides` `[{elicitation, field | open, option, id, state}]`, and on an aside's own transcript `pinned` (the strip's choice node, as above) and `asidePanel {question, option, header, text, label, description, answer, multi}`: the 1-based question and option numbers (an open item's Other is the option after the last), the anchored question's words, `answer` and the question number as the Send back panel decides them (the same rule as the Mac's "Answer question N") and `multi` when its note goes to the composer. An open-items prefill for Other has an empty `value` and the text as `note`. `aside.detail` in `agents.sessions` is the tree detail above. `agents.aside` with an option anchor and no `question` fills it from the pending question or open item.

Close. Closing an aside deletes it: its log, checkpoints and agent session (`session/close`, `session/delete`). Any close of its parent (by hand, auto-close, a hand-off, a removed worktree or workspace) deletes its asides; auto-close waits until the parent's asides are quiet for the hour too, and checks them again in the same step that closes the parent, so an aside that starts a turn while auto-close runs keeps its parent open. A prompt to an aside whose parent is closed is refused ("the aside's session is closed or gone"). For an aside with turns that sent nothing, `agents.busy` says "Close without sending?".

On the Mac. The aside opens beside its parent: Ask aside (an option's hover button or menu; on the open items card `a` then the option's 1–4 or `o` (Other: a Send back as the answer goes in as its Other text), which asks without picking it and disarms on ⎋ or any other key; ⌘\ on a passage) shows it in the slot `agents.aside` next to the parent (under it below 1100pt) and focuses its composer, so the card stays in view; the plugin keeps which aside is beside which parent per workspace. Its header reads `Aside · ‹option›` with a `read-only` badge, `from ‹parent›` (⌘[: the parent, scrolled to the card or passage), Send back (⇧⌘↩), Keep (⇧⌘K), Full page (⇧⌘\) and × (closes it, confirming with `agents.busy`'s message). Full page selects the aside in `agents.main` with the parent's question pinned on top and Beside in its header; clicking an aside's row in the tree opens it there too. The transcript starts with a "Forked from the question" (or "Forked from a passage") block; the passage renders its inline markdown (code, emphasis); list lines keep their "- " markers, since the `markdown` node is inline-only (the phone draws bullets). A parent with open asides has Aside (or `Asides · N`) in its header, ⌘], which shows the next one beside it. Send back opens a panel at the end of the aside: To (Answer question N while that question is pending and doesn't answer on pick, else Draft in its composer), From (Summary, which runs `agents.aside-summary` when the panel opens and fills the text when that turn ends; if the turn ends without a message the panel switches to Last message and says so; or Last message, the aside's last message before any summary request; switching refills the text unless you typed in it), the text, and "close the aside after" (on); ⌘↩ sends it through `agents.aside-send` and ⎋ cancels. After a send that closes the aside, the parent's composer has focus. Keep makes it an ordinary session and opens it; × (at the end of the header's title line, never in the "…" menu) and ⌥⌘⌫ while anything in the aside pane has focus (its prompt, a message, a card) close it.

## Task tree

A session with a task (`task` in `agents.sessions`) or with a pending connect request has its repos as children in the Mac sidebar, in landing order, each named by its repo with a folder glyph. The plugin builds these rows; single sessions and ordinary children keep their rows. A repo's row is its child session when it has one, otherwise an item with no session behind it that selects the orchestrator (`repo:<orchestrator>:<path>`, skipped by ⌃⌘↓/⌃⌘↑). Other children of the session follow the repos.

| Row | Word | Glyph | Tone |
|---|---|---|---|
| `merged` | merged | `checkmark.circle` | success |
| `onBranch` | on branch | `checkmark.circle` | none |
| `prOpen` | PR open | `arrow.triangle.pull` | none |
| `left` | left | `minus.circle` | none |
| `failed` | failed | `xmark.circle` | danger |
| `asking` for you, or a `Land ‹repo›?` request | asking | `exclamationmark.circle` | warning |
| `asking` for its orchestrator | asking | `arrow.up.circle` | accent |
| `running` | elapsed ("4 min", "2 h"), else running | `play.circle` | accent |
| `ready` with no request | queued | `circle` | none |
| `waits` | waits | `circle` | none |
| a repo with a pending connect request, once the session's turn ended | connect? | `exclamationmark.circle` | warning |
| the same while the turn runs | connect? | `circle` | none |

Every word fits in 9 characters. The parent's detail is its summary, also `taskSummary` in `agents.sessions` (a pending connect alone shows it on the Mac row only): "2 need you" counts child sessions that need you, Land requests, repos asking to connect, the parent's own permission or question and, once any of those is there, failed repos; failed repos alone read "1 failed"; with nothing to do it reads "8 repos". A summary that wants you is amber on the parent's detail and glyph, unless the parent itself stopped with an error; the failed child stays red.

The parent is open by default while its session is open. It folds the first time you open it after its task is finished (every repo landed or left, no turn running, nothing waiting); unfolding it by hand holds. A folded parent keeps the rows that need you, failed or are asking pinned under it (`pinned` on a tree item), which is the only case where a session that needs you sits under a folded row.

A repo child of a task with two or more repos shows a crumb above its header: Agents / ‹task› / ‹repo›, "2 of 8", and ⌃ ⌄ buttons with ⌥↑ ⌥↓ moving to the repo before and after it that has a session. There is no wrap-around: at the end the button is disabled. While the repo waits, its header carries a badge with what for ("waits for internal-auth").

The title pill reads "ads +1" in the accent ink while a folder an agent added was not seen (`seen` on the folder); opening the title picker marks them seen (`workspace.folder.seen`). Until then `git.worktree-new` on a folder AFK added and nobody has seen runs none of the repo's hooks or filters while it checks out, included config too, so LFS files stay pointers; if it cannot list them, or the checkout fails, it removes the worktree and the branch it made for it and reports the error. That covers the checkout only: later git commands in that worktree, an agent's or a plugin's, read the repo's config as usual and can run them. Two plugin commands that would run them are refused in such a folder until it is seen, with "it was connected while you were away and you haven't seen it yet: open the title picker to look at it first": `git.branch-switch`, and `git.worktree-remove` without `discard` (its `git status` and `git worktree remove` can run clean filters), so a child in such a folder isn't checked for a merge at all: it stays open and done with `cleanupOnSeen` on its record, and the cleanup runs again when the folder is marked seen (`workspace.folder.seen`), closing the child if it is still open and removing its worktree and branch if it was merged; a child closed in between is cleaned up too. A connect that AFK did is a system row in the orchestrator's transcript (`repo` and `path` on the notice): neutral, an AFK badge, its time and what happened (opened a PR, or a PR at most), and one action, Remove from ‹workspace›…, which opens the Remove folder sheet. Once the folder is gone the row says so and the action goes. On the phone the notice stays a plain line.

**Archive task.** Archive Task… on the parent row menu and Archive task… in the Repos card footer show once every repo merged, stays on its branch, has a PR open or was left, and no session of the task runs; the sheet (`agents.archive-sheet`, slot `agents.sheet`) is never automatic. It comes from `agents.archive-plan {session}`, read now, and says per repo what happens to the worktree and the local branch and why. A worktree goes when it is clean; ignored files don't count, except secret files (below). One with uncommitted files, a secret file or detached commits, or that an open session outside the task works in, stays, with what it would lose (`.env.local would be lost`) or the session it closes and a Remove it anyway box; ticking one turns Archive red with the Remove button's words (`Archive · lose 3 files, .env.local and 1 commit`, `Archive · closes ‹session›`), plain until then. One that holds a submodule or a repo, is locked (it says why), one a running session of any workspace works in, one open in Code in any workspace or one that couldn't be read stays with no box. A local branch goes under `git branch -d` rules: it must be proven in the default branch (by ancestry, or for a squash by `git merge-tree`, `landed` on `git.worktree-state`; `git branch -d` decides, and only that exact proof, read again right before, deletes a branch it calls not fully merged, and only while it still points at the tip seen; no repo hook runs), and an open PR from the child's last report, a remote branch of that name, the repo's stay rule or a worktree that stays keep it. Since git can't delete a branch a worktree holds, the sheet says "it stays with its worktree" where the board says Delete branch. Remote branches are never touched. Cron jobs that run in a worktree that goes are named, and pause; a worktree that then stays resumes them. "Also remove ‹folder› from ‹workspace›" is offered, unticked, for a folder this task added that no other open session or task uses, except the first folder; it goes through the Remove folder path. Close sessions only moves the sessions to Closed and touches nothing else. `agents.archive {session, choices}` runs it: a worktree goes only when `choices` name it with the `seen` of the plan, and each is read once more right before it goes (tip, merged, cost), checked for holders, sessions and tasks as well as terminals of every workspace (`git.worktree-sessions`, which also sees a shell that `cd`ed in, the editor's own Neovim aside), and passed to `git.worktree-remove` with what you saw; anything that changed or failed is kept and listed on a sheet titled `“‹task›” is archived`, with Close. Refused while a session of the task runs, waits or is queued, and while a repo hasn't finished. Everything here is the user's only: the commands and the sheet's actions refuse an agent, and `git.branch-delete` takes the agents plugin's word alone.

**Clean up worktrees.** "Clean up worktrees…" in the title picker's footer and folder actions, and "Clean up 163…" on a folder's Stale row, open one sheet (`agents.cleanup-sheet`, `agents.cleanup-plan {folder?}`), never automatic. It lists every worktree that is stale or made by hand, once even when two folders share a repo, except a live one, the main or default checkout (in a bare layout, the worktree on the default branch), one open in Code in any workspace and a child's in an open task that hasn't settled, which Archive task deals with, in groups by what removing them costs, each row in one group, so the groups add up: `191 · 182 stale, 9 by hand`.

| Group | Goes | Ticks |
|---|---|---|
| Merged and clean | worktree and local branch | ticked, group box |
| PR merged, remote branch deleted | worktree and local branch | ticked with proof, group box |
| Remote branch deleted, not found in main | worktree; branch stays | none |
| No commits for 30 d, all pushed | worktree; branch stays | one at a time |
| Unpushed commits or uncommitted files | worktree; branch stays | one at a time |
| Made by hand | worktree | one at a time |

Proof for the second group is the child's merged report, which git checked, or the change in the default branch, by ancestry or `git merge-tree`; unpushed commits beat the report, and only that exact proof beats them, as a squash leaves its branch unpushed by definition. There is no "PR closed without merging" group, since git can't tell it. A branch still on the remote, an open PR or a stay rule keeps the branch in any group, and the row says so. Ignored files (node_modules, build output, caches) are regenerable: they cost nothing, so a row with only those is ticked like a clean one. A secret file is the exception: a row holding one stays in its group unticked and says so (`keeps .env.local`), the group says how many (`2 worktrees with secret files, not ticked`), and its group box or its own box ticks it; nothing else that costs anything is ever ticked for you. A secret file is an ignored file named `.env`, `.env.*`, `*.pem`, `*.key`, `.npmrc` or `.netrc` that isn't inside an ignored folder (`node_modules/` counts as a whole, so a package's test key doesn't hold its worktree); the list lives in `secretNames` in `plugins/git/index.go` and `secrets` on `git.worktree-state` ([SDUI protocol](sdui-protocol.md)). Clean up, Archive and Remove folder share it. Rows that can't be read, hold a repo or are locked have no box, and a locked one says why. A row with an open session that isn't running isn't pre-ticked, stays out of the group box and names the session its own box would close, like a child just restored; a session that opened after the sheet read the row keeps it. The row and the button turn red with what is lost, in the Remove words (`Remove 138 · lose 3 files, .env.local and 1 commit`; two or more secret files read `2 secret files`); the button is plain while nothing is lost and waits for every ticked row to be read. A filter or a folder narrows the list, and only what shows counts: a tick on a hidden row removes nothing.

The plan comes from the cached index and one `git.worktree-unpushed` per folder, so 400 worktrees open at once. Cost is read only for what is shown or pre-ticked, 24 at a time, at most 8 at once in git, and a row moves group when its numbers say so. Rows go one by one through the Archive path: re-read right before it goes, holders of every workspace, terminals and Code checked, a change or failure kept and listed on a sheet with Close. Cron jobs that run in a listed worktree are named, and pause when it goes; one whose worktree stays resumes. The sheet and its actions are yours only.

**Restore.** `agents.restore {session}` (Restore on a closed task's row menu and footer, `reopen` in `agents.sessions`) brings a closed session and its closed children back, read-write, and returns `{restored, stayed: [{session, title, why}]}`. It is refused, with the reason under the footer, when the session's own worktree is gone, its folder left the workspace or the worktree was recreated on another branch than the one it closed on (`closedBranch`). A child that can't return stays closed and is named under the footer, so a task with one removed worktree still comes back. Any other closed session keeps Reopen, which brings back only that session, under the same refusals. The board's "recreates a worktree from its branch" isn't built.

**Remove folder sheet.** Remove from ‹workspace›… (the AFK row, and the title picker through `agents.folder-sheet`) opens one sheet over the window in any section, drawn by the plugin in the slot `agents.sheet` (`docs/sdui-protocol.md`). It says what removing changes, from `agents.folder-impact`: sessions, worktrees, graphs, hooks, instructions, the trust that is kept per repo, the Cron jobs that run in the folder, each by name, and that Backlog and triggers are untouched. A row with nothing to say stays away, except Sessions, Cron jobs and Untouched. Removing never deletes files, except the worktree whose box you tick, through `git.worktree-remove`. A worktree shows what its box costs: files that are lost, secret files (a single one by name; other ignored files cost nothing), detached commits on no ref, or commits that stay on its branch. Only the first 5 worktrees, those with open sessions first, have a box, and one holding an initialised submodule or a repo inside has none and says why, since what's in there can't be counted. Remove (⌘↩) is plain until a ticked worktree would lose something, then it is red and names it (`Remove · lose 1 file, .env and 1 commit`, without the workspace's name or the sheet's note, so the cost is never cut); ⎋ cancels. It is disabled while a session of this workspace runs, waits or is queued in the folder, a session of any workspace does so in a ticked worktree, while a task of this workspace still lands there or an unsettled task of any workspace has its child in a ticked worktree, or when it is the workspace's last folder, and the sheet says what holds it and follows it live. If a ticked worktree changed since the sheet read it, nothing is removed and the sheet shows the new cost. The check marks the folder and the ticked worktrees as leaving under a short lock, then the folder goes and the worktrees after it, so a refusal or a failed folder loses no files, and until it ends a new session, a handoff or a prompt there is refused while everything else carries on. A worktree is read once more before its teardown: if it would lose more than you saw, a terminal of any workspace is open in it, or git fails, it stays on disk and the sheet, now titled `internal-auth left ads`, gives its path and why, with Close. What teardown itself writes doesn't count. If Cron stops answering after the sheet read it, nothing happens and the sheet says so; pressing Remove again on the sheet that now says Cron couldn't be read goes on without pausing. Removing pauses the Cron jobs with a reason (`internal-auth left ads`); if the folder can't be removed they resume. Triggers stay as they are. The sheet and its buttons are yours only: `agents.folder-sheet` and its actions refuse an agent.

## Neighbours

Children work in parallel, each in its own worktree. Two of them can change the same files. The agents plugin finds this overlap and tells the sessions that must know. It does not lock files and it does not refuse a start or a landing. Code: `plugins/agents/neighbours.go`.

**Neighbour.** Session N is a neighbour of session S when all of these are true:

- N is open, not an aside, not a night session (its sandbox gets no git call), not a land gate reviewer and not a node of an open gate run.
- N works in the same repo (the same main checkout) as S, in a worktree other than S's, and that worktree still exists.
- N's branch is not the default branch and is not landed into N's base (`gitrepo.LandedIn`: an ancestor, or a squash whose merge gives the base's tree). A branch with no commits of its own is never "landed".
- N is not on S's base chain: N is not an ancestor or a descendant of S, N's branch is not S's base, and S's branch is not N's base.
- N and S have one or more touched files in common.

**Touched files** of a session are the files changed in its worktree against `git merge-base <base> HEAD`: commits, tracked changes (staged or not, deletions too) and untracked files that git does not ignore (`gitrepo.TouchedFiles`). The base is the branch's recorded base (`git.worktree-base`), else the default branch. The plugin computes them on demand and keeps them for each session until it sees a sign of a change: the worktree's HEAD or branch, the tip of the base, the worktree's index file (`gitrepo.IndexStamp`), or a tool call of a session that works in that worktree. For the session that a delivery point asks about, a tool call counts at once; for its neighbours it counts when their entry is older than 10 s, so a busy group of sessions does not compute each other again on each prompt. An edit by hand gives none of these signs, so an entry is also computed again after 2 min (`CLUBMATE_AGENTS_TOUCHED_AGE`). HEAD, the branch and the default branch come from one `git worktree list` for each repo; all sessions of the repo share it for 2 s. It skips a session of another repo before any git call (it reads the main checkout from the worktree's `.git` file) and computes the rest in parallel; a computation that the caller's time limit stops is not kept.

**Delivery.** Each point below sends text only when there is an overlap, and only for (neighbour, file) pairs that it did not tell to that receiver before. The told pairs are stored on the receiver (`neighboursTold`), so a restart does not send a pair again. A pair is stored only after the prompt reached the agent (the `session/prompt` call returned no error, also after a retry) or the reply was sent, never while the prompt is built; a prompt that failed tells its pairs again on the next turn. Pairs of closed sessions are dropped.

- **Orchestrator.** The `[Clubmate] Child …` line of a child whose turn ended gets one more sentence: `It changes files that open session "‹title›" (‹id›, branch ‹b›) changes too: a.go, b.go.` The key is the unordered pair of sessions and the file: when A and B both change `a.go`, the parent is told once, not again when B stops; a later C and B on `a.go` is told. A child that landed itself has no worktree when its turn ends, or is closed while `git.land` kept its worktree (a terminal or an editor has it open), so the landing (below) stores its overlap on its record (`landOverlap`), and the line uses that for a closed child or a child with no worktree.
- **Lander.** `agents.land-gate` gives `neighbours` with an approved reply, also in a repo with no gate. `git.land` adds it to `note` after the commit: `Open session "‹title›" (‹id›, branch ‹b›) changes a.go too; its landing can conflict with yours.` The files are those of the landed change (old target tip to the landed tree). The told pairs and `landOverlap` are stored only when `git.land` reports the commit (`agents.land-done`), so a landing that fails after the gate tells the pairs again on the next try.
- **Session with a later prompt.** From the second prompt on, the prompt gets a `[Clubmate neighbours]` block: `Open sessions change files that you change: "‹title›" (session ‹id›, branch ‹b›): a.go, b.go. Before you edit these files, read their version: git -C ‹worktree› diff ‹merge-base› -- a.go b.go. Do not edit their worktree. If your changes clash, tell your parent session.` A file that is untracked in the neighbour's worktree shows as `‹worktree›/‹file› (new, not committed)`. A top-level session gets `tell the user in your report` at the end.
- **`agents.sessions`.** Each session has `overlaps: [{session, branch, files, more?}]`: at most 10 files per neighbour, `more` counts the rest. The list reads only a cache and never runs git. When the last check of the workspace is older than 10 s, or an open session has no entry, a check starts in the background: one pass finds the overlaps of all open sessions and runs git only for a session with a sign of a change (see Touched files). A change kicks the workspace, so the phone's delta carries it.

The texts show at most 4 neighbours and 10 files for each, then `and N more`. Every Clubmate user gets this with the app: there is no setting, no CLAUDE.md line and no line in the MCP instructions. Nothing is sent when there is no overlap.

## Title picker

With two or more git folders the pill names the folder Code shows, "ads / internal-auth ▸ main". The title picker draws what `agents.worktrees` decides: up to 20 worktrees in the workspace everything by folder, above that it opens on search with Now, Live (never capped), Recent (7 days, 6 at most) and Folders, and a folder shows Live, Recent, By task (2, then a count), Others (3, then a count), and Made by hand and Stale folded. A workspace of one folder has no folder rows: few worktrees list plainly, many open on that folder's sections. Search spans folder, branch, task and session names in every folder, live first and stale last, a task being one row, and only filtering by typing lives in the client. Picking a worktree in another folder moves Code there through `editor.code-root`, even when that folder's selection doesn't change, and so do Open in Code on a Repos row and picking a repo child in Code's chat. If the agents plugin doesn't answer the picker falls back to the plain list of `git.worktrees` and `git.branches`.

## Repos in a folder

A workspace folder that isn't a git repo but holds repos, like `~/develop/kufar` with 79 repos one level down, has no worktrees, branches or repo children of its own. The git plugin finds the repos in it (`git.repos-in`, [SDUI](sdui-protocol.md)): two levels down, skipping hidden directories and `node_modules`, never looking inside a repo it found, and stopping after 3 s with what it has. The Add repos sheet lists them with boxes, newest commit first, nothing ticked, with a filter field, a box that ticks every row the filter shows and the age of each repo's last commit; a repo that is already a folder has no box and reads "in ‹workspace›". Ticks stay while the filter changes. Add N to ‹workspace› (⌘↩) adds the ticked repos as folders in the sheet's order, after the folders the workspace has, so the parent stays first; they are yours (`addedBy` empty) and seen, so no "+N" and no AFK rules. ⎋ cancels. A walk that ran out of time says so under the list.

The sheet opens by itself once per folder, the first time the workspace's window opens with that folder, or when the folder is added while it is open, and only while no other sheet is up; the plugin remembers it (`reposOffered/<workspace>/<folder>` in its storage), so cancelling ends the offer. After that "Add repos from ‹folder›…" stays in the title picker's footer next to "Add a folder to ‹workspace›…" (`picker.addRepos`), as long as the folder holds a repo that isn't a folder yet. A folder whose repos are all added is offered nothing.

A file shows once, under the deepest folder that holds it: with `kufar` and `kufar/ads-management` both folders, search and the docs list read `ads-management/main.go`, not `kufar/ads-management/main.go`. Code's file tree is Neovim's, of the one folder Code shows, so with `kufar` shown it still lists `ads-management/` as a directory.

An agent connecting a repo inside one of the workspace's folders (`agents.connect-repo` with a path under `kufar`) still gets the card, like any repo the workspace doesn't have: the agent can write inside that folder, so a `.git` it made there could carry config that runs commands, and only your yes or the Add repos sheet adds it. While the orchestrator is AFK the AFK rule applies instead.

## Review progress

A session that opened or received an open review (see [nvim integration § Review changes](nvim-integration.md#review-changes)) shows its progress. The agents plugin follows the bus topic `editor-nvim.review`, keeps every open review and its comment states until its `closed` event, stores them with the last event's seq in its storage (`reviews`) and on start resumes the topic from that seq, so progress and card states survive a restart of the plugin. When a review closes, its last comment states are written into the log of every session that holds its Review card, looked for among its opener, its recipient and the sessions in its worktree (`review/closed {id, states}`), and leave the storage, so a closed review's card keeps its final states for good; on start, states stored by an older version for a review that is not open are moved into the logs the same way. `editor-nvim.ready` (editor-nvim started again and holds no review) closes every open review the same way and gives each session the newest review whose `opener` or `recipient` it is: `reviewProgress {id, reviewed, total, open, sent, text, short}` in `agents.sessions`, `text` reading `review N/M · K` (K open comments) and `short` reading `N/M · K` (`N/M` without open comments). The phone row shows `text` in place of the age of a done, idle or review session ("review 5/15 · 1" instead of "2 h ago"). The Mac sidebar row shows `short` there under the review glyph (`eye.circle`) instead of the status glyph, so it fits the 6 em a list keeps for a detail; a running, waiting or failed row keeps its own words and glyph. The Mac session header adds the badge `Review N/M · K issue(s)`, after Send `Review M/M · N comments sent`, success once every file is reviewed, and the button "Open review" ⌘R, which shows that review in the Code section: its nvim tab current, nvim focused (`editor-nvim.review-show {id}`). Closing the review drops the field, the row text and the header. Each review payload also publishes `agents.changed` kind `review` for its opener and recipient, which is how the phone learns the text changed. Neither client works the text out itself; the phone has no review panel. "Review changes" in the header and "Full diff" on an edit card pass the session and the turn, so the review reads `Before turn N` and the session is its opener. The agents plugin reads `editor.state` at start and again on `plugin.changed` for an editor: only while `review` is `diffview` does it send "Review changes" and "Full diff", and with `hidden` (both editors off) it also drops "Open in nvim" from tool cards; edit cards keep their inline diff, and the Mac and the phone show what it sends. On the Mac "Review changes" opens a popover whose "Compare with" picks session start (the first checkpoint) or a local branch (its merge-base with the worktree, the default branch listed first); the session remembers the pick until the plugin restarts, and the phone's button uses it.

## Search

`agents.search query=<text>` (remote, agent) finds the workspace's sessions, closed ones included, by title and by what was said in them: your prompts and the agent's replies, not thoughts or tool output. It is a case-insensitive substring match of the whole query. Up to 50 sessions come back, the ones whose title matches first, then the most recently updated, each with up to 3 hits `{kind: title|prompt|reply, turn, snippet}` and `older: true` when the session is in [Older](#older-sessions); a snippet is up to 120 characters from about 12 characters before the match (from the line start when that is nearer, else cut at a word), whitespace collapsed, as plain text (markdown marks and link targets dropped). The plugin reads the text from its own session logs (`sessions/<id>.jsonl`) once, in the background after it starts, and then only the lines appended since the last search, so typing stays fast with hundreds of sessions. The Mac and the phone render this result and never match or rank on their own.

On the Mac the Agents sidebar has a "Search sessions" field above the filter chips. While it holds text the chips and the tree give way to the results: a row per session (status glyph, title, when it was last updated) with its prompt and reply hits under it; clicking either opens the session. Cleared, the tree comes back. The phone's Agents list has the same search (stock `.searchable` under the large title, only when the Mac offers `agents.search`): while it holds text the list shows the workspace's matching sessions in the list's own rows, a closed one the list doesn't hold in a plain row with its status. Not done: jumping to the matched turn, and a key that focuses the field.

## Service sessions

A session started with its own system prompt or tool limits is a service session: the voice session (`systemPrompt`, `disallowedTools` or `mode` on `agents.new`, accepted only from the voice plugin) and the night sessions of [self-improvement](#self-improvement), which the agents plugin starts itself. Such a session has `service: true` in `agents.sessions`. The Mac sidebar lists it under the docked Service row at the bottom of the sidebar, outside the scrolling tree (see [Needs you](#needs-you) § Dock), folded by default; the phone in a "Service · N" row after Closed, folded too. It is not counted in "All" or "Running", nor in the Mac status bar's "N agents" (`active` is false), and its `group` is `service` (see [Needs you](#needs-you) § Groups). While it needs you it counts under "Needs you" and is never folded: on the Mac its group opens and reads "asks you" in an amber badge after its label, on the phone it moves to "Needs you". A session a service session starts with `agents.new` is not its child: it is a top-level session of yours, in the workspace's root, with your defaults, and it is selected like one you started. `model` on `agents.new`, from any caller (an agent through MCP, the user, `cm`), does not make a service session either: the session, a child included, keeps its parent, its group, advisor and worktree, and switches to that model right after `session/new`. A resume restores the model the session last ran on, so a later pick in the model menu sticks. When the agent offers no such model (or no model choice), the transcript gets a notice "Model <name> is not available" listing the offered ones, and the session runs on its current model. `firstMode` on `agents.new` (from the user or Backlog's Start agent only, which refuses anyone but the user and passes the caller on, so a session of any workspace is refused here too; an agent, a plain `cm` from an agent's shell or another plugin gets `invalid_params`) does not make a service session: it is the mode the session starts in instead of the worktree's remembered one, applied once on `session/new`; a resume takes the worktree's mode like any session.

## Gates

A repo can have land gates: then an agent's landing in it that a gate matches waits until a run of the gate's graph decided exactly that change (the built-in `opus-review` graph by default: one fresh read-only Opus session). Gates are config, like [triggers](triggers.md): `agents.gates` lists every gate for you and, for an agent, only the ones on its workspace's git folders (`{gates: [{id, repo, point, into, sessions?, wrote?, tool?, how?, text?, graph, test?, pin?, rounds, paused, createdAt, name, words?, state, last?, landings?}]}`, `repo` the canonical main checkout, `point` `land` (Landing), `turn` (Done, see [Done gates](#done-gates)) or `tool` (Tool call, see [Tool call gates](#tool-call-gates)), `into` which landings a land gate holds, `graph` the graph that decides (default `opus-review`; a gate keeps the graph it was saved with), `pin` the graph content hash it trusts, `hash` the graph's current content hash, `rounds` default 2, `name` the gate's name from its match: "Landing into any branch", "Landing into main" (the default branch's name), "Landing into the parent's branch", "Landing into release/*" (Done and Tool call gates are named in their sections); the rest is [what each gate shows](#the-gates-section)), and `agents.gate-add {repo, point?, into?, sessions?, wrote?, tool?, how?, text?, graph?, rounds?, test?}` (`point` default `land`; `into` and `test` (the [test command](#test-gates)) only for `land`, `sessions` and `wrote` only for `turn`, `tool`, `how` and `text` only for `tool`), `agents.gate-update {id, paused?, rounds?, into?, sessions?, wrote?, tool?, how?, text?, graph?, trust?, test?}` (`trust` sets `pin` and must equal the gate's current `hash`, else it fails as changed since shown; a new `graph` is pinned as it is now) and `agents.gate-delete {id}` take the user's word only, so an agent can't drop its own gate. Nothing is gated in a repo without one. Pausing a gate stops new holds only (ruling 11, the same for every point): what it already holds stays held until its review ends, and nothing is released or moved by the pause. A gate stored before `into` existed (`{kind: "fable-review"}`) reads as `point: land, into: any, graph: fable-review` (the default when it was stored).

`into` is `any` (default), `default` (a landing into the repo's default branch), `base`, named the parent's branch (a landing into the source branch's recorded [base](git.md#gitland), when that base is usable; only a branch that `git.worktree-new {from}` made from a worktree on a non-default branch, as `agents.new` with `branch` does from the caller's worktree, has a base, so a branch made with `git branch` has none and its landing into an integration branch is held only by an `any` gate or a gate on that branch's name; the base comes from the git plugin's storage, never from git config, and when it can't be read every `base` gate of the repo matches the landing, so it is reviewed; a `base` gate also matches a landing into any base the landing session or one of its ancestors had when it started or resumed, kept on each session as `bases`, so a session on B with base A that switches to a new branch or worktree still lands into A only through the gate), an exact branch name, or a branch glob (`*` stays within one `/` segment and `**` as a whole segment matches any depth: `release/*` holds `release/1.0`, `release/**` also `release/1.0/hotfix`; `into` can't hold spaces, start with `refs/`, or be `Any`, `Default` or `Base` in another case). A repo has at most one gate per `(point, into)`: adding or changing a gate to an existing match fails naming that gate's id. When several active gates match a landing, only the most specific one decides — an exact branch, then a glob (the longer pattern first, then the older gate), then `base`, then `default` (also when the default branch can't be read), then `any` — so a landing into main with an `any` and a `default` gate runs one review, logged under the `default` gate. You set them up in the [Gates section](#the-gates-section) on the Mac, or with `cm command run agents.gate-add repo=<path>`.

Agents land with [`git.land`](git.md#gitland) (the finish texts say so; a child whose parent is on a feature branch lands into that branch). A top-level session in finish mode merge whose branch has a usable [base](git.md#gitland) (recorded by the git plugin, existing and checked out in some worktree, wherever the session's worktree lives) is told to land into that base instead of main ("it squashes your branch into ‹base›"), and `git.land` without `into` lands there. The base is read once per worktree and branch and read again after the next `git.changed`. In a gated repo `git.land` from an agent asks `agents.land-gate` (only the git plugin may) for a verdict on `(old, tree)`: the target branch's tip and the tree the landing would leave there. Verdicts (`{old, oldTree?, tree, outcome, by, reviewer?, commit?, findings?, deferred?, at}`) are kept in the plugin's storage by `(old, tree)` and again by `(oldTree, tree)` (`land-verdict-tree/<oldTree>-<tree>`, the trees only), which is what `git.land` trusts, and mirrored to files under its state, `land-verdicts/<old>-<tree>.json`, which the git hook reads without a call (the hook keeps commit keys and also checks that the new commit's parent is `old`).

- An `approved` verdict lands. A moved target or a changed branch is a new key, so it needs a new review. A target that moved to a commit with the same tree finds an approval by `(oldTree, tree)` (see Carry); this is how a [review ahead](#landing-queue) lands.
- **Carry.** When the target moved since an approved review (`old₀` → `old₁`) and the landing's run is still live, the plugin compares the files the target changed (`git diff --name-only old₀ old₁`) with the files the landing changes on the new target (`old₁` → `tree₁`), and the landing's own change on both (`git diff --binary --full-index`, byte for byte; with no shared file this is the same as an equal patch). No shared file and the same change: the run is prompted again with a short prompt, "The base moved on other files since your approval: ‹files› … Confirm that it still holds on the new base", not the first-review prompt; the gate's test steps run again on `tree₁` with it, as in every round. An approval in that round (a reviewer's, or a deciding step's pass) is stored and logged `by: carried` with `carriedFrom: old₀` and `reason: base-moved`; its summary goes to the commit like a reviewer's, and the gate page's verdict row reads "approved · carried" (counted as approved in its History line) with the summary row under it. A move that changes no file (an empty commit, a revert pair, or a review ahead whose head landed the predicted tree) with the same tree is approved at once from the `(oldTree, tree)` verdict, `by: carried` with `carriedFrom` the commit it was reviewed on, with no prompt and no run, also while the gate waits for trust; the commit-keyed verdict for the new `old` is written then, for the hook. Only an approval by a reviewer, a deciding step or an earlier carry is carried; a Land anyway (`by: user`) or a docs-only approval is not. A shared file, a changed change, no approved verdict for `(old₀, tree₀)` or no live run: the full first-review prompt, as before. The graphs engine has no way to run only a gate's test step in a round, so a gate with both a test step and a reviewer also prompts the reviewer (with the short prompt).
- With none, a change whose every path ends in `.md` (case ignored), outside any `.claude/`, `.clubmate/`, `.github/`, `.agents/`, `skills/`, `commands/`, `agents/`, `prompts/` or `rules/` folder and not a `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `GEMINI.md` or `SKILL.md`, is approved `by: docs`.
- Otherwise the plugin starts a run of the gate's graph, or prompts the run already deciding this landing, and `git.land` fails with `review pending: <session>` (the session of the graph's verdict node, the first agent node whose `outcomes` hold both `approved` and `changes`), or, while a deciding step of the run runs in this round (see [Test gates](#test-gates)), `review pending: the gate runs <node> (<command>) on this change. End your turn; Clubmate prompts you with the verdict.` (with `; waiting: another landing's tests run` while it waits for the repo's test lock); a second `git.land` of the same change while that step runs gets the same text and starts nothing; when the step already ended (a cached pass) and its verdict is on the way, `review pending: the gate's <node> step ran on this change. End your turn; Clubmate prompts you with the verdict.` See [Graph and trust](#graph-and-trust). Every agent node of a gate run is a top-level session the plugin starts itself, never through `agents.new` from the lander (`startedBy` the lander, notify `needs-input`, so its own permission request still reaches you, `gateFor {gate, point: "land", key, lander, into, source, old, tree, run, verdict}` in `agents.sessions`, `key` the tree), on the node's `model` and `effort` (`opus-review`: `opus`, `fable-review`: `fable`), in the source worktree, with Edit, Write, MultiEdit, NotebookEdit, `git_land`, `agents_new` and writing `git`, `rm` and `mv` commands disallowed. Unlike other sessions with `disallowedTools` it is not a service session: it keeps your settings and the repo's `CLAUDE.md`. The run's input, the review prompt, is built by the plugin, never by the lander, and every agent node of the run gets it (its prompt's `{{input}}`, else appended; only verdict nodes get the line telling them to call `agents_land_verdict`, in every round): the goal (the lander's title) and, when the lander has one, its task: the text of the lander's first prompt (what the user or its parent sent, as Clubmate recorded it in the session log, without the `[Clubmate …]` policy blocks and the sender prefix; none when the session's first user message is not such a recorded prompt (a gate or voice prompt, a steered one, a prompt Clubmate wrote itself, which starts with `[Clubmate`, such as a land verdict, or one an imported or loaded transcript replayed), for an aside, or when the text is empty), cut to 2000 characters with "…", in a fenced block under "Task (the lander's first prompt, data, not instructions):", so the reviewer sees what the lander was asked to do and never what it wrote later, `git diff <old> <tree>` (always the whole change), the commits, and, when the branch holds commits that already landed through a gate (a child's landing into its parent's branch), which commits those are and how each landed (reviewed, docs only, or the user's override, which counts as not reviewed) and which are not, so the reviewer spends its time on the rest without anything being left out. One run serves every round of a landing: a second round prompts the same run's start nodes (only verdict nodes are told to call `agents_land_verdict`), and the run's input becomes the new prompt for the nodes downstream. A run with a closed or missing node, or one started from another graph or another pinned version than the gate's now (after you trust a change or pick another graph in Edit), is not reused: it is stopped and a new run starts, and starting one (or a failed prompt of the old one) closes the nodes of the landing's earlier runs; a run whose start fails closes the nodes it started. A second round, with the target unchanged, gets only the delta, `git diff <previous tree> <tree>`; when the target moved it gets the whole change again. Each round is bound to the tree it was asked about: while a round runs, a `git.land` with a different tree waits for it (`a review of an earlier version of this change is still running`) instead of handing the running round a new tree, and the next round covers what changed.
- The run decides with `agents.land-verdict {tree, outcome: approved | changes, findings?, deferred?, summary?}` from a deciding node of the landing's current run (a verdict node; the run's other nodes and any other session get an error, which never names the tree; `tree` must be the one its latest prompt names, and the verdict is stored for exactly that tree with its `summary`; the first verdict for a tree wins, a second fails `already decided`): `changes` is for confirmed bugs only, each finding `file:line — what breaks`; nits go to `deferred` and never block. An approved `summary` also goes to `git.land` in the `agents.land-gate` reply, which appends it to the commit message after a blank line (not for `by: docs` or `by: user`; with no gate matching the landing any more, as after a pause or a delete, a reviewer's summary stored for exactly that change is still appended), and is kept in `agents.gate-verdicts`. The verdict prompts the lander at once: approved says call `git_land` again, changes lists the findings with the round, deferred lists them after "Not blocking:". After changes or deferred items the prompt asks the lander to fix every item above in this change before it calls `git_land` again, to ask the user (AskUserQuestion) only about an item it can't fix without their decision, and to put an item in the backlog only when it is a separate task of its own. The verdict is also the verdict node's outcome in the run, which a `changes` or `approved` never stops: the run stays for the next round. A verdict node turn that ends without a verdict is prompted once; a second one, or a node turn that fails or is cancelled, or a node closed before the verdict, stops the run. Any stop of a gate run while its round has no verdict (that, the turn budget, a failed step, a round whose moves all end before one reaches a verdict node (when an agent node with outcome edges ended its turn without an outcome yet, the run waits 30 s for that outcome first; the wait lives in memory, so a plugin restart within it stops the run at once), the user's `agents.graph-stop`) tells the lander the review ended without one, and its next `git_land` starts a new run; it is never a pass. The stopped run's node sessions are closed at once (a turn still running is cancelled); one that stopped with an error stays open, so its row shows under the lander. Other nodes' turns end as in any graph and pass their reply on along the edges. A gate run has no caller, so neither the lander nor its children can stop it.
- After the gate's `rounds` verdicts of `changes` for one landing (lander, target branch, source branch), the next `git.land` opens a request instead of a third review: the lander's `landReviews[]` holds it, it needs you ([Needs you](#needs-you)), `pending` carries its id (`lg_…`), and so does `cardRequests`, the gate card ids among `pending` (a held turn's and a held call's too), whose Needs you notification the plugin posts itself, so a client posts no Permission alert for them, `pendingSummary` is `{kind: "land", text: "‹graph› still asks for changes after N rounds", onMac: true}`, and the Mac and the phone dock it as a `request.land` card (title "‹graph› still asks for changes after N rounds", the graph's title stored as `landReviews[].graph` when the card opens; question "Land ‹branch› without another review?") with the last findings; the Mac offers Land anyway (`merge`) ⌥1, One more round (`more`) ⌥2 and Stop (`leave`) ⌥3, the phone's land dock still only Review… and Land anyway, so One more round and Stop are Mac-only for now; the card's buttons and the command answer it the same way. `agents.land-answer` answers it, the user's word only, and `agents.land-commits` lists its commits for the phone's sheet. Land anyway stores an approved verdict `by: user` for the change the card was opened for and prompts the lander to call `git_land` again (if the change or its target moved after that answer, the next card says it was asked again because something moved); One more round raises that landing's cap by one (`landReviews[].extra`, so `rounds + extra` is the cap that `review n/m…` and the next card count against), clears the request and prompts the lander to call `git_land` again for one more review round (when the card's change already has a stored `changes` verdict the prompt says to fix its findings and commit first, since `git_land` returns them until then); the extra round reviews the delta from the last reviewed tree (the card keeps its own change in `reqOld`/`reqTree`, apart from the last reviewed `old`/`tree`), and it does not count as an answer for the next card's "asked again" note; Stop prompts it not to land. A waiting request keeps the lander from auto-closing; a closed lander's request no longer needs you and can't be answered.
- **Notification.** When a gate's card opens (a landing past its rounds, `landReviews[].request`, a held turn's card, `held.request`, see [Done gates](#done-gates), or a held call's card, `calls[].request`, see [Tool call gates](#tool-call-gates)) the agents plugin posts one `needs-input` notification for the session, with the card's request id as `request`, like a permission: a Mac notification and a phone push, the session's title as the title, the card's title and question as the message ("‹graph› still asks for changes after N rounds · Land ‹branch› without another review?" for a landing, "‹graph› still asks for changes after N rounds · Accept the plan as done?" for a held turn), collapsed per session, and a tap opens the session. It is posted once per card: a re-render, a second `git_land` while the card waits or a restart (the cards a session loads with count as posted) posts nothing, and a gate run in progress, which has no card, is silent. A session whose effective policy is `muted` (its own or a parent's) gets none; `needs-input` and `all` get it. A turn of the session that ends done while its card waits posts no "Finished", so it never replaces the card's notification; "Asks you a question" and "Stopped with an error" still post. When the card goes away (answered with any action on the Mac or the phone, the hold released or dropped, the session closed) the plugin publishes `agents.changed` `resolved` with the request and `requestKind` `gate`: the phone drops that session's delivered `needs-input` notification whose `request` is that id, and the Mac marks that entry handled and removes its delivered notification; a session's other card, a turn-end question and a connect request keep theirs. The `agents.changed` resolve has always named its request; what older builds lack is the `request` on the card's notification and on the resolve push: a resolve push without `request` (an older `remote` plugin) withdraws every `needs-input` notification of the session, as before, while a card notification posted without `request` (an older agents plugin) is not withdrawn by a resolve that names one (backlog #913). One card's answer that closes another (Stop) resolves each by its own id.
- **Pause.** A landing whose review round is open when its gate is paused (no verdict yet, `landReviews[].last` empty, or its Land anyway / Stop card out) stays gated: the paused gate still matches it for that lander, target and source, so `git.land` answers `review pending` until the verdict, an approved verdict lands it with its summary, and after `changes` the lander gets the findings once and its next `git.land` isn't caught. Any other landing lands without a review while the gate is paused. A target that moved after a paused gate approved makes a new landing: it isn't caught, lands without another review, and without the summary, which belongs to the exact change reviewed.
- After a landing `git.land` calls `agents.land-done`, which records the landed commit on its verdict, stops the gate run and closes every node session of the landing's runs (cancelling a turn still running).

#### Done gates

A gate with `point: turn` (named Done) holds an agent turn that ends done until its graph decides. It matches by session kind and, optionally, by what the session wrote. When its rounds are used up, or its review ends without a verdict twice, the hold asks you on a card (**Card** below).

- `sessions` is any of `top` (a session without `parentSession`, including ones a cron job, a trigger or an agent started at the top level), `child` (one with a parent) and `afk`: a session matches when its kind is listed and, if it is in AFK mode (its own or inherited from an ancestor, as everywhere else), `afk` is listed too, so an AFK child needs `child` and `afk`. `top` and `child` say which sessions; `afk` only lets the AFK ones of those kinds through, so `["afk"]` alone or none is refused. Default `["top", "afk"]`. A Done gate never matches a gate node (`gateFor`), a graph run node (`run`), a service or night session, or an aside.
- `wrote` is a list of file globs (`/`-separated, `*`, `?` and `[…]` within one folder, `**` for any number of folders, never absolute); empty matches any turn. A glob is tried against each written file's path relative to the session's repo root and relative to the file's own repo root (a file outside any repo: its absolute path without the leading `/`), after symlinks are resolved, so `**/plans/*.md` matches a plan written to `docs/superpowers/plans/` when `docs/superpowers` links into another repo.
- What the session wrote: every completed edit tool call (Edit, Write, MultiEdit, NotebookEdit, delete and move; a subagent's too) since the session's `gateSince`, plus every path that differs between that commit and the worktree now (tracked and untracked, ignored files left out). `gateSince` marks the session's last reviewed done. A done released as approved (by the reviewer, by Accept as done, or on a stored approved verdict for its key) sets it to a commit of the tree that was reviewed (`refs/clubmate/cp/<session>/reviewed`) and `gateAt` to when that tree was taken (`held.treeAt`; an Accept that waits for a running cycle keeps it), so edits after it count, also in a later cycle of the same turn (a background task's wake, a reminder). With no reviewed done yet it is the checkpoint of the first turn after the last reset, set when a turn starts and it is empty, and edits count from that turn. It is kept across turns that end with an error or a cancel, and across a release by a paused, deleted or changed gate while another active Done gate still holds the session's kind. Where no active Done gate holds the session's kind a finished done (or such a release) clears it, so a gate added later reviews from the session's next turn. A cancelled turn keeps it everywhere, and so does a done in a folder that is no longer a git repo (no `.git` up its path) while any active Done gate exists. When it is empty at a hold, the base is the session's latest turn checkpoint, else its HEAD, so the review never diffs from nothing; a turn's start sets it, so this only happens in a later cycle of a turn that finished where no Done gate held it, and that cycle's review includes the turn's own edits.
- Several Done gates may match one turn: one whose `wrote` matches decides before one without; between equals the older gate. A repo has at most one Done gate per `(sessions, wrote)` (compared as sets); a duplicate fails naming the existing gate's id.
- Names: "Done · plans and specs" for `["**/plans/*.md", "**/specs/*.md"]`, "Done · any file" for `["**"]`, "Done · any turn" without `wrote`, else "Done · ‹first glob›".
- Only land gates make a repo gated for the git hook below; a repo with only Done or Tool call gates gets no hook environment.
- **Held.** A turn that would end done (also the done that comes later, when a background task or a pending permission or question outlived the turn) is checked once, before its status is set. It is held when a Done gate matches, unless the turn was cancelled, errored, abandoned or closed; the session still has children `running` or `waiting` (the turn that ends after they finish is checked); nothing changed since `gateSince` (no written paths and the worktree's tree equals the checkpoint's); or an `approved` verdict is stored for the turn's key. A turn that ends with a question is held like any other. When the worktree can't be snapshotted, a Done gate for the session's kind holds the turn even if its `wrote` doesn't match what the edit tools reported, so a failed snapshot never lets a turn through. Neither does a failed read: when the gates can't be read (the last good read then decides which gates there are; it is kept on disk, so it still decides after a restart, and a turn already held this way keeps its gate when no read is known at all) or the session's repo can't be listed (`git worktree list` fails in a folder that has `.git`) and an active Done gate of the session's repo holds its kind, a turn not yet held stays held under the first such gate with `held.unread`, `held.reason` (git's first line, `fatal: ` dropped, or the storage error) and `held.readRepo` (the repo's name, its main checkout's folder also for a linked worktree, the session's own repo folder when `.git` can't be followed; empty when the gates were unread). The session's repo is found from the worktree's `.git` (a linked worktree's `commondir` leads to its main checkout, checked against git's link back to the worktree; a submodule's is its own checkout, the `core.worktree` of its git dir, which must lead back to the folder); a session in a repo with no such gate ends done, and one whose `.git` can't be followed so (unreadable, no link back) is held under the first such gate of any repo; a folder with no `.git` up its path ends done. A turn already held this way keeps its gate while that gate is active and holds its kind. With no gates read since the plugin's state was made (no `last-gates.json`), a turn not yet held is still held when the plugin knows a gate without storage: the gate the session carries (`heldGate`), the gate of one of its call holds, or the first rule in the `rules.json` of its repo's tool hook folder; otherwise it ends done. Such a hold's card names no graph or gate, and once the gates read again it moves to the repo's Done gate or is released. No run starts. The plugin checks the turn again as a turn end would after 5 s, 30 s and 2 min (`CLUBMATE_AGENTS_RECHECK=<duration>` sets all three, for tests; a restart re-arms the next one from the stored hold), each failure adding its time to `held.tried`: as soon as a check reads, the turn is held by its gate as round 1, released, or done as usual. Meanwhile the row reads `held · checking again` (accent, also the header badge), the phone line `Held · checking the gates again…`, the gate page and held block `checking again`; it doesn't need you. After the third failed check the card opens (**Card** below). A turn already held keeps its hold; a folder that is no longer a git repo ends done. The key is a hash of the gate's id, the worktree's HEAD, its tree and the content of the matched files (every written file for a gate without `wrote`).
- A held session keeps status `running` and gets `held: {gate, key, round, since, …}` in `agents.sessions`: no `agents.turn` event, no notification, no parent wake, `agents.wait` doesn't return, a landing and graph edges see it running, and it never needs you until its card opens. On release it becomes `done`, `held` is cleared, `gateSince` moves as above, and everything a done turn does happens then: the `agents.turn` event, the notification, the parent's wake, the review mark and the child cleanup. A release is logged in the gate's history: `approved`, or `released` by `gate-paused`, `gate-deleted` or `gate-changed` (the gate page reads "released: gate paused", "released: gate deleted", "released: gate changed"); a Stop is logged `stopped` and reads "stopped by you".
- **Review.** A hold starts a run of the gate's graph at once (`held.run`, `held.reviewer` the verdict node's session), with the same rules as a landing's run (pinned graph, nodes started by the plugin, read-only, no rows in the tree): node sessions are titled "Done review · ‹session title›", carry `gateFor {gate, point: "turn", key, lander: <held session>, run, verdict}`, run in the held session's worktree, and steps run in a gate worktree at the key's tree (`held.treeKey`, a commit of the tree on `gateSince`), reset to the next round's tree while the run is reused and its steps are idle, made fresh for a new run, and dropped when the run is replaced or the hold ends. The input names the repo, the gate, the session's title, `git diff <gateSince> <tree>` in its worktree, the matched files' paths and contents (at most 64 KiB in all), a file holding a NUL byte or invalid UTF-8 listed by path only as "(binary, not shown)" and not counted toward the 64 KiB and the session's last reply; verdict nodes are told to answer with `agents.land-verdict {key, outcome, findings?, deferred?, summary?}` (`tree` stays an alias of `key` for a landing; a Done verdict node gets "[Clubmate Done reviewer]" as its system text, and its nudge says "Your Done review ended without a verdict"). The verdict is stored per gate and key under `turn-verdict/<gate>/<key>`, so a hold moved to another gate never reuses the old gate's verdict, deleted with the gate, and logged in the gate's history with its round.
- **At the verdict** the key is recomputed: when the worktree moved since the round was asked (a follow-up cycle, your own edit), the verdict is refused ("the session's files changed since this review began") and the same run is prompted again on the new key; the third move in one round stops asking and sets `held.request`. A cycle that ends while the session is held recomputes the key the same way. `approved` releases the held turn (as below), stops the run and closes its nodes. Every release first closes the run's open round, so the `agents.turn` event of the release says `finished: true` when nothing else runs under the session. A Done review node has no row of its own in the tree (`hidden`) while it belongs to its session's current hold (its run is the hold's, or its key is, which covers the moment a new run starts before the hold records it), unless it asks you or fails. `changes` keeps it held and prompts the session with "[Clubmate] From the gate · ‹repo› · ‹gate name› · round N of M · changes", the findings, the deferred ones after "Not blocking:", a line that asks to fix those too (only when there are some), a line that asks to ask the user only about an item it can't fix without their decision and to file in the backlog only a separate task (the transcript block leaves both lines out), and that its next finished turn goes back to the gate (M is the gate's `rounds` plus `held.extra`); this prompt doesn't drop the hold. When that turn ends with a new key it is round N+1 on the same run (the verdict node gets the delta `git diff <last reviewed tree> <tree>`); with the same key it counts as a round with the stored findings and no run, so the session is prompted again with them. After `changes` in round M the session is not prompted and `held.request` is set (the card comes later). A Stop (⎋) during that turn ends it `idle` and logs `stopped`, as on a held turn; a Clubmate restart during it sends the gate's prompt again.
- **No verdict.** A run of a held turn that stops without a verdict (budget, a failed or interrupted step, a restart that loses it, the user's `agents.graph-stop`, the verdict node's second turn without one) is started again once for the same round; a second end sets `held.request`. It is never a pass. A run that stops after its verdict was stored applies that verdict: `approved` releases the turn, `changes` prompts the session with the findings. A gate whose graph changed since it was pinned keeps the turn held with `held.wait` ("the gate's graph changed: waiting for the user to trust it"); trusting it, or picking another graph in Edit, starts the run. When a held session's turn ends and its worktree can't be read (the gates, its repo or a snapshot), the hold keeps its key: a fix turn then counts as a round with the stored findings.
- **Pause.** Pausing a gate leaves a turn it holds held: its review finishes and decides as on an active gate (approved makes it done; `changes` prompts the agent with the findings; at the cap, after no verdict twice or after the third moved key the card opens; a gate waiting for trust keeps it held until it is trusted or the session is stopped). While the hold's round has no verdict, or its card is open, the paused gate still holds that session, so a follow-up cycle that ends mid-review takes a new key and stays held. Once the round's verdict is `changes`, the session's next finished turn is new: the paused gate doesn't catch it and it is released as done, logged `released` by `gate-paused` (editing the paused gate's match during that fix turn logs it `released` by `gate-changed` instead, even when the gate still matches).
- **Gate changes.** Deleting or changing a gate so it no longer matches a held session moves the hold to the most specific other active Done gate that matches (round 1, a new run under that gate; when the worktree can't be snapshotted it keeps the old key until the verdict recomputes it), else releases it, logged `released` by `gate-deleted` or `gate-changed`. A `held.unread` hold moves the same way to the first other such gate of the session's repo, keeping its checks and its open card (its `held.graph`, `held.name` and `held.repo` follow the new gate, `held.readRepo` and `held.reason` the repo's read then), else is released. A hold moved while its session is busy (a fix turn or a follow-up cycle runs, a background task, a pending permission or question) starts the new gate's review only when that turn ends, on the tree it leaves. A release asked for while the session is mid follow-up cycle, has a background task or waits on a permission or question is kept in `held.release` and happens when that settles. When a held session's turn ends again (its fix turn, a follow-up cycle) and the turn would no longer be held, it is released as done and logged `released` by `children-running`, `unchanged` or `unmatched` (or `gate-changed` when no active Done gate holds its kind); one that now matches a stored approved verdict is released without another entry.
- A held session survives a restart as it is: still `running` and held, no cut-off prompt, and its parent is woken when it is released. Its review resumes once the graph engine has started, and also when the engine fails to start (the run can't start, so the hold shows why it waits or opens its card instead of staying held with nothing to answer). A prompt to a held session (yours, its parent's, a child's wake, a trigger's; not the gate's own `changes` prompt, which keeps that mark while it waits in the queue across a restart) starts a turn at once, drops the hold, stops its run and closes its nodes; `gateSince` stays, so the files written before are still checked when that turn ends. When the gate is paused and still held the turn (no verdict yet, or its card out), the session keeps it as `heldGate`: the paused gate counts for that session's next finished turn, whatever started it, so the turn is held again (round 1) instead of ending done unreviewed; the same goes for a follow-up cycle released because children still run, held again after the children's wake. Stop, a hold and any other done clear it, and so does pausing the gate while no hold of it is live (a prompt dropped it and the turn runs on: pause means no new holds). An error that ends a held turn keeps the gate the same way, so the next prompt's turn is held again. Stop (⎋) drops the hold without a release: the session goes `idle`, its parent is not woken, and the gate's history logs `stopped`. Only the user can: an agent's `agents.cancel` on a held session fails. Closing the session drops it too, and so does any status other than `running` or `waiting` (an error when its agent process exits mid follow-up): such a session is never held again after a restart, only the next turn it takes is held by the paused gate as above.
- **Card.** Its opening posts a Needs you notification and its going away withdraws it, as for a landing's card ([Gates](#gates)). `held.request` (`lg_…`) is set after the gate's `rounds` (+ `held.extra`) verdicts of `changes`, after a second run end without a verdict, after the third key move in one round, and after the third failed re-check of a `held.unread` turn. That card reads "Couldn't check this turn against its gates", question "Accept as done without a review?", caption "held since ‹HH:MM› · tried ‹HH:MM›, ‹HH:MM›, ‹HH:MM›" and rule "When the turn ended, Clubmate couldn't read ‹the gates | ‹repo›'s repo›: ‹reason›. It tried N times. Check again tries now. Stop leaves the session idle and the turn not done."; its ⌥2 is **Check again** (`more`): no review ran, so it counts against no rounds; it checks at once, and a failure shows the card again with one more time. Accept as done stores no verdict and leaves `gateSince` where it was; with it `held` carries `graph` (the graph's title), `name` (the gate's name), `repo` and `asked`. The session stays `running` but its row reads `asking` (amber), it needs you and counts once in Needs you, `pending` carries the request id, `pendingSummary` is `{kind: "land", text: <title>, onMac: true}`, and the Mac and the phone dock it as the same `request.land` card as a landing past its rounds: title "‹graph› still asks for changes after N rounds" (after `changes`), "The files kept changing during ‹graph›" (after the third key move) or "‹graph› ended without a verdict", caption "‹repo› · ‹gate name› · held since ‹HH:MM›", the last findings and the summary in `detail`, and `question`: "Accept the plan as done?" for plans and specs, "Accept the turn as done?" for any turn (Nothing required), "Accept the changes as done?" for any file and Files like… (a land gate's card asks "Land ‹branch› without another review?" under "‹graph› still asks for changes after N rounds"). Its options are **Accept as done** (`merge`) ⌥1, **One more round** (`more`) ⌥2 and **Stop** (`leave`) ⌥3; the phone draws a `request.land` card whose options include `more` as a choice card (title, detail, One more round and the `merge` option side by side, the `reject_once` option below in the danger ink), not as its Review and merge dock, so a held turn and a land gate past its rounds are answered there with all three. The user answers with `agents.gate-answer {request, action: accept | more | stop}` (the card's buttons send `agents.land-answer`/`landAnswer` with `merge`, `more`, `leave`, mapped the same way; an agent gets an error, an answered request `this landing request was already answered`). Accept releases the turn as done (logged `approved` by `user`) and stores an `approved` verdict by `user` under `turn-verdict/<gate>/<key>`, so a later turn that ends on the same key is not held again. One more round after `changes` raises `held.extra` by one and sends the agent the findings again ("round N of rounds+extra"), so its next finished turn is the next round; after no verdict it runs the review again on the same key (one retry again, no extra round). Stop is ⎋: the hold is dropped without a release, the session goes `idle` and the history logs `stopped`.
- **Docs wait too.** While a session's turn runs (status `running` or `waiting`) in a repo whose active Done gate holds its kind (or in a git worktree git can't read while an active Done gate of its repo holds its kind; a folder that isn't a git repo never waits), and it isn't asking you a question, its `docs_open` and `docs_present` calls show nothing: the docs plugin asks `agents.doc-gate {session, for?, path, mode: open|present, title?}` (docs plugin only, `{queued}`; `session` is the calling session, whose turn decides, `for` the session the call named), the call answers `{path, note: "queued: shown when this turn is reviewed"}`, and the session's record keeps the doc in `gatedDocs` with the turn that made the call (it survives a restart). It can't be known mid-turn whether the turn will match, so every such call queues. When the session's status leaves `running`/`waiting` without a hold (done, also after a release; error, unless a usage-limit wait (`limitWait`) will resume the turn, whose end decides then; a cancel; a stop; a turn a restart cut off, when Clubmate starts) the plugin runs each queued call again in order as the session made it, with `gated: false` so it isn't queued twice; a closed session's queue is dropped. So does a question to you (an MCP form or AskUserQuestion, not a permission request): a doc the agent opened right before asking you about it shows while you're asked. A question its parent session answers (a child's form question while the parent is open) or a question while the session is held is not one: the docs wait for the turn's end or the hold's release. A replayed doc's card goes on the turn that queued it, even when the session's next turn has already started. A call leaves the queue once the docs plugin answers it, also with an error, and when it times out or the core can't tell whether it ran (it may have shown, so it isn't run twice) or the workspace is gone; when the docs plugin isn't there (not started yet, stopped, disabled, or its command not loaded) the doc stays queued and is run again on `docs.ready`, at the session's next such status change or at the next start; a plain not-found (`-32004` with no reason) is tried at most 5 times, then the doc leaves the queue. A prompt that drops a hold keeps the queue for the next turn's end. So a held turn's presented cards are drawn only once it is released.

#### Tool call gates

A gate with `point: tool` (named Tool call) matches an agent's tool call by the tool's name and its input. This is the record, the match, the hook that turns a matching call into a permission request, the hold that takes that request before anyone is asked, and the review that decides it.

- `tool` is a tool name or a glob (`path.Match`: `Bash`, `Write`, `mcp__*`), default `Bash`. The input a gate reads (and the key of a held call) is, for `Bash`, the call's `command` field, read by its exact name (`Command` is another field), with its ends trimmed (a trailing space escaped by a backslash stays; `description`, `timeout` and `run_in_background` are left out), and for any other tool its whole input as compact JSON with sorted keys (`{"content":"…","file_path":"/r/a.md"}`); an `Agent` call's input is that JSON with its subagent's prompt in it, so a `contains` or `regex` match on the prompt matches the `Agent` call itself. For a tool other than Bash `contains` and `regex` run on the JSON text, so a quote or a newline in `text` is written as JSON escapes it (`\"`, `\n`).
- `how` says how `text` matches, default `starts` for Bash and `contains` for any other tool (`agents.gate-add` sets it from any caller). A tool other than Bash takes only `contains` and `regex`; changing a gate's `tool` without `how` keeps its `how` when it still applies, else sets the new tool's default.
  - `command` (Bash only; the presets git push and gh pr create): `text` is a binary and its subcommand words, at least two (`git push`, `gh pr create`). It is tried on every command of the line as the shell reads it (split at `;`, `&`, `|`, a newline, `(` and `)`, the commands inside `$(…)`, backticks and `<(…)` included, also those in the body of a heredoc whose tag isn't quoted (`<<EOF` … `$(git push)`, not `<<'EOF'` or `\$(…)`), quotes removed, a backslash-newline joined, redirects and their targets left out (`>/dev/null`, `2>&1`, `&>log`, `git >log push`), a `#` comment left out), after dropping leading `VAR=x` words, the shell words `if`, `then`, `else`, `elif`, `while`, `until`, `do`, `{` and `!`, `function f`, `coproc`, and the wrappers `sudo`, `timeout`, `env`, `time`, `nohup`, `command` and `exec` with their options (`sudo -u bob`, `sudo -h host`, `timeout -s KILL 30`, `env -i -u X A=1`, `env - …`; `env -S 'git push'` reads its string as the command, split as the shell would split it, so `env -S 'bash -c "git push"'` is `bash -c`; at most 8 `-S` strings per command are split; a command with more is held by every `command` gate as one Clubmate can't parse, whatever it mentions), each by its base name (`/usr/bin/env`); the binary is compared by its base name (`/usr/bin/git`), then options before each subcommand word are skipped, with their value for git's `-C`, `-c`, `--git-dir`, `--work-tree`, `--namespace`, `--config-env` and `--attr-source` and gh's `-R` and `--repo`. So `cd x && sudo git -C y push`, `git -C "$dir" push`, `2>&1 git push`, `if git push; then …`, `echo "$(git push)"`, `gh -R o/r pr create` and `for r in a b; do git push $r; done` are a git push or a gh pr create; `git stash push`, `echo git push` and `git pushx` aren't. Quoted text and heredoc bodies are data: Claude Code's commit form `git commit -m "$(cat <<'EOF' … EOF )"` is no git push whatever its message says, nor is `git commit -m "fix; git push later"`.
  - `starts` (Bash only; Custom…): `text` is one command (no `;`, `&`, `|`, `(`, `)`, `$(…)`, backtick or heredoc; else the gate is refused), read like a command of the line. A command of the line, read as `command` reads it (so quoted text, heredoc bodies and redirects are never matched), after dropping leading `VAR=x` words, the shell words above and the bare words `time`, `sudo`, `command`, `exec`, `nohup` and `env` (as written, not by base name), has `text`'s words as its first words: `time make deploy`, `(cd x; make deploy)`, `make "deploy" prod` and `if x; then make deploy; fi` start with `make deploy`; `sudo -u bob make deploy`, `/usr/bin/make deploy`, `echo "x; make deploy"` and a heredoc body holding `make deploy` don't. `starts` has no wrapped-command rule: `sh -c "make deploy"` doesn't start with `make deploy`.
