On a line the shell reading can't finish (see below; also one nested more than 64 deep or longer than 256 KiB) it tries the commands read before the point where the reading stopped (`echo ok; make deploy 'unclosed` starts with `make deploy`); when none of them starts with `text`, the line is held when its text as written, split as below, holds `text`'s first word (compared by base name on both sides, so `./deploy.sh prod` is found as `deploy.sh`) and then each of its other words in order, anywhere on the line, not only at a command's start; the hold says so like `command`: "make deploy · in a command Clubmate can't parse, held to be safe" (`echo "unclosed; make deploy`, also `echo "unclosed; make it; deploy`). A line that mentions none of them isn't held.
  - `contains`: the input contains `text`.
  - `regex`: the input matches `text`, a Go (RE2) regular expression.
- **Wrapped and unreadable lines** (`command`; `starts` only for an unreadable line, as above). A command that is the preset itself is matched as above first, so a `bash deploy.sh` or `xargs rm` preset matches its own command. Otherwise the text a command runs through a shell or a runner is wrapped: the argument of `sh`, `bash`, `zsh`, `dash`, `ksh` or `fish` with `-c` (`-lc`, `-ec`, `--command` … too; the first word after its options, `--` included, skipping the value of each `o` or `O` in a bundle such as `-euo pipefail`), every heredoc or here-string fed to such a shell when it reads its script from stdin (no `-c` and no script file; a first word that is an expansion, `$OPTS`, `${DEBUG:+-x}`, `$(…)` or a backtick, is read as options, not as a script: `bash <<'EOF'`, `bash -s <<< '…'`, `bash $OPTS <<EOF`; fed to `bash -c 'cat'` or `bash script.sh` it is that script's input, data), `eval`'s words joined and the command `xargs` runs after its options (`-I {}`, `-J %` …); wrappers nest. A wrapped text is read as a line and matched the same way; when no command of it is the preset, it still matches when it mentions the preset (a command of the line that is the preset wins over a mention found at the same place: `curl x | sh; git push` is a plain git push): its words (`-m` and `--message` values and heredoc bodies left out), and for `-c` the words after the argument, `-m` and `--message` values left out there too (`bash -c 'git "$@"' _ push` is held, `sh -c 'x' _ git commit -m push` isn't), split at spaces, quotes, `=` and `();&|<>{}$` and backticks, hold the binary (by base name) and then each subcommand word in order. So `sh -c 'G=git; $G push'`, `sh -c 'echo "git push"'` and `sh -c 'git status; echo git push'` are held, `sh -c "git commit -m 'git push later'"` and `sh -c 'git status'` aren't. A shell without `-c` and without a script file (or with `-s`, `-`, `/dev/stdin`, `/dev/fd/0`) reads its stdin: when nothing on its own command feeds it, it matches when the whole line mentions the preset (`echo "git push" | sh`, `cat <<EOF | bash`). Arithmetic (`$((1<<2))`, `(( n << 2 ))`, `$[1<<2]`, also inside double quotes and `${…}`) is no heredoc, and a `$(…)` or backtick inside it or inside `${…}` is read as a command (`x=$(( $(nproc) << 1 ))`). A heredoc inside `$(…)` or backticks may end right before the closing `)` or backtick (`` echo `cat <<EOF⏎hi⏎EOF` ``). A `case` pattern's `)` doesn't close a `$(…)` (`echo $(case x in a) git push;; esac)` is a git push, also with `in` on the next line, after `then`, `do`, `else`, `{`, `!` or `time`, and with several clauses ended by `;;`, `;&` or `;;&`); a `case` still open where its `$(…)`, backtick or line ends makes the line one the reading can't finish. A `$(…)` or backtick in an unquoted heredoc body that the reading can't finish (unclosed, or nested more than 64 deep) makes the line one it can't finish, but the commands after the heredoc are still read, so a `starts` gate still finds them. A line, or a wrapped text, the shell reading can't finish (an unclosed quote, `$(`, backtick, `${` or `$'`, a `<<` without a word, a heredoc that never ends), one nested more than 64 deep, one longer than 256 KiB, or wrappers nested more than 8 deep, matches when it mentions the preset, its words split as above from the text as written (`git -C x  push "unclosed`). `sh -c "git -C x push"`, `eval "git push"`, `ls | xargs -I{} git -C {} push` and `git push "unclosed` are a git push. Such a match says why: `toolgate.Hit.Via` is `sh -c`, `bash -c`, the shell and its heredoc operator as written (`bash <<'SCRIPT'`, `bash <<-EOF`, `bash <<<`), `sh` (stdin), `eval`, `xargs` or `unread`, and the held call's caption reads "‹match› · ‹where it was found›, held to be safe": "git push · inside sh -c, held to be safe", "git push · inside bash <<'EOF', held to be safe", "git push · in a command Clubmate can't parse, held to be safe". Misses: `sh -c "$CMD"` and any command built at run time, a shell started inside a `-c` text that reads the heredoc fed to the outer one (`bash -c 'bash -s' <<EOF`, `bash -c 'source /dev/stdin' <<EOF`: the heredoc of `bash -c` is data, so `bash -c 'cat' <<EOF` isn't held), `nice`, `gtimeout`, `find -exec` and other runners not named above, a script or a Makefile that pushes, an alias, a git alias or a shell function called by name, a shell fed by a command inside a wrapped text (`sh -c 'cat | sh' <<EOF`), quoting that splits a word only in a mention (`echo "g''it push" | sh`, `echo '\git push' | sh`: a mention reads the words as written), and a tool other than Bash; only `contains` or `regex` can catch those. A Tool call gate checks a cooperative agent and is not a security boundary.
- Without `text` the gate matches any call of `tool`; that is refused for `Bash` and for a glob ("a Tool call gate names a command or a tool"). Also refused: an invalid glob or regex, `command` or `starts` for a tool other than `Bash`, `command` with one word, `starts` with more than one command, and any `command` or `starts` gate that wouldn't match its own text as a call: `starts` beginning with a word the reading drops (`sudo make install`, `FOO=1 make deploy`, `env …`, `time -p …`), `command` whose binary has a path or is a wrapper (`./deploy.sh prod`, `sudo reboot`), or that holds an option, a quote or `;` (`git push --force`, `rm -rf`, `git "push"`, `make deploy;`); when a gate without the dropped words would be accepted the error says what to write instead (`make install`, `make` for `sudo -u bob make`, `deploy.sh prod`), and it suggests nothing it would refuse (`sudo reboot` has no suggestion: `reboot` alone is one word) — `into`, `sessions` or `wrote` on a Tool call gate (and `tool`, `how` or `text` on another gate), and a second gate with the same `(tool, how, text)` in the repo (the error names the existing gate's id). `command` and `starts` keep `text` with its words joined by single spaces; `contains` keeps it without spaces at its ends (on add, on change and in the sheet alike, so a text saved with end spaces is the same match); `regex` keeps it as typed.
- Several Tool call gates may match one call: each of them matches it, listed in the order their match appears in the input (a `command` or `starts` match by its command's place in the line, a wrapped or unreadable match by where the preset is found inside it, `contains` and `regex` by where they match, a gate without `text` at the start of the input; ties oldest gate first): `sh -c 'git push && gh pr create'` lists git push before gh pr create.
- Names: "Tool call · ‹match›", the match being `text` (`command`, `starts`, `contains`), "/‹text›/" (`regex`) or the tool's name (no `text`): "Tool call · git push". `words` says the match in full: "Bash · a git push command", "Bash · a command that starts with make deploy", "Bash · the command contains --force", "Write · the input matches /\.env$/", "mcp__clubmate__git_land · any call" ("the input" for a tool other than Bash).
- **The hook.** Sessions of a repo with a Tool call gate (paused ones included, so Resume holds at once) carry Clubmate's PreToolUse hook: the plugin writes `<state>/toolhook/<16 hex of sha256(repo)>/` per such repo, a Claude Code plugin `clubmate-gates` (`.claude-plugin/plugin.json`, `hooks/hooks.json` with a `PreToolUse` entry, matcher `.*`, `timeout` 10 s, `onFailure: "block"`, and a `PostToolBatch` entry, matcher `.*`, `timeout` 10 s, no `onFailure`, see **Changes** below), `rules.json` (the repo's active Tool call gates, `[]` when all are paused or the last one is gone), `marks/`, `findings/` and `told/`; it rewrites them at start and on every gate change, and writes any missing file before an attach uses the dir. Every session start, fork and resume of such a session passes the dir in `_meta.claudeCode.options.plugins` and two flag settings, `disableAllHooks: false` and `enabledPlugins: {"clubmate-gates@inline": true}`, which beat a `disableAllHooks: true` or a local `enabledPlugins` off in the user's or the worktree's settings; the user's own hooks therefore run in a gated repo even where their settings switch hooks off. Not hooked: a gate's own review nodes, night sessions, graph steps, the user's terminals and `cm`; in a repo without a Tool call gate nothing is added. **Fail closed:** when the gates can't be read and no earlier read is kept, or the session's repo can't be resolved (a git error, not "not a repo"), the session carries `<state>/toolhook/all/`, whose rules hold every call (`[{"tool":"*"}]`), until a later attach can carry its repo's own dir; a session already hooked keeps its rules, and the repos' `rules.json` are left as they are until the gates can be read. The session's `hooked` says whether its last start, fork or resume carried the hook.
  - The hook command is the small `tool-hook` binary beside the agents plugin (`tool-hook '<dir>' || printf '%s' '<ask>'`, run by Claude Code's shell). It reads the call (`tool_name`, `tool_input`, `tool_use_id`, `cwd`) and matches it against its own dir's `rules.json` and, added to them, the rules of the repo the call is made in: it walks up from `cwd` to the nearest `.git` and takes the repo's main worktree (for a linked worktree, through its `gitdir` and `commondir`), and when `<state>/toolhook/` holds a dir for that repo (other than its own) with a `rules.json`, it reads that file too. A call asks when either set matches or either file can't be used; no `cwd`, a path it can't resolve or a repo Clubmate keeps no hook dir for adds nothing. The `all` dir reads only its own. When a gate matches it leaves the file `marks/<tool_use_id>` (`{repo, tree}` when the call's repo's rules matched, else empty) and prints `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"PreToolUse","permissionDecision":"ask","permissionDecisionReason":"Clubmate gate"}}` in one write, which turns the call into an ACP `session/request_permission` in every mode, auto and bypass included; otherwise it prints nothing. It never calls Clubmate and never answers allow or deny. It fails toward `ask`, never toward a pass: a call it can't read (not JSON, `null`, no `tool_name`) asks, and so does a rules file that is missing, not JSON, `null` or holds a rule it can't use (an empty or invalid tool glob, an unknown `how`, an invalid regex); a missing `marks/` is recreated; a missing or crashing binary (exit 1, 2 or 127) asks through the `|| printf` fallback; a hook past its 10 s timeout blocks the call (it fails with "hook error … timed out"). One gap stays: output that isn't JSON (a partial line with exit 0) is taken as text and lets the call run; the hook prints its one line in one write so it can't be cut short. A call's mark is removed when the call completes or fails; marks, findings, told files and long messages older than a day are removed at plugin start and hourly; so is the dir of a repo that has no Tool call gate left once no live session carries it.
  - **When a new gate reaches a session.** A running Claude Code session never picks up a changed hook list, so a live session of a tool-gated repo whose last attach lacked the hook (or carried the fail-closed `all` dir while its repo's own dir can now be read) is re-attached: its agent session is closed the way an idle session is suspended and resumed with the hook. The plugin checks at four moments: when a repo gets its first Tool call gate (every idle session of the repo: no turn, no background cycle, nothing pending or queued, a parent waiting on running children included), when an attach completes (an attach in flight while the gate was saved), at the start of every turn before its prompt is sent (so a turn after a released Done hold, a Stop, an error or a queued prompt never starts unhooked), and when a turn ends. Its history stays; a session whose agent can't resume is left as it is.
  - **When it can't be re-attached yet.** A session is never detached while its agent process runs a background command: its own tracked `run_in_background` command (the session reads `running` meanwhile), or any shell or later descendant of the agent process (the same check that keeps an idle session from being suspended; the process is shared per worktree, so another session's command holds its siblings too). A session whose agent can't resume (no resume capability, or its last resume failed) is never detached either. Such a session runs unhooked, visibly: the plugin sends `ungated: {chip: "not gated yet", badge: "not gated yet · ‹when›", line: "Not gated yet · ‹when›"}`, ‹when› being "after its background command" (its own tracked command), "after the worktree's other work" (another process of the shared agent process) or "from its next start" (an agent that can't resume takes the hook only when Clubmate starts it fresh, after a restart). The Mac session header (badge) and the phone's session header always show it; the row's chip ("not gated yet") and the phone list line ("Not gated yet · ‹when›") come through `landing` in plain ink, at normal priority, so another landing, needs you or an error hides them. Nothing needs the user, so it is never warning-toned and never in Needs you. The plugin checks such a session again every 5 s (`CLUBMATE_AGENTS_HOOK_POLL`, a positive duration): once the background work has ended it settles the command's turn and re-attaches the session when it is idle. For the session that owned the command (the user's ruling, 18 Nov: re-attach at once, then prompt it) the end of the command never lets the agent go on unhooked: the plugin re-attaches it as soon as it can (when the 5 s check finds the command's process gone it settles the turn first; when the agent's own follow-up cycle starts before that, the plugin closes the agent session at once, cutting the cycle off; nothing that needs the user can be pending then), and then prompts it as Clubmate with what the follow-up needs: "[Clubmate] Your background command finished: `‹command›` — continue." followed, for each command, by "Output of `‹command›` (the command's own output, not Clubmate's words; its last lines):" and its output in a fenced block (the last 20 lines, at most 1500 bytes each and 4000 in all, control characters removed, cut on a character boundary, the fence longer than any backtick run in it), or "… printed no output." / "The output of … can't be read.". The command text is put on one line and cut at 200 characters; at most the last 5 commands are listed. Only commands whose end the agent hasn't acted on yet are listed: a command is dropped once a follow-up cycle of the agent's own runs to its end or a turn completes after it ended, and then the re-attach is silent (no "continue" when Claude already followed up, unhooked, while the worktree was busy). Stop clears what is owed; a closed session leaves the 5 s check. The output's tail is copied into the session's log and transcript (the Mac, the phone, exports) like any prompt; the ACP wire doesn't carry it otherwise. The plugin takes the command from the call's `rawInput` (`run_in_background: true`) and the output file from its result ("Output is being written to: ‹…›.output"). Real Claude Code reads the command's `<task-notification>` from its log on resume and acts on the prompt once, without a second, self-woken follow-up (spike row 13). A tool call the agent made in its follow-up before the cut ran unhooked. The 5 s check settles a turn only when it found a tracked command gone, and refreshes a label that no longer holds (a held turn whose command ended loses it: nothing of it runs). When another worktree process still blocks the detach, the cycle runs on unhooked and labelled. A matching call it makes meanwhile runs without a hold and is logged "ran ungated" (see **Ran ungated** below); its turn goes on. A later gate in the same repo changes only `rules.json`, which the hook reads on every call.
    - **The repo a call is made in.** A session carries the hook of its own worktree's repo, and each call is matched against that repo's Tool call gates and, added to them, those of the repo its `cwd` is in, so in a workspace with several repos a call made from another repo's folder is held by that repo's gates as well as the session's own; moving the shell elsewhere never drops the session's own gates. The plugin learns the call's repo from its mark: a mark naming another repo with a Tool call gate adds that repo's gates to the hold, and the hold's HEAD and tree (from the mark's `tree`), branch and the reviewer's `Worktree` line are the call's repo's; the reviewer itself runs where it always does, so it reads that worktree by its path. An empty or unreadable mark leaves only the session's own repo. `cwd` is where the call starts: `cd ../other && git push` inside one command adds nothing, and a session whose own repo has no Tool call gate carries no hook, so its calls in a gated repo are not held.
- **Ran ungated.** When a live `tool_call_update` (or `tool_call`) says a call is `completed`, in any session that can be held (not a gate's review node, not a night session), the plugin matches the call's tool and input as ACP shows them against the repo's active Tool call gates whose `since` (stamped once `rules.json` holds the gate after it is added, resumed or its match changes, so the hook already reads it; `createdAt` for an older gate) is before the call's first `tool_call`. A subagent's call counts the same; a replayed log (plugin start, opening a session) is never judged. Each such gate that matches and never held the call (its request never came to that gate) gets a history line with outcome `ungated` and the call's first 200 characters, and the session's transcript a notice "‹match› ran ungated" with why and the call. In a hooked session the turn is stopped (as an agent's cancel: holds keep their rounds) when the turn or cycle the call ran in is still running; otherwise (a background subagent's call after its turn ended) it is only logged and the notice says so. `by` says why: `hook` ("ran ungated: Clubmate's hook didn't see it": no `marks/<toolCallId>`, so the hook didn't run or didn't match; a hook plugin switched off, a hook that printed something other than its one line) or `other` ("ran ungated: the hook asked, something else let it run": the mark is there but no request came, e.g. another hook's allow or a mode that allows silently). In a session that doesn't carry the hook (it reads "not gated yet", or its turn was already running when the repo got its first Tool call gate) it is `unhooked` ("ran ungated: the session isn't gated yet") and the turn goes on, since the session runs without the hook until its next attach. The call is matched against the session's repo and the repo its mark names (see above). When the gates were never read since the plugin started (storage fails and `last-gates.json` can't be read), the call is matched against the `rules.json` Clubmate kept for that repo, when the file is older than the call; such a match logs and stops the same way. A completed call whose `rawInput` is empty can't be matched: it is logged once per session to the plugin's log and not judged. While no gate anywhere is a Tool call gate, completed calls are not checked at all. The check sees what ACP shows, after the call ran: it can't undo it, and it can't see what another hook rewrote.
  - **Not a security boundary.** A Tool call gate checks a cooperative agent. Known ways round: a command the match can't see (`sh -c "$CMD"`, a script, an alias), the same command typed into a Clubmate terminal (never hooked), another PreToolUse hook the agent plants that rewrites a call ours didn't match into one it would (`updatedInput`: the wire shows only the original input, so **Ran ungated** can't catch it either; no PostToolUse hook is added for it), a tool that ignores the hook's `ask` (Claude Code's `SubagentHandback`), and changes to Clubmate's own state dir (the hook plugin, `rules.json`, the marks).
- **Holding the request.** Every permission request of a session whose repo has a Tool call gate (a gate's own review nodes and night sessions never) is matched again by the plugin, before the night and normal permission paths: the tool's name and input come from the request's `toolCall.name` and `toolCall.rawInput` (fallbacks: `_meta.claudeCode.toolName`, then the earlier `tool_call` update of the same call, also inside a subagent), matched against the repo's active Tool call gates as the hook does. A matching request is held: no permission entry, no card, no Needs you, no push, nothing a parent agent's `agents.permission` can answer (it gets "unknown permission request"); the session stays `running`. It is held whatever the session's mode and whether or not the session carries the hook, so a mode's own request for a matching call is held too. A request takes every gate it matches at once or waits: a gate holds one request of a session at a time, requests wait in arrival order, and none overtakes an earlier one on a gate that one needs, so two parallel calls on the same two gates are taken one after the other. Taking it stores one hold per gate on the session (`calls` in the stored record: the gate's key over the tool, its input, HEAD and tree, round 1 for a new hold, `call` the `toolCallId` waiting now, the tool, the input (at most 2 KiB), the caption of a wrapped or unreadable match, `seen`), and marks the tool entry with `gate {state: "held", holds: [{gate, name, repo, round, of, caption?}]}` (the holds in the order their match appears in the call), through a log line, so it replays and moves the entry. A held request is answered only with the option of kind `allow_once` or `reject_once`, never `allow_always` or `reject_always`; with no such option it is answered cancelled and logged. When the answer is a refusal the mark reads `gate {state: "refused", round}`, otherwise it is cleared (`gate: null`). After a restart the holds and their rounds stay, `call` is cleared and the mark with it; the session gets the usual cut-off prompt, and the agent's retry is held again. `agents.sessions` sends `calls: [{gate, tool, input, caption?, round, of, since, held, request?, wait?}]` (`held` while a request waits on it) and, while a request is held, `landing {words: "held", tone: "accent", verdict: "reviewing", held: ‹match›, badge: "held · ‹match›", badgeTone: "accent", line: "Held · ‹match› · ‹graph›…", hint: "Held by a gate — messages queue", phoneHint, header, headerAccent, glyph, glyphTone}` (see [Transcript folding](#transcript-folding)) and `callHeld: true`; with several gates `badge` is "held · ‹first match› +N" and `line` "Held · ‹first match› +N · K graphs reviewing" (a land review or a Done hold that needs more attention wins). A request that no gate matches but that the hook marked, or whose tool or input can't be read, goes on to the usual permission card, which only the user may answer: a parent agent's `agents.permission` gets "only the user answers this request". A gate holds a call only inside a turn (ruling 15): a held call always has an open turn of its session, a running turn or a follow-up cycle Clubmate tracks (one that starts when the agent sends updates after its turn ended, as after a background command). Updates that come after the prompt's response (the plugin marks the turn answered as the response is read, before any later update) are such a follow-up even while the turn's end is still settled: the turn hands over to a follow-up cycle when it ends with no queued prompt, also when it ends in an error (the turn failed, so the session keeps `error` and its failure card while the cycle runs and after it ends; the cycle still holds its calls, and a held call shows its card as usual), and when a queued prompt starts the next turn, the follow-up's calls stay held in that turn. A matching request that arrives with no turn open (a background subagent's call after the turn ended, or a call while only a background command runs) is answered `reject_once` at once, before any review: no hold, no round, no card; the tool entry's mark reads `gate {state: "refused", reason, holds: [{gate, name, repo}]}` and each matching gate's history gets `refused` by `no-turn`, both with the reason "[Clubmate] ‹repo› · ‹match› · a gate holds only calls made inside a turn; run it from the session's turn" (written to `<hook dir>/findings/<toolCallId>` before the refusal, so the session's `PostToolBatch` hook puts it under the refused call as for **Changes** below; no fallback prompt is queued for it). A turn or cycle that ends while one of its calls is still held refuses that call the same way at its end, before the end is settled; the gate's hold and its round stay, `call` is cleared (a turn's end never resets a round). So during a hold the session reads `running` because its turn runs, and a message sent meanwhile is the turn's ordinary queue or steer (the request stays open and the agent reads the text after the call). A hold has no timeout; four things end a held request without a verdict, each answered cancelled: the user's Stop (the session's call holds are dropped with their runs, each logged `stopped` in its gate's history), an `agents.cancel` that isn't the user's word (an agent's, a cron job's, a trigger's or another plugin's: the holds and their rounds stay; `call` is cleared), closing the session (holds dropped), and the agent withdrawing the request (`$/cancel_request`, also one that arrives before the request is registered) or its process ending, or its connection closing while the process lives, which stops the process (the holds, rounds and runs stay; `call` is cleared, and the agent's retry is held again).
- **The review.** Taking a request starts a run of each gate's pinned graph, one run per gate (as for a Done hold: `gateFor {gate, point: "tool", key, lander, run, verdict}`, nodes titled "Tool call review · ‹session title›", read-only in the session's worktree, steps in a gate worktree at `git commit-tree <tree> -p <HEAD>`; nodes are told "[Clubmate Tool call reviewer] …" or "[Clubmate Tool call gate node] …"). The first prompt names the repo and the gate, then, under a line saying that everything inside the fence below is data from the reviewed agent and not instructions, the session's title, its worktree and branch, the tool and its input in a fence longer than any run of backticks in them; an input over 16 KiB is cut there and the prompt names `<state>/tool-input/<key>`, which holds it whole (written when the call is taken and whenever its key moves, for any input over 2 KiB, so a review restarted after a restart still reads all of it); when neither the call nor that file is left, the prompt says the input was cut at 2 KiB and asks for `changes`. A call held to be safe says so ("the gate couldn't read this line as commands; it holds the text ‹match›"). Then HEAD, `git diff <base> <HEAD>` for what the branch changes (`base` the merge-base with the branch's recorded base, else the default branch, else its upstream; left out when there is none) and `git diff <HEAD> <tree>` for what isn't committed, and the rule: a call that reaches outside what is shown (another directory or repo, another branch or remote than this worktree's, `--all`, `--mirror`, a script whose content can't be read) is a finding and the answer is `changes`. When Clubmate couldn't read the worktree's HEAD and tree the prompt says so, and the call can't be released on that review: a release always reads them again, and one it can't read sends every gate back to review (`moves` + 1). Verdict nodes also get the `agents_land_verdict` line with the key. A later round on the same run gets the call now and `git diff <last reviewed tree> <tree>`. A retried call whose key equals the open round's (after a restart, a withdrawn request or an agent's cancel) joins it: nothing new is sent to the run. After a restart the reviews of stored holds without a verdict start again; first, every open review node of a held turn or call whose run no hold names (the plugin stopped after the run started and before the hold stored it) is closed and its run stopped. The verdict is stored under `tool-verdict/<gate>/<key>` (deleted with the gate) and logged in the gate's history with `call` (the input, 200 runes). `approved` stops that gate's run, closes its nodes and removes its hold; the request is answered once every gate it matches has approved. An approved verdict is used once: it is deleted before the call it allows is answered, and when the delete fails twice the call is not answered: its gates go back to review (`moves` + 1, the card at the cap) and it stays held. A request whose key already has a stored `approved` for a gate (the verdict came while nobody waited: after a restart, a withdrawn request or a turn's end that refused the call, or its request ended before the other gates decided; a release that finds its request already answered leaves the approvals for the retry) takes it without a run, and the call after it is held again; every approval a gate gives a call is also noted as that gate's `spare` in the session's record until the call it allows runs, so after a restart or a withdrawn request it serves only the session's next call on that gate: when that call's key differs, the approval is deleted, also when the spare was noted while that call was being taken; Stop and close delete every spare's approval. Before a release every gate's key is computed again from HEAD and the tree as they are now: an approval for another key is stale and not used (a verdict-time move re-keys the call too, so an approval for a tree the worktree has left never serves it after it moves back); that gate's stale approval is deleted and the gate goes back to review on the new key in the same round (`moves` + 1; the third move opens its card), the tool entry's `gate.holds` lists it again and the call stays held, so only approvals for the key as it is now let it run. `<state>/tool-input/<key>` is removed when its hold ends or moves to another key, and files no stored hold names are removed at plugin start.
- **Changes.** A gate's `changes` is kept on its hold (`last`, the findings, the deferred ones, `told` false) and the gate counts as decided for the request waiting on it; nothing is refused or sent until every gate the request matches has decided. Then, unless a gate that said `changes` has its card open (its rounds used up: the call stays held), the request is refused once (`reject_once`) and the agent gets one message, the gate's findings, read right under the refused call's result in the same turn. The message has one group per gate that said `changes`, in the order their match appears in the call, each "[Clubmate] From the gate · ‹repo› · ‹match› · round N of M · changes", its findings as `- ` lines and its deferred ones after "Not blocking:", the same fix lines as a Done gate's changes prompt (the transcript block leaves them out), and one tail, "The next ‹match› goes through the gate again, as round N+1." (the first group's match); gates that approved aren't named. It is never steered and never sent while a request is open: before the refusal the plugin writes it to `<hook dir>/findings/<toolCallId>` (temp file and rename) and sets `sent` to that id on every hold in it. The session's `PostToolBatch` hook (`'<tool-hook>' batch '<dir>' || true`) runs after the batch with the refused call: for each `tool_calls[].tool_use_id` it reads `findings/<id>`, prints one line `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"PostToolBatch","additionalContext":…}}` with the texts, an identical text once (two refused calls of one batch each keep their file, or for one gate the later file carries both, so both are read), and only after that write succeeded moves each file it read to `told/<id>` (a crash or a failed write before the move leaves the file, so the findings can be printed again, once more, never lost; a plugin claim between the print and the move, as when a parallel call's refusal merges them, sends them once more as a prompt); joined texts over 9 000 characters are cut there with a line naming `<hook dir>/messages/batch-<first id>`, which holds them whole; with nothing to take, or on any error, it prints nothing and exits 0 (`|| true`: an exit 2 would end the turn with no reply). Claude Code adds the text right after the batch's results, before the agent's next model call, so for a subagent's call the subagent reads it and its parent only its report. A message over 9 000 bytes (Claude Code cuts hook context at 10 000 characters into a 2 KB preview and a file; spike row 15f) is cut to 9 000 bytes with its head, a line naming `<hook dir>/messages/<toolCallId>` (which holds it whole) and its tail, so the hook passes it on as it is. A queued gate message is never handed to the session's own `agents_wait` as the user's words. When the refused call's `failed` update arrives the plugin adds the message to the transcript itself, directly under the refused call (inside a subagent's fold for its call): a log line `{sessionUpdate: "gate_message", groups: [{head, findings, deferred}], tail, call}` that becomes a `user` entry `gate-‹call›` with the text and `gateMessage {groups, tail, call}`. `told` becomes true when the findings are in the agent's context: `told/<sent>` exists (read from disk when the session goes idle and when a gate takes another request of the session; then `sent` is cleared and the file removed), or the fallback's message went out as its turn's prompt. **Fallback** for every way the hook can miss (a timeout, bad output, a crash, a session attached before it carried the `PostToolBatch` entry, an unhooked session): when the turn or tracked cycle ends by itself, or ends with an error, with `sent` set (a call is held only inside one, ruling 15) and no `told/<sent>`, before the turn's end is decided (so no "done", notification or Done review comes first), the plugin removes `findings/<sent>` (its claim; "not there" with no `told/<sent>` either still counts as undelivered) and queues the message built at the refusal (after an errored turn it stays queued and goes out first when the next prompt starts a turn; a user's own prompt drops it as it does any queued gate message) (kept on the hold as `message`, so a later take of the gate with another key doesn't change it) once as a prompt that goes alone (never merged with another queued prompt) and starts the next turn, which keeps a Done hold (`gate` on the queued prompt, `message` with the groups); the prompt is recorded in the `gate-‹call›` entry (it takes it over where it already stands, else it starts it), so one message per `call` shows; for a subagent's call the prompt goes to the session and the copy in the subagent's fold drops out of the fold's text. Stop, an agent's cancel and close remove the file, queue nothing and drop every gate message already queued (so does an approval of the same gate for that call's message) (Stop and close drop the holds; an agent's cancel keeps the findings on the hold with `told` false and `sent` cleared, so nothing is queued for them; a cancel that lands while a queued gate message's turn is starting, before its prompt goes out, drops the message but keeps `sent`, and the next turn's end queues it again); a later refusal by the same gate while the earlier call's message is still undelivered (no hook, or `findings/<sent>` not yet claimed) and not being sent puts the earlier groups first in its own message and removes the earlier file and queued message, so neither call's findings are lost (when the earlier message is being sent at that moment, it is not merged and its holds stay with it until its prompt is recorded, so its findings go out once; the later message carries only its own holds); a Clubmate restart settles `sent` the same way when it resumes the holds, and first counts as delivered a queued gate message whose prompt the session's log already holds (a crash between the prompt and its saved record). A request whose key has a stored `changes` for a gate, when the gate's hold has that key (the verdict came while nobody waited, or the same call again), counts that gate as decided `changes` with those findings and no run; a stored `changes` for any other key is dropped. A request with another key after `changes` is reviewed on the new key (in the next round once the refusal went out, see Rounds), and nothing is refused on the old findings.
- **Rounds.** Each gate keeps its own round per session on its hold. When a gate takes a request and its hold is at `changes` with no card open: if the agent was told the findings (`told`, read from disk first), this is the next round (`round` + 1, the findings cleared, the no-verdict retry and the moves counted afresh; the run stays and gets the delta prompt); if not (a blind retry: the refused call's findings haven't reached the agent yet), a same-key retry burns no round: it is refused again with those findings and a file of its own; another key counts as the next round when the agent made it (its `tool_call` came) after this round's refusal went out (`sent`; a call of the same batch, made before the refusal, doesn't), and is reviewed in the same round when the `changes` landed with nobody waiting. When the round moves, the hold forgets the old refusal (`sent`, `told`) and its message is dropped unless another gate's hold still carries it. A stored `changes` decides a call only when the gate's live hold has that key; any other is dropped and the call reviewed. The same call after it was told counts a round with no run: the stored `changes` for its key decides it again, with those findings. A request that comes while the gate's card is open adds no round: it waits as that card's call. **The cap:** once every gate the request matches has decided, if any gate that said `changes` is at `round >= rounds + extra`, nothing is refused or sent: one request id is set as `request` on every hold of the call that said `changes` this round (one card per call, see **Card**) and the call stays held (ruling 6) while its turn runs; a turn that ends first refuses it as any held call (ruling 15), and the card and the hold stay. **No verdict:** as for a Done hold: the run is retried once for the round, then the gate's card opens (`request`), and One more round reviews the same key again. **Reset** (ruling 9): a hold with no call waiting, no card open and at `changes` is dropped (its run stopped, its nodes closed, its gate worktree, `findings/` file and stored `changes` for its key removed, a queued gate message dropped, so the same call is reviewed again as round 1) when the user prompts the session (`agents.prompt` from the user, the phone or voice; the Mac's composer, a starter, an answer to open items, a review sent from the editor) or when `seen`, the last time that gate took or answered a call of the session (stored, so it survives a restart), is more than 24 h ago (`CLUBMATE_AGENTS_ROUND_RESET` in tests): checked when a gate takes a request, at plugin start and hourly. An agent's prompt (a parent's `agents.prompt` or `agents.answer`, a child's wake), a cron job's, a trigger's, another plugin's, Clubmate's own messages (the gate's message too), an agent's `agents.cancel`, a turn's end and a restart never reset it; a hold under review, with a call waiting or with its card open is never reset. The user's Stop and close drop every call hold of the session and remove the stored `changes` for their keys too.
- **Card** (ruling 13: one card per held call). The holds of a call that need the user share one request id (`lg_…`) as `calls[].request` in `agents.sessions`: the holds that said `changes` once any of them is at its cap (also those with rounds left), a gate whose run ended twice without a verdict, and a gate whose key moved a third time (its reason is its group's line); a gate that needs the user while others of the call still review opens the card at once, and a gate of that call that later says `changes` joins it. The session stays `running`, its row reads `asking`, its `gate` reads `needs-you`, it needs you and counts once in Needs you; `pending` (and `requests`) list the request id once per card; `pendingSummary` is `{kind: "land", text: <title>, onMac: true}` (a held turn's or a landing's card first). The Mac and the phone dock it as the same `request.land` node as a held turn's card (`id` `‹session›/landgate/‹request›`, `request`, `gate: true`), with these props, decided here:
  - `title`: one gate, as for Done ("‹graph› still asks for changes after N rounds", "‹graph› ended without a verdict", "The files kept changing during ‹graph›"); several, "N gates still ask for changes" (all said `changes`) or "N gates need you on this call" (mixed).
  - `caption`: one gate, "‹repo› · Tool call · ‹match› · held since ‹HH:MM›"; several, "‹match› · r of m rounds" per gate in command order, joined by " · " ("git push · 2 of 2 rounds · gh pr create · 1 of 1 round"; `m` is the gate's rounds + extra when the card opened, kept on the hold as `of`).
  - `detail`: the call's input (the waiting call's, else the one the holds were made for), a blank line, then for one gate its findings as `- ` lines and "Summary: …"; for several, one group per gate in command order, "‹match› · ‹graph›" on its first line, then its findings and summary, or its reason (the one-gate title) for a gate on the card without `changes`. Groups are split by a blank line.
  - `question` "Run it without another review?"; `name` the session's title; `rule` "Run anyway runs the call as it is. One more round refuses it and sends the agent the findings; its next matching call is reviewed again. Stop cancels the call and stops the turn." (with no `changes` on the card: "… One more round runs the review again on this call. …").
  - `options`: **Run anyway** (`merge`, `allow_once`, `opt+1`, short "run anyway"), **One more round** (`more`, `opt+2`), **Stop** (`leave`, `reject_once`, `opt+3`). With a held turn's or a landing's card on the same session that card docks first and has the keys; the call's card follows once it is answered.
  - The user answers with `agents.gate-answer {request, action: accept | more | stop}` (the buttons send `landAnswer` with `merge`, `more`, `leave`); an agent gets an error, a stale request `this landing request was already answered`. **Run anyway** overrides every gate on the call, also one still reviewing (its run stops): each gate's hold is dropped and logged `approved` by `user` ("you: run") with the call, and the waiting request is answered `allow_once` at once in every mode, with no re-key and no second permission card (the answer is the permission). With no call waiting (a restart, a withdrawn request) it stores an approved verdict by the user for each hold's key instead, so the retry with that key runs by mode. **One more round** gives each gate on the card that said `changes` and is at its cap one more round (`extra` + 1; a gate with rounds left keeps its cap), then the call is refused with the gate's message as above; a gate on the card for no verdict or a moved key runs its review again on the same key, and the call stays held until it decides. **Stop** is the user's Stop: the request is cancelled and the turn stops (it ends as any turn the user stops, not `idle` as a held turn's Stop), every call hold of the session is dropped and logged `stopped`, and the session's other open cards close with it (a landing's card is answered as left and logged `stopped` by `user` in its gate's history), each resolving its own request id.
  - A call that arrives while the card is open with nobody waiting becomes that card's call (`calls[].held` true); the card's detail shows it and Run anyway runs it. When that call has another key than the holds it takes, or the holds it takes sit on more than one card, they get one new request id (the old card resolves, the new one posts its notification), so an answer meant for the old card is stale and never runs a call it didn't show; two parallel calls that take the holds of one card apart each get a card of their own.
- **Released, by mode** (ruling 3: after the gate, the user's own rules decide):

  | Session mode | An approved call |
  |---|---|
  | `auto`, `bypassPermissions` | runs at once |
  | `default`, `acceptEdits` | runs when the user's rules allow it, else the usual permission card |
  | `plan` and any other mode | the usual permission card |

  "The user's rules allow it" means the Bash command is a plain one (words, `'…'` quotes, no `;`, `&&`, `|`, `$`, redirects or leading `VAR=`) and the rules decide `allow` the way a graph step's are read: allow rules count only from the user's `~/.claude/settings.json` and the managed settings (and their drop-ins), never from the worktree's `.claude/settings.json` or `settings.local.json`, which the agent can write; ask and deny rules count from every file. So `git commit -m x && git push` always gets the card in an asking mode, even under an allow rule for `git push`, and a tool other than Bash always gets it there. A deny rule denies the call before any request, so there is nothing to hold.

- **A moved worktree, gate changes, trust.** At a reviewer's verdict the deciding gate's key is computed again (the same call, HEAD and tree now); when it moved the verdict is refused ("the session's files changed since this review began"), the hold takes the new key and the same run is prompted on it, and the third move opens the gate's card (`request`); the other gates of the call are checked at the release. Which repo a session's worktree belongs to is cached per path with the worktree's `.git` entry and read again when that entry is not the same file any more (the worktree removed, moved or made again, maybe in another repo), so a path made again in another repo is matched against that repo's gates and its next attach carries that repo's hook. A gate whose graph changed since it was pinned keeps the call held with `wait` set on the hold (`calls[].wait`; the session's `landing` reads "waits for trust", warning tone) and trusting the gate starts the review. Pause (ruling 11) stops new holds only: the paused gate leaves `rules.json`, a request still waiting its turn for it is taken off it (and released by mode when no gate is left), and a call it already holds stays held: its review finishes and decides as on an active gate (approved runs it, `changes` refuses it with the message, the cap opens the card, a trust wait keeps it held). Deleting a gate, or changing its match so it no longer matches a held call, drops its hold, stops its run, drops what the gate kept outside the hold (its approval and spare, its queued gate message) and logs `released` by `gate-deleted` / `gate-changed` (with the call) in its history; the call's gate mark is redrawn without it; the gate is taken off the waiting request, which is released by mode, refused or carded when the gates left have decided, and released when none is left. A gate changed so it now matches a held call doesn't take it: it holds from the next call. A hold with no call waiting stays on a change and goes with a deleted gate.

#### Test gates

A gate graph (a graph file with `"gate": true`, see [agent graphs § Gate graphs](agent-graphs.md#gate-graphs)) may have a start step with `decide` and `run: "{{gate.test}}"`, which the run fills with the gate's `test` command (one line, at most 500 characters, set in the Gates sheet's Test command field or with `test` in `agents.gate-add`/`agents.gate-update`, the user only). The step runs in the gate's worktree on the exact tree that would land, so it tests the landing on top of its target, not on the branch's old base. When it ends with an outcome in its `decide`, the plugin writes the verdict itself, through the same code as `agents.land-verdict` after its decider check: `pass` → `approved`, `fail` → `changes` with one finding `<node>: <command> failed (exit N)` and the last 60 lines (at most 4000 characters) of its output in a fenced block, `by: check`, `reviewer` `<graph>/<node>` (no session). So the lander is prompted as for a reviewer's verdict ("approved by test-gate/tests"; the findings and the round), the round counts toward the gate's rounds and the "still asks for changes" card, and `landReviews[]`, `agents.gate-verdicts` and the commits list of the next review (`(checked)`) carry it. The next round reruns the start steps on the new tree. Built-ins: `test-gate` (only the step, no model) and `test-review` (the step decides only `fail`; a pass goes to the Opus reviewer).

- **Pass cache.** A `pass` of a deciding step is stored under the gate (`gate-check/<gate>` in the plugin's storage: pin, node, sha256 of the test command, tree, time; 50 per gate, oldest dropped). When the same gate, pin, node, command and tree come again (the target moved but the tree is the same, or a run stopped by a restart starts again; a move that changes no file after an approval of the same landing is carried first, see Carry in [Gates](#gates)), the step doesn't run: its outcome is `pass` with the note `cached: passed on this tree at <time>`. A `fail` is never cached. A gate delete and a new graph or pin drop the gate's passes.
- **One test run at a time per repo.** A `{{gate.test}}` step takes a lock on the repo's main checkout (its real path) before it runs; one that waits shows `waiting: another landing's tests run` in the run and in the lander's `review pending` text. The lock is in memory: a restart stops gate runs anyway.
- **On the land row.** A landing whose gate run has no reviewer session counts as `reviewing` while its deciding step is open (the lander's `landing.verdict`, the gate's `state` and its `landings`), and reads its deciding step in `landing.words` and `landing.check` ("tests by check"), and the Mac's landing line shows it where Open review would be; the gate page's verdict rows read "approved · by check" and "changes · by check" (counted as approved and changes in its History line; the Verdict column widens to hold them), its `landings` entry has `since` from when the step started, and its live card reads "Running ‹command›", or "Waiting: another landing's tests run" while another landing of the repo runs its tests, where a reviewer's latest line would be; a selected "changes · by check" row's card shows the finding as "‹node›: ‹command› failed (exit N)" with the output tail under it in a mono block (6 lines, then Show all). Mac and phone draw the same plugin fields.

#### Graph and trust

The gated agent can't touch what decides: a gate's graph is read only from the repo's main checkout (`.clubmate/graphs/<name>.json` in its working tree), your own graphs or the built-ins, never from a worktree, so a lander's `.clubmate/graphs/opus-review.json` is ignored. `agents.gate-add` pins the graph's full content hash (prompts, models and edges included) and refuses a graph without a verdict node or a deciding step (`‹graph› can't decide a landing: no node reports approved and changes`), and a graph that uses `{{gate.test}}` on a gate with no test command (`this graph runs the gate's test command: set Test command`; `agents.gate-update` refuses the same when it changes `graph` or clears `test`). A gate graph (`"gate": true`) is for land gates only: a Done or Tool call gate refuses it at add, at a `graph` change and at `trust` (`<graph> is a gate graph: only a land gate runs it`), and the sheet doesn't offer it for those points. The gate's own test command runs with no Bash approval only as a step whose `run` is exactly `{{gate.test}}`, in that gate's worktree: you typed it in a user-only command, and a change of `test` is a gate update, so a new command needs you again. A longer `run` that holds `{{gate.test}}` asks for the resolved command as any step does. The trade-off: the command is yours, but it runs the lander's tree (its `Makefile`, test code and scripts) without asking each time. When the graph's hash no longer equals the pin, a landing that needs a run doesn't start or prompt one: `git.land` fails with `the gate's graph changed: waiting for the user to trust it` and the gate's `state` is `trust` until you `agents.gate-update {id, trust: <hash>}`. A gate pinned to a built-in (`pinBuiltin`), or stored before pins, follows that built-in across app updates without trust; one pinned to a graph of the repo or yours never falls back to the built-in of its name when that file goes: it waits for trust like any change. Agent nodes that don't decide are told so (their system text and brief say to report findings in their reply, not with `agents_land_verdict`). A gate run's edges are delivered by the plugin, without triggers, and its run isn't listed among the Agents tree's runs: every node session of the run is a review session (`gateFor` with the run's `run`), so like the reviewer it has no row in the tree and shows under its lander only while it asks you or fails (see **In Agents** below). What the run itself needs from you reaches you through its lander: a step waiting for approval or an edge held at its round cap makes the landing `asking` with the need's text in `landing.ask`, its `agents.gate-verdicts` landing `needs-you` with `ask` and `run`, and its gate `needs-you` (the dock's "1 asking"). The run's own entry in `agents.sessions` (`kind: run`, `needsYou`) is the one counted in Needs you; it carries `rowUnder` and `nestUnder` (the lander), `gateRound` and the title "Round n review", and makes the lander's group `needsYou`. On the Mac the lander unfolds to a child row `run:‹run›`: the amber `shield`, "Round n review", the need's text in warning; it counts once in Needs you. Selecting it (and the Mac notification's reveal of `run:‹run›`) opens the gate's page with that landing picked, whose live card reads "Round n · asking" with the text in warning and, below it, the run's own card: the step's permission card (Allow once ⌥1 · Always allow… ⌥2 only for a step that runs in the main checkout, which a gate step on its gate worktree never does · Reject ⌥3, answered with `agents.graph-approve`) or the round cap with Continue ⌘↩ (`agents.graph-continue`). The gate page (in Agents and in `agents.gates.main`, which the phone's Gates screen draws) refuses both from an agent, like the Graphs page. The phone nests the run's entry under its lander in Needs you and opens it on its run screen, which answers with the same commands. A gate graph's steps need no `agents.graph-trust`: the gate's pin is your trust. Each still needs [approval](agent-graphs.md#steps) like any step outside the main checkout: a user or managed allow rule, or your answer. They run in the landing's own gate worktree, `<main checkout>/.worktrees/.gate-<gate id>-<key>`, detached at `git commit-tree <tree> -p <old>`, so a linter or test step checks exactly what would land and the lander can't edit what it checks; the plugin prepares it with [`git.gate-tree`](git.md#gitgate-tree) before it starts or prompts a run, only for a graph with steps. The tree gets the main checkout's `.clubmate/worktree-include` files and runs `.clubmate/setup.sh` only when you already trust it; an untrusted `setup.sh` is skipped (never asked for during a landing), so a step that needs the setup fails until you trust it. Deleting a gate removes its trees and any `.gate-<gate>` dir git no longer lists. Each landing through the gate (lander, target and source branch) has its own, so two landings at once never check each other's tree. A second `git.land` for the same landing while its worktree is prepared answers `review pending`; when that preparation or the run's start then fails, the lander is prompted with the error and asked to call `git_land` again. It is made with the landing's run (the repo's `.clubmate/setup.sh` runs then, only when you already trust it) and reset to the next round's tree only when it prompts that same run and no step of the run is running or queued, so a build stays warm across that landing's rounds; when a step still runs, the round starts a new run on a new worktree instead. It is removed when the landing lands, when a new run replaces its run, when the lander closes (which also stops the run, forgets it so a late verdict from one of its nodes is refused, and closes its review sessions, for a graph without steps too; a lander that closes while its run starts gets no run) and when the gate is deleted (every landing's). Agent nodes stay in the source worktree. The pin covers the graph file only, not a script a step calls by path: that script comes from the tree being landed.

The backstop for plain git: a session whose worktree is in a git repo gets, at its start or resume, `core.hooksPath` pointed at the plugin's `githooks` folder through `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` (appended after any entries already in the environment), plus `CLUBMATE_LAND_COMMON` (the repo's common dir), `CLUBMATE_LAND_VERDICTS` and `CLUBMATE_LAND_QUEUE` (the repo's queue mirror folder, `<githooks>/queue/<hash of the common dir>`, see Hold below), service sessions and reviewers included. A repo with any active land gate (whatever its `into`; a Done gate alone doesn't make a repo gated) adds `CLUBMATE_LAND_PROTECT` and `CLUBMATE_LAND_PROTECT_GLOB`; in an ungated repo the hook protects no ref and only holds a queued branch. Night sessions (sandbox clones) and sessions outside a git repo get none of it (inherited ones are dropped), and a gate added later applies to a running session from its next start or resume. A session started before this existed gets the variables at its next start or resume. Because the folder is injected in every git repo, a tool that reads `core.hooksPath` (for example `pre-commit install`, which refuses to install when it is set) sees Clubmate's `githooks` folder in an ungated repo too; run such a tool in your own terminal, which has no injected variables. Each hook in that folder runs the repo's own hook of that name (its `core.hooksPath`, read without the injected entry, else `<common dir>/hooks`) with the same arguments and input, so the repo's pre-commit still runs.

What the hook protects: `refs/heads/<default branch>`, for a child of a session on a feature branch that parent's branch, and for a session whose branch has a recorded base that base and its ancestor sessions' bases (also while no worktree has it checked out, so a base checked out after the session started is already protected); a session's list only grows: a resume adds to the list it had and never drops a ref from it, in that repo only. Every active land gate with an exact or glob `into` adds its own: an exact name is protected like the default branch (a branch that doesn't exist yet too) and a glob goes to `CLUBMATE_LAND_PROTECT_GLOB` as a ref pattern (`refs/heads/release/*`); `any`, `default` and `base` add nothing (the default branch and the bases are protected as above, and the review inside `git.land` covers `any`), so with only these an agent can still switch to a new branch, commit on it, rename it, add a worktree for it and delete a spare branch. A glob gate removes that for every branch its glob matches: the hook refuses any update of a matching ref without a verdict, a new branch and the session's own branch included, so a gate with `into: fix/*` stops a child on `fix/x-t1` from committing with plain git, and `into: **` stops every agent commit. Give a glob gate a pattern that the agents' own branch names don't match, or use an exact name. The globs come from the active gates at the session's start or resume; an exact name stays in the list, as the list only grows, until the app restarts, also after its gate is deleted or paused. The hook matches a pattern with `landgate.Match`, the matcher `intoRank` uses (`*` within one `/` segment, `**` as a whole segment at any depth), written again in awk, and ignores ASCII case for it (a ref is refused when it matches the pattern as written or both lowercased; `intoRank` matches both lowercased too, and compares an exact `into`, the default branch and a base ignoring case, so a gate on `main` also reviews a landing into `MAIN`, and `git.land` refuses an `into` that names an existing branch only in another case; so `into` can't hold upper-case letters in a `[...]` class, a `\`, or a `[` inside a class such as `[[:alpha:]]`; `?` and classes count bytes in the hook under `LC_ALL=C`, so a non-ASCII name can miss). A branch a pattern names is refused like a protected one, the session's own branch included, so `git push . feat:release/1.0` and a commit on `release/1.0` are refused, and so is `git branch release/9.9` and a `git switch -c release/9.9`. One way past it is left: git writes the new ref of `git branch -m` and `git branch -c` outside a reference transaction, so a rename or copy onto a protected name that doesn't exist yet (`release/3.0`, or an exact gate's branch before it exists) isn't caught; the landing review in `git.land` still covers the landing itself. It matches protected refs ignoring ASCII case (on macOS `refs/heads/MAIN` is the same file as `refs/heads/main`), so `git update-ref refs/heads/MAIN`, `git branch -f MAIN` and `git push . feat:MAIN` are refused like `main` on any disk. A session's own branch is not protected unless a land gate names it (an exact name or a glob), so a parent that merges a child into its own branch with plain git is not refused; that change reaches the default branch only through the parent's own landing, whose review covers the whole diff. `reference-transaction` refuses, in its `prepared` state, any update of a protected ref whose `(old, tree)` has no approved verdict, `--no-verify` included, and any deletion of one or turning it into a symbolic ref, and says to land with `git_land` and that `git reset --merge` puts back what the command already changed. It lets through an update that leaves the ref where it was (`reset`, `stash`, `rebase --abort`, `reset --merge`) and a fast-forward to a commit already on the branch's own upstream (`branch.<name>.remote` and `.merge`, else `origin/<name>`), so `git fetch` and `git merge --ff-only` with it keep the branch in sync; a commit on any other remote-tracking ref (a pushed feature branch) or a rewind to an older pushed commit is refused. `pre-push` refuses pushing to a protected branch's name on any remote, or deleting it there, unless the pushed commit is already on the local protected branch or has an approved verdict against the remote's value, so `git push origin feat:main` followed by a pull can't move it either; pushing the branch itself passes. A forced update without an old value is checked against the ref's current value. Other repos, temp repos in tests included, are untouched, and so are your own terminals and commits, which never get the hook. Bypassing it takes a deliberate step (`-c core.hooksPath=`, dropping the variables, writing the ref file, forging the upstream's remote-tracking ref with `update-ref` or `fetch . x:refs/remotes/origin/main`, which can't be told apart from a real fetch, or pushing to the repo itself with `git push .`, whose receiving side runs without the session's variables), so the plugin also logs `agents: main moved without a land verdict: <sha>` when a turn of a session on the default branch of a gated repo leaves a commit with no verdict for its parent and tree. It is a log, not Needs you, since your own commit on main looks the same.

**Hold.** `reference-transaction` (`prepared`) also refuses an agent session's update of `refs/heads/<branch>` while the file `$CLUBMATE_LAND_QUEUE/refs/heads/<branch>` exists and is not empty (the queue's mirror: a branch with a landing queue), in any repo with the hook env, gated or not, unless it is a fast-forward to a commit already on the branch's own upstream (the same sync rule as above). The text is `the branch <into> has a landing queue; stage your change and call git_land with mode index to take a place`, with the `git reset --merge` note. It holds the agent's plain commit, merge, `update-ref` and deletion on that branch, in any worktree; the branch's own landing passes, since `git.land` runs in the git plugin with no session variables, and so passes the user's own shell, which never has the hook. The hold stops commits, not edits. The hook reads only whether the file is non-empty, not its content, so the head's own session is held too (the head commits through `git.land`, which has no session variables). A missing or empty file holds nothing. The folder name is the hash of the repo's canonical common dir, the same value as `CLUBMATE_LAND_COMMON` (`gitrepo.Dirs`), and whoever writes the mirror files (the landing queue, backlog #1256) must hash that value. The verdict check of a protected ref is also stricter: a verdict `(old, tree)` approves an update to a commit with that tree only when `old` is that commit's first parent, so a verdict can't be reused on a commit built on another base (`pre-push` keeps the earlier check, as it compares with the remote's value).

### The Gates section

Gates is its own section of the agents plugin (`agents.gates`, after Graphs, off the Mac rail (`rail: false`, see Pages under [Needs you](#needs-you)); slots `agents.gates.sidebar` and `agents.gates.main`). It lists the gates whose repo is a folder of this workspace; a gate on a repo of another workspace shows there. A workspace sees only its own landings: each logged verdict records the workspace of its lander (or of the `git.land` call), and the section, `agents.gates` and `agents.gate-verdicts` show only this workspace's, so an agent can't read another workspace's titles and findings. A gate on a repo that is no folder of any open workspace shows only in your `agents.gates`; `agents.gate-delete {id}` removes any gate, and `agents.gate-add` with a match the repo already has fails naming that gate's id.

- **Verdict history.** Every verdict on a gated landing is also logged under its gate (`land-history/<gate>` in the plugin's storage, newest first, the last 100; deleting the gate deletes it): `{gate, outcome, by, lander?, title?, into, source?, round?, reviewer?, findings?, deferred?, old?, oldTree?, tree?, reason?, carriedFrom?, at}`, `by` `reviewer`, `check` (a deciding step, see [Test gates](#test-gates)), `carried` (an approval carried across a target move on other files, see Carry above; with `carriedFrom`, the target commit of the first approval, for a review ahead the synthetic base commit), `docs` or `user` (Land anyway, or outcome `stopped` when you Stop the landing's card or Stop on a call card closes it), `round` the review round it closed (the rounds before a Land anyway; none for docs only), `title` the lander's title then. A land verdict from a reviewer, a deciding step or docs only also has `old` (the target's commit it was reviewed on), `oldTree` (that commit's tree) and `tree` (the tree that would land), and, when it is not the first review of its landing (a docs-only approval is not a review: one with no review before it leaves the next review a first one), `reason`: `round` (the landing's last verdict asked changes), else `base-moved` (`old` changed since the last review or docs-only approval) or `tree-changed` (`old` the same, the tree changed); the landing's review record keeps what it compares with (`landReviews[]`), so a plugin restart doesn't lose it. So the log tells a review that ran again only because the target moved from one that checked new work. `agents.gate-verdicts {id, limit?}` (a gate on this workspace's folders, else `no such land gate`) returns `{landings, verdicts}`: this workspace's landings the gate holds right now (`{lander, title?, into, source?, state, round, reviewer?, findings?, since?}`, `state` `reviewing` while its reviewer's round runs, `needs-you` while its Land anyway/Stop request waits or its review run needs you, then with `ask` and `run`) and the logged verdicts.
- **What a gate shows** is decided by the plugin and sent with each gate in `agents.gates`: `state` is `trust` while the gate's graph changed since it was pinned, else `needs-you` when one of this workspace's landings waits for you, else `paused`, else `reviewing` while a review runs, else `active`; `last` is the newest logged verdict; `landings` as above. `trust` never hides the rest: the row's detail follows the dock's priority (`to trust` › N asking › N landing › paused › last verdict), a paused gate that still holds something reads "paused · N landing" ("paused · N held" for Done and Tool call) in the row and in the page header's badge, and the page header still shows `paused` and "needs you" next to `to trust`. The phone's Gates screen groups the gates by repo (a repo row with its gate count, unfolded) and names each by its `name`.
- **Detail.** The header has the Landing glyph, the gate's `name`, active or paused ("paused · N held" in accent while a paused Done or Tool call gate still holds something), "to trust" (warning) and "needs you" badges when they apply, `last: <verdict> <age> · N reviewing`, and Pause/Resume (P), Edit (E) and Delete (⌘⌫, confirmed: landings it holds then land without a review, their runs stop and their review sessions close, and the history goes too). Then Repo (and its path), Holds ("an agent's landing (git_land)"), Match (what its `into` holds: "landings into main, the default branch", "landings into the parent's branch, the branch Clubmate made the worktree from; a branch made with git has none", "every landing: into main, into a parent's branch, and a child's merge into its parent's branch", "landings into a branch like release/*", or one branch), Graph (its title, built in / yours / in ‹repo›, "pinned" and the first 6 characters of `pin`, Open graph on the Mac, which opens the `graph:‹name›` page; then each node numbered: its role, `agent · ‹model› · ‹effort› effort` with "approves or asks for changes" on the verdict node, or `step · ‹command›`; then "ends approved · changes"), Rounds and Docs only, a line counting the last 7 days' verdicts ("History · last 7 days · approved 2 · changes 1"), and a table: the landings under review or waiting first, then the history (When, Verdict, Landing with its round and finding count, Into, and Open review, which opens the reviewer session in Agents while it exists in this workspace; a needs-you row has Open landing instead, the lander with its Land anyway/Stop card). Reviewers have no row in the tree, so this table and the lander's header are the way to them. With a landing under review selected (or none selected), a live card under the table reads "Round n · reviewing", `‹source› → ‹into› · ‹elapsed›`, the reviewer's latest message line and a note that findings show when it decides, with Open review ⌥R and Open landing. An approved verdict with a `summary` has a muted row under it, "Summary · ‹summary›" (row id `‹at›/summary`; selecting it selects that verdict's card). The selected verdict with findings, deferred items or a summary shows them in a card instead, in plain ink whatever the verdict (a `changes` verdict is history, not something to act on), with Open review ⌥R for that round's reviewer. A gate with no landings says the next landing it holds waits for its graph. With no gate selected the page explains gates and has New gate ⌘N.
- **Trust card.** While `state` is `trust` an amber card sits on top of the page, the same tree on the Mac and the phone: "‹graph› changed: trust the new version?", "Pinned ‹a› · now ‹b› · N of M steps changed" (first 6 characters; M counts the steps of either version, so a removed step counts; no count when no step changed), then everything the pinned hash covers that changed, each with a line naming it and a `diffLines` node under it (every − and + line shows, however long; each run of unchanged lines inside it folds into "N unchanged lines", which unfolds on a click or tap): "Graph · title, description or limits changed", per step "‹role› · step / agent · command, prompt, model, effort, settings changed" (settings is the rest of the node as JSON: outcomes, so whether it decides, exits, inputs, timeout, branch…; a multi-line value is diffed line by line), added steps "‹role› · ‹kind› · added" with what they do and their prompt as "+" lines (never the step's raw settings JSON; "Order changed:" shows where it went), removed steps the same with "removed" and "−", one `markdown` line "Order changed: …" (the step titles in the new order, the moved ones in bold, a title shared by two steps followed by " · ‹id›") and "Edges · which node runs after which, and what it is told changed" (the edges as JSON, their texts included); a pin stored before 18 Nov is compared in its own, older shape (it lacks a step's prompt and an agent node's command), so those fields show nothing for it and the card falls back to "changed in a way this card doesn't list" when nothing else differs; every field the pinned hash covers reaches one of these lines (a step's prompt, an agent node's command or args and a step's args beside `run` go in its prompt or settings line); the unchanged steps fold into one line "‹steps›: unchanged"; if none of that differs, a line says the content changed in a way the card doesn't list. Then, while the gate holds something, the waiting line "Until you trust it, the gate runs nothing." with "1 done turn waits: ‹title›", "1 call waits: ‹call›" or "1 landing waits: ‹branch› into ‹base›" ("N done turns wait: ‹first title› +N−1", "N calls wait: …", "N landings wait: …"); with nothing held, that the gate runs nothing until then (a landing it holds is refused and runs through the new version when its agent calls `git_land` again). While the gate waits for trust its header meta reads "graph changed ‹age› · N waiting for trust" on the Mac (the age of the graph file; "· N waiting" left out at 0) and "Graph changed · ‹repo›" on the phone. What waits counts the same everywhere: the gate's held turns, held calls and landings while its `state` is `trust`. The plugin keeps the pinned version once a new pin is saved (`gate-pin/‹gate›` in its storage, deleted with the gate); a gate pinned before that says the pinned version wasn't kept and shows the whole current graph: its title and limits, every node with what it does, its prompt and settings, and the edges. Its buttons are **Trust and run** (`agents.gate-update {id, trust}` with the hash the card showed, so a file changed since fails as changed since shown), Open graph (Mac only) and Pause the gate (no confirm; what the gate holds stays held). On the Mac Trust and run is ⌘↩ and runs at once; on the phone and iPad (`agents.gates.main`) it asks once in a system alert, "Trust this version of ‹graph›?" / "What the gate holds is reviewed by it." / Cancel · Trust and run (the button's `confirm` and `confirmMessage`). After Trust the card goes and what waited for trust is reviewed (a held turn's round line reads "round 1 reviewing"). A graph file put back to its pinned content needs no trust: the gate leaves `trust`, and a held turn waiting for trust checks the graph again every 30 s (`CLUBMATE_AGENTS_TRUST_POLL`), so its review starts and the gate's Needs you entry goes as after Trust. A graph that can't be read shows why instead, with Pause.
- **New gate and Edit** open a sheet in `agents.sheet` (so over any section), with "Holds agents only; what you do yourself is never gated." as its note until the gate is complete, then the gate in one sentence ("Holds an agent's landing into main in shop; Opus review decides."). Repo is a menu of the workspace's git folders by repo name ("‹name› · N gates" when it has some), with its path and default branch under it. Holds is Landing ("An agent's landing waits for the graph."). Into is a menu: the default branch (‹name›), the parent's branch, any branch, Branches like… (which adds a pattern field); under the parent's branch a line says only a branch that Clubmate made from a parent's worktree has one and that a branch made with git lands as before, so use any branch to hold every landing into a feature branch, under any branch that it holds every landing, under a pattern how `*` matches. Into the same match the repo already has marks the field "Landing into ‹…› is there already" and disables Create gate; another match on the same repo is fine. A new gate in the sheet starts on the first free of default branch, base, any; `agents.gate-add` without `into` stays `any`, the match old gates migrated to. Decided by is a menu of the graphs the gate can run (the repo's main checkout, your own, built in; "‹title› · built in / yours / in ‹repo›", built in first), the chosen graph's nodes under it ("Simplify → Opus review → Lint → approved / changes"), and one muted line naming the graphs that have no node reporting approved and changes ("‹title›, ‹title› can't decide a landing: no node reports approved and changes"); those are not in the menu, and neither are graph files that can't be read ("‹names› can't be read: open them in Graphs to see why"). A land gate's sheet then has Test command while the picked graph uses `{{gate.test}}` or the field has a command (Edit of a gate with one), a live text field (hint `make test`) with the caption "Runs in the gate's worktree on the exact tree that would land, without asking each time. The command is yours, but it runs the lander's Makefile, test code and scripts."; with such a graph an empty field reads "required: this graph runs it" (a command of two lines or over 500 characters: "one line, at most 500 characters") and Create gate is disabled. The menu lists the gate graphs (`test-gate` "Tests", `test-review` "Tests, then Opus review") with the others; the Graphs section doesn't. Rounds 1–5, Create gate ⌘↩, Cancel ⎋. Edit shows Repo as text and changes Into, Decided by, Rounds and Paused. ⌘N anywhere in Gates runs `agents.gates.new`, which opens the New gate sheet and is the user's only. Every event of the section and the sheet is the user's only, like the commands: an agent's `ui.event` there is refused.
