- **In Agents** a lander says how its landing stands, from `landing` in `agents.sessions` (left out when no landing of it is gated; with several, the one that matters most: asking, then reviewing, changes, approved): `{round, of, verdict, findings?, asking?, words, tone?, badge, badgeTone?, into, source?, reviewer?}`. `verdict` is `reviewing` while its reviewer's round runs, else the last verdict (`changes` or `approved`); `round` is the round under review, or the one the verdict closed (an approval counts as its own round; a Land anyway after the last round keeps that round), `of` the gate's rounds (at least `round`); `findings` the last `changes` verdict's count; `asking` while its Land anyway/Stop request waits or while its review run [needs you](agent-graphs.md#needs-you) (a step outside the allow list waits for approval, or an edge waits at its round cap), then with `ask` (the run's need text: "‹Role› wants to run ‹command›", "‹From› → ‹To› ran N of N rounds") and `run`, the verdict staying `reviewing`; `reviewer` the latest reviewer session (`landReviews[].reviewer`, the verdict node of a review run). `words` and `tone` are the row's detail, decided here: `review…` (`review 2/2…` from round 2 on) in `accent`, `N changes` in `plain` (the text-2 ink), `approved` in `success`, `asking` in `warning`. The Mac row shows them as its detail, running or not, except while the session needs you (then `asking` from its status) or stopped with an error. `badge` and `badgeTone` are the verdict badge: `reviewing` (accent), `N changes` (no tone), `approved` (success). The lander's session header gets a second line while a landing is gated: shield · "Landing into ‹into›" · "round r of n" · the verdict badge · Open review ⌥R, which opens `reviewer` in the main pane. `gate` stays as before (`reviewing`, `changes`, `approved`, `needs-you`) for older clients. Both go when the landing lands, when its gate is deleted, and when its gate is paused unless the paused gate still holds it (round open or card out).
- **A reviewer's page** (opened from Open review on the lander's header, from a verdict's Open review on the gate page, or from its asking row) has the header "Round n review", meta "of ‹lander› · ‹source› → ‹into›" (for a Done gate's node "of ‹held session› · ‹gate name›" and the hold's round) and the plugin's usual parts but never "Input only", the verdict badge (`reviewing`, `N changes`, `approved`) and "asks you" while it asks. After its landing landed the round and verdict come from the gate's verdict history. Above it a crumb `Agents / ‹branch› / ‹lander› / Round n review`, or `Agents / Gates / ‹repo› / Round n review` when it was opened from the gate page, with Back ⌘[ to where it was opened from (the lander by default). The reviewer stays a top-level session `startedBy` the lander, but while its round runs (`gateFor` with no verdict yet) it counts as the lander's child for `childStatus`, `group` and `finished`: a lander whose turn ended on `review pending` lists under Running with the child-running glyph (the phone's "Waiting for subagents"), and so does its own parent, instead of reading Done while the review goes on. The lander counts the same while its round runs with no agent node at work (a command step runs, the verdict node is not reached yet): from the start of a round to its verdict or the run's end its `childStatus` reads running and its turns are not `finished`. Neither such a lander nor a node of a live gate run is auto-closed. The reviewer itself has no row (see [Needs you](#needs-you) § Reviewers).

- **Done gates** (point `turn`) show in the same section with the Done glyph (a speech bubble with a check), after the repo's landing gates and then by name; under their repo in the dock only the match shows ("plans and specs", "any turn"), and the row's detail reads "N held" (`accent`) instead of "N landing", "no turns yet" before the first. The page's facts read Holds "an agent's turn that ends done" and Match "‹sessions› · wrote ‹what›": the sessions read "top-level sessions", "child sessions", "all sessions" (both), with Only AFK "top-level AFK sessions", "child AFK sessions", "AFK sessions"; what reads "plans and specs: **/plans/*.md or **/specs/*.md", "anything" (Nothing required), "any file", "files like …", with no Docs only. Its table has When, Verdict and Held (no Into): a held turn under review is a `reviewing` row with Open review on its verdict node ("waits for trust" while the gate's graph does, `wait` in `agents.gate-verdicts` landings), one waiting on its card an `asking` row with Open session; a user's Accept as done reads "you: done". Its trust card says a held turn stays held and its review runs on the new version once trusted. The live card's note says the session gets the findings as a message from the gate. In the sheet, Holds is a Landing · Done · Tool call chip row (fixed in Edit, shown as text); Done has "An agent's turn that ends done waits for the graph." under it, Sessions (Top-level · Children checkboxes, then "Only AFK sessions" on its own line under them, default Top-level and Only AFK; the note "Holds done turns from the sessions you pick. Only AFK leaves sessions you're running yourself alone." under the field, in warning ink with Create gate disabled while neither Top-level nor Children is picked) and Wrote (Plans and specs, which shows its globs · Any file · Nothing required · Files like…, which adds a comma-separated glob field), no Into and no Docs only; a match the repo has marks Wrote "Done · ‹…› is there already". Its sentence reads "Holds ‹whose› done turn in ‹repo› when it wrote ‹what›; ‹graph› decides.", whose being "a top-level session's", "a child session's", "any session's" (both), with Only AFK "a top-level AFK session's", "a child AFK session's", "any AFK session's" ("Holds a top-level AFK session's done turn in shop when it wrote a plan or a spec; Opus review decides."); with neither Top-level nor Children it reads "Pick top-level, children or both." Edit sends `sessions` and `wrote`.
- **Tool call gates** (point `tool`) show with the terminal glyph, after the repo's Done gates and then by name; under their repo in the dock only the match shows ("git push", "/deploy|release/", "Write"), and the row's detail reads "N held" (`accent`), "no calls yet" before the first; a paused Tool call gate that still holds calls reads "paused · N held" (`accent`), since pausing leaves what it holds held. Held calls count in the Gates dock row's "N held" with held turns. The page's facts read Holds "an agent's tool call" and Match `words` ("Bash · a git push command", "Bash · a command that starts with make deploy", "Write · the input contains secrets", "Bash · the command matches /…/"), with no Docs only. Its table has When, Verdict and Held, the Held cell "‹session title› · ‹call›", from round 2 "round 2 · ‹session title› · ‹call›" so the tail truncation can't cut the round (the first line of `call`, which is the held call's input, at most 2 KiB, on a landing in `agents.gate-verdicts` and its first 200 characters on a logged verdict, also on "stopped by you" and "refused: no turn"): each session's hold of the gate (`calls`) is a landing while a request waits in it or its card is out — `reviewing` with Open review on its verdict node, "waits for trust" while the graph does, `asking` while its card or a node of its run waits for you, "changes · waits for ‹other match›" (plain ink, no Open review; `waitsOn` names the other gate's match, "+N" for more) while this gate said changes and another gate on the same call still reviews, the call still counted as held — and the history's "you: run" (Run anyway), "released: gate deleted" / "released: gate changed", "stopped by you", "refused: no turn" and "ran ungated" (outcome `ungated`, warning ink), which the 7-day line counts too. Its trust card's waiting line names each call as "‹match› in ‹session title›" ("1 call waits: git push in Ship the cart fix", "3 calls wait: git push in Ship the cart fix +2"); with nothing held it reads "Until you trust it, the gate runs nothing. Calls it holds wait, and their review runs on the new version once you trust it."; Delete's confirm says the calls it holds then run without a review. In the sheet Tool call has "An agent's tool call. The agent waits inside it while the graph runs." under Holds, then Call: chips git push · gh pr create (both `how: command`) · Custom…, and under them what the match resolves to ("= Bash · a git push command"). Custom… adds Tool (a text field, empty reads Bash) and a segmented starts with · contains · regex (for a tool other than Bash only contains · regex, contains chosen) on one row, and the text (its ends trimmed for contains) full width on the row below; its words line follows the tool's noun ("= Bash · a command that starts with make deploy", "= Write · the input contains secrets"), and a text `agents.gate-add` would refuse marks the field with the refusal and disables Create gate. A match the repo has shows a warning line "‹repo› already has Tool call · ‹match›" with an **Open it** link (closes the sheet and opens that gate) and disables Create gate; a new Tool call gate starts on the first free preset. When live sessions of the repo won't carry the gate until their turn ends, a muted line says "N sessions mid-turn pick this gate up when their turn ends" (the repo's sessions mid-turn when it has no Tool call gate yet, else the ones still waiting to be re-attached; a session whose agent can't resume takes the hook only from its next start and isn't counted). The sentence reads "Holds an agent's ‹match› in ‹repo›; ‹graph› decides." ("Holds an agent's git push in shop; Opus review decides."); for a tool other than Bash "Holds an agent's ‹tool› call in ‹repo› when the input ‹contains / matches› ‹text›; ‹graph› decides." ("Holds an agent's Write call in shop when the input contains .env; Fable review decides.", without the "when" part for any input). Edit's Paused line for a Tool call gate reads "Paused: holds no new calls; calls it already holds stay held until their review ends." Edit shows Holds as text and changes Call, Decided by, Rounds and Paused, sending `tool`, `how` and `text` only when the match changed.
- **A held turn in Agents** gets `landing` too, decided here: while its graph reviews (not while the agent works a `changes` round) `{words: "held", tone: "accent", verdict: "reviewing", held: ‹match›, badge: "held · ‹match›", badgeTone: "accent", line: "Held · ‹match› · ‹graph›…", reviewer?}`; while a node of its run asks (also mid follow-up cycle), `words` `asking` with `ask` and `run` and no `line`; while the gate's graph waits for trust (`held.wait`), `words` "waits for trust" in `warning` and `line` "Held · ‹match› · waits for trust"; nothing once its card is out (the row then reads `asking` from its status). The Mac row shows `words`, the header shows `badge` beside the status, and the phone's state line shows `line` when it is set, else `words`. `turnHeld` (true) says a held turn's agent is not working: the review runs, a node asks, the graph waits for trust or the card waits; it is false while the agent works (a `changes` round, a follow-up cycle). The session stays `running`, so the phone shows no "Working…" under a turn with `turnHeld`. With it comes `heldRound` `{text, run?}` (none when the hold's gate is gone): the graph and each round of this hold ("Plan pipeline · round 1 changes · round 2 reviewing", "round N waits for trust" while the graph does), from the same gate history as the Mac's held block, and the gate run; the phone draws it in caption ink where "Working…" was, updated in place, and a tap opens the run. The held turn's transcript ends with an accent card "Gate · ‹graph› · round N", one line per finished round of this hold from the gate's history ("Round 1 · changes · 3 findings") and, while the review runs, "Round N · reviewing" (or "Round N · waits for trust", no button, while the graph waits for trust); each line has Open review (`openHeldReview`) while its verdict node exists in this workspace. A Done run's nodes have no rows, except a node waiting on its own permission (`Round n review · asks you` under the held session).

The phone opens Gates from the "Gates" row at the end of its Agents list and draws the same list (grouped by repo) and detail from the shared SDUI, so Pause/Resume, Delete and the trust card's Trust and run work there; New gate and Edit stay on the Mac, since the phone doesn't draw `sheet`. A gate waiting for trust is the first row of the phone's Gates screen, above How gates work: the needs-you glyph in amber, its name and "Graph changed · N waits" (warning ink; "· N waits" counts what it holds, left out at 0; "Graph can't be read" when it can't be read), and it opens the gate page. A verdict's or the live card's Open review, and Open landing, answer the `ui.event` with `{open: {session}}`, so the phone opens that session's own screen even for a reviewer with no row. It shows the land gate's request card. In its Agents list a session with `hidden` has no row and no count; one with `rowUnder` nests under that lander (in Needs you while it asks, with the shield glyph and its permission line; its Needs you entry and banner read "Round n review · ‹lander›"); a lander's state line is `landing.words` in `landing.tone` (`accent`, `plain` as the secondary ink, `success`, `warning`) except while the lander itself needs you or stopped with an error.

## Landing queue

An agent's landings into one branch of one repo land one at a time, in a stored queue: one queue per (repo common dir, target branch), in the agents plugin storage (`landQueue/<id>`, id = the first 16 hex digits of `sha1(common + "\x00" + into)`), restored at start, so it survives a plugin and a core restart. `agents.land-gate` (from `git.land`, agent callers only) takes the lander's place first, before the gate is looked up, so a repo with no land gate is queued too. The user's own `git.land` and commits are never queued.

| State | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `head` | Its turn: the gate reviews it and `git.land` commits it. |
| `ready` | It called `git_land` and merges cleanly on the target's tip. |
| `waiting` | Its place is behind the head; it conflicts with the tip, or it was passed and has not called `git_land` again. |
| `blocked` | The head waits for uncommitted edits in the target worktree (`why` names the files); a `dirty` entry has this state too. |
| `asks` | An approved head of a session in finish ask waits for the user's yes on its Land card. |

Entries:
- One entry per (session, source); a session can hold several. Mode `branch` squashes a branch, `index` lands a change staged in the target worktree, `dirty` stands for uncommitted files in the target worktree that the head changes too.
- A lone lander is head at once and goes the usual way. Behind the head `git_land` fails with `queued: place N of M into ‹into›; ahead: "‹title›" (‹branch›)[, …]. End your turn; Clubmate prompts you when it is your turn.` (an index entry shows `(staged)`, a dirty one `(edits in ‹files›)`, the user's as `"the user"`). Only the head gets a gate review, and with Review ahead on, entry 2 (below).
- Index rule: an index entry goes before every branch entry, since a staged change in the target stops every branch landing. Only one index entry per target worktree: a second session gets `another session's change is staged in ‹worktree›; wait for it to land, then stage yours` and no place.
- Dirty rule: before the head commits (when it joins and when it gets its turn) the plugin reads `git status` of the target worktree. Files the head's landing changes that are dirty there (staged files of an index entry aside) make a `dirty` entry before the head, and the head is `blocked`. The owner is the open session in that worktree whose recorded edit calls touched the file, else the user. A session owner gets one prompt: `[Clubmate landing queue] Your uncommitted edits in ‹worktree› (‹files›) overlap the queued landing of "‹title›" (‹branch›) into ‹into›, which waits for them. Stage them and call git_land with mode index to land them first, or drop them.`; its index landing takes the dirty entry's place. A user owner makes the queue need you. Dirty files the head does not change do not block.

Turns:
- When the head lands (`agents.land-done`, or a commit on the target whose tree and parent are an entry's), it leaves the queue and the next entry gets its turn: `[Clubmate landing queue] Your turn to land into ‹into›. Its head is now ‹sha›. Call git_land again; rebase only if it reports a conflict.` (a prompt with land priority; a busy session gets it when its turn ends). The prompt is stored before it is sent, so a restart does not send it again.
- When the target moves under the head without its landing (the user's landing or commit, an outside commit), the head gets the same prompt with the new tip. The plugin checks on `git.changed` and every 30 s (`CLUBMATE_AGENTS_QUEUE_LOOP`).
- An entry leaves the queue when its branch is gone, when its branch adds nothing to the tip (it landed some other way, or the agent dropped its commits), and when the same session lands the same branch into another branch of the repo.
- Stuck head: a closed session leaves every queue at once. A head that did not call `git_land` for 15 min (`CLUBMATE_AGENTS_QUEUE_STUCK`) after its turn began, while its turn is not running and its land review is not running (a slow reviewer does not count; the 15 min count from the last time the loop saw the review run), is passed once by the first `ready` entry behind it: it moves behind that entry, `skips` counts up, and it is `waiting` until it calls `git_land` again. A stuck head with no ready entry behind it needs you.
- The user moves (`agents.land-queue-move {queue, entry, to}`), skips (`agents.land-queue-skip`, one place back) and removes (`agents.land-queue-remove`; the session is told not to land it again unless the user asks) entries; these refuse an agent session. A move or skip that puts a branch entry before an index entry is refused (`staged (index) entries stay before branch entries`), and a dirty entry can't be moved, skipped or removed (`a dirty entry stays until its files are committed or dropped`). A new head is prompted.

Edit warning: a session whose edit call (`edit`, `delete`, `move` kinds; the call's locations, diff paths, `file_path`, `notebook_path`) touches a file that a queued entry changes, inside the queue's target worktree (the file's own repo root is the target, so an edit in a session's own worktree never warns), gets one note with the next tool batch (`PostToolBatch`; the plugin writes it to `findings/<call>.queue` in the hook dir, beside the gate findings of that call, and the batch hook prints both): `[Clubmate landing queue] ‹file› is in the queued landing of "‹title›" (‹branch›) into ‹into›. An edit here makes it wait for your change and may make it rebase. Prefer to make this change in your own worktree.` It never blocks. Each (session, file, entry) is told once, and marked told only after its note is written; the told set is the session record's `queueTold` (stored, so a restart does not repeat it; dropped when the entry leaves every queue). A session in a repo with a non-empty queue gets a tool hook dir, as in a tool-gated repo, with no rules; running sessions of the repo are hooked again when an entry joins. Dirty entries (the user's or a session's uncommitted files) give no note.

Finish ask: a top-level session whose Finish is "Ask" (not a child, a graph node or a gate reviewer) gets `[Clubmate finish mode: ask] When the task is done, call git_land with path your worktree (on the target branch itself, stage your change and call it with mode index); Clubmate asks the user before it commits. In a repo whose own instructions land through a pull request, follow them instead. Don't merge, squash or remove worktrees and branches with plain git.` When it is head and its landing is approved by the gate, by docs only, or at once with no gate (not by the user: a Land on the gate's rounds card already is the yes), `git_land` fails with `queued: approved; waits for the user's yes`, the entry is `asks` (needs you) and the session's record gets `queueAsks [{request (lq_…), queue, into, source?, detail, since}]`, which is a Land card on the Mac and the phone: `Land into ‹into›?`, the detail `‹n› commits · review approved · tests pass` (only the parts that hold; `staged` for an index entry, `no land gate` in place of the review parts), the notification `Land into ‹into›? · ‹detail›`, buttons Land ⌥1 and Not yet ⌥2, answered with `agents.land-answer` (user only). Land stores `userYes` on the entry and prompts the session `[Clubmate landing queue] The user said yes to landing ‹branch› into ‹into›. Call git_land again now; Clubmate commits it.`; its next `git_land` commits through the git plugin as usual. Not yet removes the entry and tells the session `… said not yet to landing ‹branch› into ‹into›, so it left the queue. Don't call git_land for it again unless the user asks.` The yes holds only for the same old and tree; a new commit or base asks again. While the head asks, the first `ready` entry behind it passes it at once (no stuck wait); when the target moves under it, the card goes and the head gets its turn prompt again.

Review ahead (setting "Review the next landing ahead", storage `landQueue.speculate`, off by default; depth 1):
- While the head's land review runs or is approved for its current `(old, tree)` (`old` the queue's tip), the entry right behind it (a branch entry that called `git_land`, merges cleanly and was not passed) is reviewed on the predicted base: the plugin makes a synthetic commit `git commit-tree <head's tree> -p <old>` and merges the entry's branch on it (`git merge-tree --write-tree`); a conflict, or nothing to land, starts nothing. The entry's gate reviews `(synthetic, tree)` in the usual way (its gate worktree is `git commit-tree <tree> -p <synthetic>`); its first prompt adds that the base is not the tip yet but the tree it holds after the landing ahead lands. Only the gate's own rules apply (its rounds; a landing whose Land anyway card is open is not reviewed ahead). The plugin starts it when a lander calls `git_land`, every 30 s and when the setting is turned on.
- An approval ahead with no deferred findings is not sent to the lander (its turn prompt comes when it is head), unless the lander's own `git_land` reached the review first (it became head while the review ahead still ran): then the verdict is sent as usual. `changes` is sent at once, so the lander can fix while it waits. The mark and the stored state are set only when the review ahead runs for that exact base and tree (or it is already decided); a busy reviewer or a gate that is not ready leaves them unset, and the plugin tries again later.
- The head lands the predicted tree (`git.land` refuses any other): the entry's `git_land` finds the approval by `(oldTree, tree)` and lands with no new review (logged `by: carried`). The head does not land as predicted (removed, passed, changed, or the target moved): the review ahead goes through the carry rule against the real base, or a full review (`reason: base-moved`).
- The entry's state (`ahead`, the head commit and tree it was made for, its branch tip, the synthetic commit and tree) is stored with the queue, and its land review with the session, so a restart keeps it. While the setting is on and the review ahead still fits the head and the entry's own branch tip, entry 2 carries the mark `ahead` ("Reviewed ahead", see In the Agents tree); turning the setting off hides it at once.

Multi-repo tasks: a task child calls `git_land` only after its task prompted it (the task's landing order and "a failure holds the rest" stay as they are); in a queued repo it then joins the queue like any lander, and the queue's turn prompt replaces a second task prompt for the same landing.

Each landing's journal row (`agents.outcomes` `landing.queueWait`) has the seconds from joining the queue to its turn; the queue keeps its last 8 landed entries for a day (stored), so the wait is there also when the landing was seen before `agents.land-done` came or across a restart.

Fields: `agents.land-queue` → `{queues: [{id, repo, into, needsYou, entries: [{id, session, title, source, mode, state, place, files (≤ 10), more?, overlaps (entry ids that change the same files), why?, skips?, needsYou}]}]}`; `agents.sessions` gives each session with an entry `landQueue {queue, into, place, of, state, why?}`. `needsYou` is decided in the plugin: an `asks` entry, a dirty entry the user owns, a stuck head no entry can pass.

### In the Agents tree

The plugin builds the queue's rows (`plugins/agents/queue_view.go`) and sends them twice: as tree items in the Mac sidebar, and as `landQueues` in `agents.sessions` (the same rows as `agents.land-queue`) for the iPhone and iPad. Clients render the fields and derive nothing. Ruling and boards: `design/features/land-queue/`.

- From 2 entries a group row `Landing into ‹branch›` (glyph `arrow.down.to.line`, the title cut at its end on a narrow row) shows, detail `N in line`, or `1 needs you` / `2 need you` in warning when entries need you. It is the first child of the owner: the oldest open session on the target worktree (not a reviewer, not in line). When the target is the repo's main checkout, or no session is on it, the group is a root row above Closed. A session in line shows once, as its entry row, and goes back to its place when it leaves the line. A dirty entry does not put its session in line: it is a row of its own, and its session (often the owner) keeps its place. The iPhone and iPad keep a session in line in Needs you and Open. The group shows in the All filter only, not while searching.
- One entry: no group. The session keeps its row with the head's word; tooltip `Landing into ‹branch› · alone in line`.
- Each entry row shows `word`, `glyph` and `tone`:

| State | Word | Glyph | Tone | needsYou |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `head`, its land review runs | reviewing | `play.circle` | accent | no |
| `head`, its check (test) step runs | testing | `play.circle` | accent | no |
| `head`, its turn, or approved and committing | landing | `play.circle` | accent | no |
| `head`, its review asked for changes on this tree | failed | `xmark.circle` | danger | no |
| `asks` | asking | `exclamationmark.circle` | warning | yes |
| `blocked`, the user's dirty files | blocked | `minus.circle` | warning | yes |
| `blocked`, another session's dirty files, or the head waiting for them | blocked | `minus.circle` | none | no |
| `ready` | ready | `circle.inset.filled` | none | no |
| `waiting` | waits | `circle` | none | no |

  A session that itself needs you or stopped with an error sends its own status word, glyph and tone in the entry's `word`, `glyph`, `tone` (`asking`, `waiting`, `Stopped`), the same on the Mac and the phone. `why` is the plugin's, else a line per state (`waits for the head to land`, `conflicts with the tip; rebase before its turn`, …). `marks` (`[{id, label, help}]`) holds `carried` (`Review kept`, help `Review kept: ‹branch› moved in other files only`) while the entry's approved review was carried across a base move on its tree, or `ahead` (`Reviewed ahead`, help `Reviewed ahead on the tree ‹branch› holds after the landing before it lands`) on entry 2 while Review ahead is on and its review ahead still fits (see [Landing queue](#landing-queue)). The Mac draws a mark as an outlined badge after the title, its help in the tooltip; on a narrow row the badge (it has no tone) hides before the word would be cut; toned badges, such as the open items count, stay. The phone puts the label first on the `why` line.
- `line` (`3rd of 5 in line · waits`) heads the row menu. `actions` are, in order: Move up, Move down, Skip once, Show changed files, Open session, Remove from line (asks first; the session and its branch stay), each with `enabled`; on the Mac the session's own menu entries follow them. Off: Move up on the head and on an entry that would pass a staged or dirty entry, Move down and Skip once on the last entry and on a staged entry above a branch entry, all of them on a dirty entry and on a head that commits. No keys: Move up / Move down are only in the menu and on iPhone swipe. The Mac menu sends `queueUp`, `queueDown`, `queueSkip`, `queueRemove` (user-only, like the commands), `queueFiles` (unfolds the row) and `queueOpen`; the phone calls `agents.land-queue-move`, `-skip`, `-remove`.
- `fileGroups`: `Shares N files with ‹title›` per overlapping entry first, then `Also changes`, from the first 10 files; `more` counts the rest. On the Mac they show under the unfolded row after the `why` line; on the phone in a sheet. A group title has no glyph; a shared file has the overlap glyph (`doc.on.doc`), another file `doc`.
- iPhone and iPad: a tap on an entry row opens its session (the row has its own selection tag, so the session's other rows don't highlight with it). A section `Landing into ‹branch›` (no glyph, the title cut at its end) with the detail on the right, after Needs you and above the other sections, an entry row per entry (glyph, title, `why` as subtitle, word on the right). The glyph is drawn filled in its tone (warning, accent, danger) like a session row's disc, an outline in caption ink without a tone; the word takes the tone's ink, accent included. Swipe left: Remove from line, Skip once; long press: the full menu under `line`.

Mirror: while a queue has entries the plugin writes `<state>/githooks/queue/<sha1(common)[:16]>/refs/heads/<into>` (written atomically, removed when the queue is empty) for the agent git hook.

## Resource slots

Agents' heavy Bash commands (tests, simulator boots) wait for a slot of their kind before they start, so many sessions do not load the Mac at once. Settings › Agents › Resource limits turns this on (on by default) and holds every value; a change is in effect at once.

**Kinds.** Each kind has an id, a name, `max` slots, a CPU limit (`cpu`, % of all cores, 0 = no check), a free memory floor (`memFreeGB`, 0 = no check), a settle pause (`settle`, seconds) and regular expressions matched on the Bash `command`. Defaults:

| Kind | max | cpu | memFreeGB | settle | in use counts | patterns |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `sim` | 2 | 80 | 4 | 20 s | `simulators` | `\bsim\.sh\s+up\b`, `\bsimctl\s+boot\b`, `\bmake\s+companion-ui-test\b` |
| `test` | `max(1, cores/6)` | 80 | 0 | 20 s | `processes` | `\bmake\s+(test\|test-stress\|lint-tests)\b`, `\bgo\s+test\b`, `\bxcodebuild\b.*\btest\b`, `\bswift\s+test\b`, `\bcargo\s+(test\|nextest)\b` |

`max wait` (default 8 min, 1–60 min) is the longest a call waits. A bad value (a pattern that does not compile, `max` below 1, `cpu` outside 0–100, a negative floor or pause, an id that is not unique) is refused with the reason and the old settings stay. Reset to defaults exists for `test` and `sim`; any kind can be removed.

**Grant rule.** Each kind has one FIFO line. Its first waiter gets a slot when fewer than `max` are in use and, unless none is in use (the first slot is always free), CPU (a 10 s average of the host ticks) is at or below `cpu`, free + inactive memory is at or above `memFreeGB` and `settle` has passed since the kind's last grant. It is checked when a slot frees, when a waiter calls and every 2 s. For `count: simulators`, in use is the larger of the kind's holders and the booted `cm-*` simulators (`xcrun simctl list devices booted`, read outside the line's lock: every 5 s while a simulator kind has a waiter or a holder, else every 30 s); the user's own simulators take no slot, but their memory counts. A `sim.sh up <name>` or `simctl boot <udid>` whose simulator is already booted gets its slot at once. A command that matches more kinds takes them in kind-id order and holds the first while it waits for the next; every request takes them in the same order, so two requests never wait for each other.

**The hook.** While slots are on, every tool hook dir's `hooks.json` has a second `PreToolUse` entry: matcher `Bash`, `'<clubmate cli>' slot hook || true`, timeout `max wait` + 60 s, no `onFailure`. Sessions with no gated or queued repo, night sessions and gate reviewer sessions get `<state>/toolhook/slots/`, which has only that entry. `clubmate slot hook` reads the hook input and calls `agents.slot-acquire` (caller `$CLUBMATE_AGENT_SESSION`) until it gets an answer other than `wait`, and stops with no output once its agent is gone (Claude Code runs the command through a shell, since it holds `|| true`: the hook's parent is that shell and its parent's parent is Claude; when either changes, the agent is gone, and a killed Claude leaves its hook running); each call returns within 20 s. `none` and `granted` print nothing (the call goes on to the permission check with its own input); `denied` prints a deny with the reason. The command is never rewritten: Claude Code checks the user's allow and deny rules against `updatedInput`, so a rewrite would break them. The wait happens before the tool starts, so it does not count toward the Bash timeout. A core or plugin that does not answer for 30 s lets the call run without a slot; a request id keeps the place while the core or the plugin does not answer, as long as the session's agent is the same (a plugin restart reaps the agents, so their requests are dropped, see Release). A changed max wait rewrites the hook dirs; a running session keeps the hook timeout it started with until its next start or resume.

**Release.** A holder is (session, `tool_use_id`). Its slot is freed when the ACP tool call with that id completes or fails, when its session's turn is no longer running, when its session closes or its agent process exits; a call that went to the background holds the slot until its background task ends. A waiter that has not called for 45 s leaves the line (its hook died: the turn was cancelled or the hook timed out), and the slots it held for other kinds are freed. A waiter whose session has no live agent keeps its place but is passed, and so is a waiter that joined under an earlier agent of its session (a turn cut by a restart and continued gets a new agent): only the agent it joined under can be granted, so its slot never goes to a call that nobody runs. Its next call is denied (`this slot request was made before the agent restarted, so it is dropped. Run the command again.`, so a heavy command of an agent that somehow survived does not run outside the limit) and it leaves the line at once; a hook that no longer calls leaves after 45 s. A holder granted before its session's agent last started is freed. Holders and waiters are stored (`slots.state`), so a plugin or core restart keeps the holders whose process still runs (a gate step's command, by pid and start time); waiters are dropped at plugin start (their hooks and steps end with it), and a hook that still calls with one of those requests is denied with the same text, so it can't join again under the new agent; at start, holders of sessions without a live agent are freed (the agents are reaped at plugin start, so their calls ended too). The user can Release a holder in the kind's Edit form (`agents.slot-release`, user only): the process keeps running.

**Denied text.** `[Clubmate slots] no ‹kind› slot in ‹max wait›: ‹n› of ‹max› in use by "‹session titles›"[; ‹why›]. Run it again later, or end your turn and tell the user.` For `sim` it also names the booted `cm-*` simulators: `shut one down that you don't use (scripts/sim.sh down <name>)`.

**Gate test step.** The gate graph's test step takes a `test` slot after its per-repo lock; the holder is the step's command pid with its start time, so a dead or reused pid frees it, also across a restart. While it waits the step's note is `waiting: test slot, ‹why›` (`GateStepOpen.Waiting` is true for any note that starts with `waiting:`).

**What clients show.** `agents.sessions` gives a session with a waiting call `slotWait {kind, place, of, why, words}` and a holder `slotHold {kind, since}`; a waiting session's `landing` is `{words: "waits for ‹kind› slot", line: "waits for ‹kind› slot · ‹why›"}` with no tone and no needs you, so the Mac, iPhone and iPad rows show it from that field. Holding shows nothing on the row. Settings › Agents shows a line per kind (`test · 2 of 3 in use · CPU 64 % · 1 waiting`) and an Edit form under the rows (inline: a modal sheet did not present from the Settings window) with the values, holders and waiters. `agents.slots` (agent tool, read only) and `clubmate slot status` return the same data; `agents.slot-config` reads the settings and, for the user, writes them.

**Not limited.** The user's own terminals and `make` runs; `cm` typed by the user (their load counts toward CPU, nothing more). A command the patterns can't see (a script, `sh -c "$X"`, an alias) runs without a slot: this is not a security boundary, as with Tool call gates. A session started before slots were turned on gets the slot hook through the same detach-and-resume path as Tool call gates (its row says `not limited yet` until then); night and gate reviewer sessions get it from their next start.

## Plain language

Settings → Agents → Plain language (`plain.enabled` in the plugin's storage) is on by default. While it is on, every ordinary session gets one line in its system prompt, after the plugin's own text and before the self's `prompt.md`: "Write and think in ASD-STE100 Simplified Technical English; Russian in УТР (упрощённый технический русский)." The models know both standards, so the line names them and does not list their rules. Children and graph nodes get it too; service sessions (voice, night) don't. The setting is read when a session starts or attaches again; a running session keeps the prompt it has.

## Self-improvement

Once a day Claude reviews its own day, changes how it works and tidies each project's memory. In the morning you get a short report and can undo any change. It is off by default: Settings → Agents → Self-improvement (`self.enabled` in the plugin's storage). While it is off no session gets the self and no night runs. Turning it off during a night cancels the running session and ends the night; what already landed stays. The repository stays too, so turning it back on continues. Once there is a prompt or a skill, a second row appears with Reset (confirmed), which empties the prompt and removes the skills. Reset keeps `lessons.md`, the reports and the memory patches, and does not touch memory.

**The self** is a git repository in the plugin's state dir (`self/`): `prompt.md` (at most 6 KB, appended to the system prompt after the plugin's own text), `skills/<name>/SKILL.md` (at most 5, delivered as the local plugin `self`, so a skill reads `self:<name>`), `lessons.md` (hypotheses and rejected changes, never sent to sessions), `memory-patches/<date>/<workspace>.patch` and `reports/<date>.md`. Every ordinary session gets it, children and graph nodes too; service sessions don't, and it does not make a session a service session. It holds no scripts, hooks or settings. A blank `prompt.md` counts as no prompt. A running session keeps what it has; a change reaches a resumed session the next time it attaches.

**The night** starts at 04:00 local time, on the plugin's own timer. If the Mac slept through it, it runs on the next core start or wake, but only before 08:00; later the night is skipped. The next digest reaches back to the last night that finished without stopping early (`self.digestedUntil`; `self.lastRun` only keeps a night from running twice), so a skipped or failed night loses nothing. `cm command run agents.night-run` starts one now, in the background (`self-improvement is off` while the setting is off, `a night is already running` during one). Each night:

1. Digest. Go collects, per workspace, the sessions updated since then: your prompts, the agent's final replies, and facts from the log (cancelled turns, denied permissions, failed tools by name, repeated Bash commands). Tool output, thoughts and fetched pages are left out. Capped at 8 KB per session and 64 KB per workspace. All digests empty: nothing runs, no session and no report.
2. Sleep. One session per project with a digest and a memory dir, one after another, titled "Sleep: <workspace>". Its sandbox holds that project's digest, memory and undone cleanups and nothing else, and it may read the project's folders. It merges, removes what is stale, checks facts against the code and shrinks. Night sessions have no tool that deletes, so it removes a file by writing it empty: `agents.self-declare` drops empty memory files before it commits, except `MEMORY.md`, since an empty index more likely means a mistake than a cleanup. Memory means `~/.claude/projects/<escaped folder>/memory/` of your workspace folders, or `autoMemoryDirectory` when set. Applying is all or nothing per project: if a memory file changed since the sandbox was made, that project's sleep is dropped and comes back the next night.
3. Improve. One session, titled "Night review", in the most recently opened workspace (Home too, when it is the most recent), after every Sleep. Its sandbox holds a copy of the self and all digests, and no memory. It checks whether a recent change's problem repeated, and reverts that change if so; then it picks at most two patterns that cost you the most and edits `prompt.md` or a skill, with a hypothesis in `lessons.md`. No clear pattern, no edit.
4. Apply. A night session calls `agents.self-declare` after each change, which commits its sandbox. Only declared changes count, and only once the session has ended; a crash or a timeout applies nothing from that session.
5. Report. See below.

**Limits.** While applying, whatever the agent did, on each commit and again on what it landed as, since a merge can change it (renames are off, so an edit can't follow a file): only `prompt.md`, `lessons.md`, `skills/*/SKILL.md` and, for Sleep, memory `.md` files may change; `prompt.md` over 6 KB or more than 5 skills is dropped; a skill is read from the commit and again as it landed, reverts and undo included, and its frontmatter may hold only `name` and `description` (it can have none), and one with shell syntax (`` !` ``, a code fence with `!` in its info string), a substitution (`$` directly before a letter, digit, `_` or `{`, since Claude Code substitutes `$ARGUMENTS` first and the parts then join into shell syntax) or a control or invisible character anywhere (a bare `\r`, any Unicode space but the plain one, a BOM, zero-width characters, invalid UTF-8; `\t` and `\r\n` are fine) is dropped, because Claude Code honours `allowed-tools` and `hooks` there and runs that syntax; a commit that removes a `Rejected by the user:` or `Undone from session` line from `lessons.md` is dropped; at most two commits touch the prompt and skills per night (a revert doesn't count and is applied first). What is dropped is listed in the report. While running, a night session is confined by the plugin: it starts in `auto` and is set to `default` before the first prompt, and it refuses the turn if that fails. It has only Read, Grep, Glob, Edit and Write, Sonnet, 60 turns and no Claude Code settings. Its allowlist covers edits in the sandbox, reads in the sandbox and (Sleep) the project's folders, and `agents.self-declare`; every other permission request is rejected at once, so nothing waits for you, and a form or URL question is declined too. Rejected requests aren't logged, but the report counts them as `Refused: N`, so an escape attempt, or a rule that stopped matching, can't pass for a quiet night. A sandbox holds no `.git`: the git dir sits beside it and Go names it explicitly, so a session can't plant a hook or a config. Each session has 30 minutes (`CLUBMATE_AGENTS_NIGHT_SESSION_LIMIT`) and the night 60 (`CLUBMATE_AGENTS_NIGHT_LIMIT`). A session at its limit ends only that session; a failure, the night's limit or turning the setting off ends the night, which then notifies you, with a report when something landed. A restart mid-night does the same on the next start: it lists what landed since the night began and saves the report, with undo from any chat, since the Improve session is gone. `prompt.md` must hold behaviour only, never a project name, path, code or secret: the Improve session sees all digests, so this rule is a prompt instruction plus the diff in the report.

**Report.** The plugin writes it after Apply, since only it knows what landed: "Night review" in the Improve session, at most 10 lines plus the diffs of `prompt.md` and skills, saved as `reports/<date>.md`. What was refused, dropped or skipped comes first, at most 3 lines so it can't push out what changed, and a diff over 4 KB ends with how much was cut. A night with nothing to change says so in one line. The notification (`Night review`, with "2 changes", "Nothing to change", or "Nothing landed, see the report" when something was dropped or skipped and nothing landed) reaches the phone like any other. Each change is an open offer labelled "Undo: …", at most 4: one per self edit, one for a revert, one for all of the night's memory cleanup. The card differs from other open items: picks are undone at once with no agent turn (it says "Undo happens at once, with no turn"), and it has no To backlog, since a night session's offers never go to the backlog.

**Undo** has no deadline. `agents.self-changes` lists the changes, newest first, and `agents.self-undo {change}` reverts one (a git revert, or the reverse patch for memory). Both work from any session, so "undo yesterday's change about X" works in any chat; only a night session is refused. An undo that no longer applies says so and changes nothing, and so does one that would leave an unsafe skill; undoing the newer change first gets past that. Every undo records in `lessons.md` who undid it, `Rejected by the user: <summary>` or, when an agent called it, `Undone from session <id>: <summary>`, so Improve doesn't redo it. Sleep never sees `lessons.md`: a project's undone memory changes (the newest 10, each with its summary and patch) reach that project's Sleep sandbox, and only that one, as `rejected.md`, which stays out of what it can declare.

**Night sessions.** Each is a [service session](#service-sessions) that counts as `done` and never under Needs you, even with open offers. Sleep is closed once it is applied, so it moves from Service to Closed; the Improve session stays with its sandbox until the next night that isn't skipped, so you can ask it why it did something. It resumes in `default` or not at all, its mode and settings can't be changed, and it can't be handed off: a hand-off would be an ordinary session in a sandbox that holds every digest. That next night deletes the previous night's sessions and sandboxes, with any unanswered offers; undo still works through chat and `reports/` keeps the report. A night session is hidden from `agents.search`, `agents.sessions`, `agents.transcript`, `agents.wait` and `agents.export` for agent callers other than the night session itself, so an ordinary session can't read a digest; you see them in the sidebar as usual. Claude Code's own logs of a night session hold the digests, so `~/.claude/projects/<escaped sandbox>` goes with that sandbox, for its path as is and with symlinks resolved, and nothing else under `projects` is touched.

## Permission card

A pending permission request shows the same content on the Mac and the phone; the agents plugin builds it once: the Mac gets it as the `permissionCard` node ([SDUI](sdui-protocol.md#node-types)), the phone as `card` `{text, title, command?, caption?, options, note?}` on each permission entry of `agents.transcript` (left out for agent callers), with the same option fields:

- Title by tool kind: "Run a command?" for a command; other kinds keep the sentence "Claude wants to edit notify.go".
- The command itself (`command`), in a monospaced box.
- The request's description as the caption, always attributed: "Claude says: Run the notify tests".
- `note`, one caption line under the buttons, only on the first request on this Mac while it waits ([First run hints](#first-run-hints)).
- Options in this order: Allow once ⌥1 · Always allow… ⌥2 · Reject ⌥3; "Always reject" (Claude's `reject_always`) sits in the card's ••• menu without a key. These labels replace Claude's own ("Yes", "Yes, and don't ask again for go test commands", "No") only when the request has the usual one option per kind; anything else (plan-mode exits, several allow options) keeps Claude's labels and gets ⌥1–3 in order.
- Always allow… asks first and stores nothing until confirmed: the option carries the `rule` Claude would store and its `scope` ("in shop on this Mac", rules go to the project's local Claude settings on this Mac). The Mac shows them in a popover anchored to the button ("Claude will run matching commands in **shop** without asking, on this Mac, until you remove the rule from the allow list.", the name from the option's `workspace`, bold and never broken at a hyphen; Cancel ⎋ is the default and ↩ cancels too, ⌥2 again confirms) and the phone in its confirm sheet; only the confirm sends the option. Claude does not send the rule over ACP, only its label, so the plugin rebuilds it from that label for commands (`Bash(go test:*)`, one rule per command prefix Claude names); when the label names no command (paths, "similar" commands, other tools) there is no `rule` and the confirm shows the option's label instead.

## Names in the UI

What the agents plugin renders uses names, not ids or command names: a session is its title, a graph run its graph's title; prompts Clubmate sends an agent ("[Clubmate] Child "Fix tests" (agt_…) is done.", the handoff summary) keep the ids for the agent but show without them in the transcript. A detached worktree is labelled "detached · 03a9ff5f". Themed UI is in sentence case ("New in worktree…"); Title Case only in context menus.

## Multi-repo tasks on the phone

The phone draws a task from what the plugin sends and decides nothing about it.

- `agents.sessions` adds, on a session with a task or a connect request: `requests` (the ids waiting for an answer, also while the turn runs, when `pending` doesn't list them yet; a waiting [land gate](#gates) request too, on any session, so the phone docks its card instead of "Answer on the Mac"), `taskRows` `[{name, path, session?, word?, tone?, connect?}]` in landing order, `taskBadge` ("1 to land", "1 PR open"), `archivable` and `taskFinished`. As in the Mac's tree, an open task's children show until you fold it, children that need you or failed and a repo waiting to connect stay pinned, and a finished task folds the first time you open it; this also holds for a session with no task while a connect request waits. A row's `word` is the repo's state; it is left out while its session runs, so the phone shows that session's own line, and a repo with no folder yet reads "not connected yet". `pendingSummary` of kind `connect` adds `lead` and `subject`: "Wants to connect" and the repo or "3 repos". With neither a task nor a request none of these fields is sent.
- `agents.transcript` adds `request`, the `request.connectRepo` or `request.land` node, and `repos`, the `repos` node. It is the Mac's card built with no choices of the Mac's: nothing is under the cursor, the failed rows are open, and it is unfolded while a Land request waits. Rows carry `path` and `session`, and every row that has buttons sends them, open or not, as the node's stack children, read by their `action`, since the phone folds and opens a row itself.
- An AFK notice entry adds `afk` `{detail, remove?}`: the line under the title and "Remove from ads…", which is left out once the folder is gone.
- `agents.land-commits {request}` lists what a Land request would land, up to the branch's live tip: `{commits: [{sha, title}], more, tip, detail}`, at most 50, `detail` the card's line with the live count. Merge stays off until the list has loaded. The phone sends `tip` to `agents.land-answer`, which refuses a merge when the branch moved; the Mac sends none.
- `agents.connect-answer` takes `seen` from the card and `agents.archive` takes `seen` from the plan. Each refuses, changing nothing, when what the user saw changed: the repos still waiting, or a repo's worktree or branch step, what would be lost, the sessions, the holds and the Cron jobs. A client with no `seen` isn't checked; the Mac sends none, since its sheet re-reads the plan itself. The archive plan adds `cronLines`, one line per job that pauses, `cronWarn` when Cron couldn't be read, and `held`. Both commands take `key`: a resend with the same key returns the first success for 7 days, also after a plugin restart, and a run a restart cut off answers that its outcome is unknown. A failure isn't kept. `anyway` and `folder` on `agents.archive` are refused through a plugin: those choices stay on the Mac.

An offline Mac: Connect and Archive queue on the phone, persisted, each with the request or plan's `seen` and a key, and are tried on reconnect and on every later event, so a plugin that comes back or a Mac that gains the command sends them; Undo works until the send starts, and they are listed on the Macs screen too, which needs no session list. A send whose answer is lost goes back in the queue and resends with the same key. A refusal is read from its machine code, never its words: the plugin marks `reason` `stale` (the request or plan moved on, or the workspace or session is gone), `answered` or `unknown` on the error, and the `remote` plugin sends it as the error's `code`. A `stale` or `answered` one drops the item with a note, and one already answered is never sent. A `refused` error (the plugin's own refusal) stops it at once, listed with the error until dismissed. Any other error keeps it and backs off, 30 seconds and doubling up to 30 minutes; after 8 tries the item stops and stays listed with the error until dismissed. Once a send has started the item offers no Undo; on a Mac without request ids (`hello.requests`) it reads "may have run" (unless no address of the Mac could be reached at all, so the request never left the phone: then it goes back undoable), and queueing the same answer can't replace it, so its key survives. When an earlier try got no answer and the resend is refused, or the code is `unknown`, the note says the outcome is unknown, not that it wasn't sent. A Mac too old for the command gets one note, and the item waits. One archive runs at a time per task; a second is refused while the first runs. Merge, Ask it to fix, Leave it unlanded… and Remove from ads… are disabled offline and never queue: a send that loses the link fails on the screen instead of waiting in the queue. Every other phone write goes through the same outbox, see [remote](remote.md#phone-outbox).

## Transcript folding

Tool calls are folded on the Mac and the phone: consecutive calls are one actions row, and a call's output opens only when you click or tap it. A completed `agents_open_items` call is not drawn on either client, since the card says the same: the plugin leaves it out of the `transcript` node and sends `hidden: true` on the entry in `agents.transcript`, where the phone skips it. It isn't counted in an actions row and a row left with no calls isn't drawn. An agent reading `agents.transcript` still gets the call. Running, failed and cancelled ones show like any other call. On an iPad or Fold wider than a phone the actions row is a card that names its first three calls ("Show all N" for the rest), as the iPad board draws it; each call's output still opens only on tap. A failed call is folded too: on the Mac its card says Failed and the actions row "N failed", on the phone its title is in the danger colour and its card or group gets a danger border. A user message longer than 40 lines or 4000 characters shows its first 12 lines, at most 1200 characters, with "Show all N lines" ("Show all" when it has no more than 12 lines; "Show less" folds it again); opened, it scrolls inside a capped box, since one row that tall leaves the transcript scrolled past its end, blank. A prompt the voice plugin sent (an entry with `voice {heard, utterance}` in `agents.transcript`) is one right-aligned capsule "🎙 <heard>" (one line, tail-cut) with a chevron; clicking or tapping it opens the prompt JSON, pretty-printed with sorted keys, in a scroll box at most 480 pt tall. A Review prompt (an entry with `review` in `agents.transcript`, sent by the review's Send) is a Review card on the Mac: `Review`, `you · N/M files · K comments`, "Open review" while the review is open, one row per comment (type glyph, `file:line`, the first line of the text, `open` or `resolved` as nrev has it now, or had it when the review closed) and the note; on the phone it stays a plain user message with the prompt text, folded like any long one. A plan card is headed "Plan · n of m" (n steps done); while steps remain its done steps are folded behind "Show done" ("Hide done" folds them again), and a finished plan lists every step. Nothing in the transcript opens itself, with two exceptions. The design boards say failures expand; that is overruled. The first exception is a thought that stands in for a reply: a "reply segment" runs from the turn's start, or from the end of the previous segment in that turn, up to a question point (an AskUserQuestion call, which is an `elicitation` entry or a tool call named `AskUserQuestion`, or the end of a finished turn; a turn still running has no end point, so nothing opens and closes while it streams). When a segment has no agent message with text, the plugin sends `open: true` on its last thought with text (the `open` prop of the `message` node, `open` on the entry in `agents.transcript`) and both clients draw that thought unfolded, because some models write what they mean for you into a thought and then ask; you can still fold it, and every other thought stays folded. A change in `open` moves the entry in the transcript cursor like any derived state. The second exception is the [Repos card](#repos-card), which is no transcript entry but a live card the plugin owns: its failed row opens by itself, the card itself opens for a failure or a waiting Land request on both clients, which overrules `MR-Merge-*`, where it is drawn folded, and the plugin decides both and sends `open`, `collapsed` and `foldKey`, so the phone renders those fields and works nothing out. A closed ```` ```mermaid ```` fence in an agent message is drawn as a diagram (one hidden web view per app renders it to an image, cached by source, theme and width); it keeps its aspect and is at most 480 pt tall on the Mac and 420 pt on the phone, shows its source as a code block while it renders, and stays a code block when mermaid cannot parse it, when it would be over 16384 px on a side or 24 million pixels even at 1×, or while the fence is still open. Clicking it on the Mac opens a sheet with the diagram fitted to the window (pinch, zoom buttons, Esc closes); tapping it on the phone opens it full screen (pinch, double tap to zoom). A Markdown link or code span in an agent message that names a doc (see § Docs in the transcript, `docLinks`) is drawn as a link in the accent colour (a code span keeps its code background) on the Mac and the phone; clicking or tapping it opens the doc, whether or not the doc also has a card. Under the message text each of its `docCards` with `presented` is a card (the same on both clients): a kind icon on an accent-tinted tile, the title on one line (tail-cut), "Document · <rel>", "Diagram · <rel>" or "Artifact · <rel>" under it (middle-cut) and a trailing open glyph; the whole card opens the doc. On the Mac the cards are at most 27 em wide (324 pt at the default 12 pt interface font) and sit side by side while each gets 20 em, else one per row at the full width; the kind word stays and the path is cut in the middle; on the phone they are one per row at the full width. The message's other `docCards` (docs it only mentions) are folded under the presented cards into one muted plate: a docs icon, "N docs mentioned" (the file name when there is one) and a chevron; clicking or tapping it opens the same cards under it, laid out like the presented ones, and folds them again. Folded is the default; on the Mac the state is kept per message until the app quits, on the phone while the transcript is open, and never saved. A change to this rule lands in both clients in the same commit.


A graph node's reply that goes on to the next node is folded on the Mac, the iPhone and the iPad: in a session of a graph run, the last agent message of a turn that an edge or the run started is one row "→ ‹next node role› · ‹first line›" (muted, a chevron, tail-cut), and a click or tap opens the whole reply under it. A turn the user started (a prompt from the Mac, the phone, the voice plugin or `cm`) is never folded, nor is a reply while its turn runs, nor the reply of a turn that ended in an error or was stopped (its error edge, not the next node, gets it). The plugin decides it: the turn's prompt in the session log records `relayed` when its prompt was not the user's word (a trigger, the graph engine or another agent sent it; queued prompts sent as one turn are relayed only when none of them is the user's), the session's `run.next` lists the roles of the nodes its done, asks and outcome edges lead to (the graph engine sends it as `runNext` to `agents.new`), and the reply's entry gets `handoff {to}` in `agents.transcript` and the `message` node in `agents.main` (`to` is the roles, comma-separated). A node with no next node, and a session started before `run.next` existed, gets no mark. One fold state per message id serves the click and the keys (`MessageExpansion` on the Mac, `FoldStore` on the phone); copy (y) gives the whole reply.
The keys fold what a click or tap folds, on the Mac, the iPhone and the iPad with a keyboard (`docs/shortcuts.md` § Agent transcript): l / → unfolds the focused block (a thought, an action, a long or voice prompt, a plan's done steps, an actions group), h / ← folds it, ↩ toggles it; from an action inside an unfolded group, h folds the group. The answer and the cards do not fold. One fold state per block id serves the click and the keys: on the Mac `ToolExpansion` (actions and groups) and `MessageExpansion` (prompts, plans and thoughts; a thought starts from its `open`), on the phone `FoldStore` (every kind, a thought also from its `open`, kept per session). Both keep it while the app runs and never save it; the state of a thought (Mac) or of any block (phone) is dropped when the block leaves the session's transcript, as `ToolExpansion` drops actions. On the iPad layout an actions group always lists its first three calls, so its fold is "Show all N", and its calls are blocks of their own.

A call a Tool call gate holds is never folded, on the Mac or the phone, also when a subagent made it. The plugin decides the block from the tool entry's `gate` mark and the session's holds and sends it as the entry's `gate {state, tone, title, caption, tool, input, lines: [{text, state, round, review}], note}` in `agents.transcript` (a subagent's held calls come as `heldCalls` on its `agent` entry, each with its `gate`, and the `agent` entry itself gets `gate {state: "held", word: "held · ‹match›"}`, "held · ‹first match› +N" for more, which is the folded subagent row's detail on both clients; on the Mac it is the subagent card's `statusWord`); the Mac gets it as a `card` in place of the call's `toolCard`, in the block's `tone` (`accent` while held, `neutral` once refused: muted shield, plain border), and a subagent's held call as a card right under the subagent's line. Each gate line's text and state are two fields; on the Mac the line is a `stack` with `twoRows`: "‹text› ‹state› … Open review" on one row while it fits (1280), else the text, then "‹state› · Open review" indented under it (640); on the phone each gate line is a surface-2 row of at least 44 pt that opens the gate's review (a chevron at its end), its state beside the text while it fits, else under it. One gate: "Held by a gate", "‹repo› · ‹match› · round N of M" (a call held to be safe shows the hold's caption, "git push · inside sh -c, held to be safe", in place of the match), Open review while the gate's verdict node runs, the call (tool, input), "‹graph› → approved / changes" with its state ("reviewing · ‹elapsed›", "waits for trust", "asking") and "Claude waits inside this call. …". Several gates: "Held by N gates", "‹repo› · runs when both approve" ("all" for more), one line per gate in command order "‹match› · ‹graph› · round N of M" with its state ("approved · waits for ‹the other match›", "approved · waits for ‹match› +1" while two or more still review; a gate's approval of a call that still waits is kept only in memory, so after a plugin restart its line reads as reviewing again until every gate has decided) and Open review; with three or more gates the sentence reads "If any gate asks for changes, the refusal waits for the others; then all their findings follow it in one message.". After `changes` the same block stays, neutral, titled "Refused by the gate · round N · changes" ("Refused by N gates · changes" when several said so), without the sentence; with several gates the caption is "‹repo› · ‹m1› · ‹m2›" and each gate keeps a line "‹match› · round N of M" (with the graph while it is known) and its state `changes` or `approved` once the gate's message is there; a call held to be safe keeps the hold's caption also after the message. It isn't a failed call and isn't counted in "N failed". The gate's message (the `gateMessage {groups, tail, call}` entry; on a Tool call gate's message each group carries `gate`, the gate's id, so the refused block matches each gate line's state by gate) is drawn directly under the block of the call it names, also when it was queued and started the next turn: it is moved under the block and no turn break is drawn between them; a subagent's stays in its fold. A Done gate's message carries `gateMessage` too (one group, no `call`) and is drawn where it stands; on the Mac both are one bubble with a group per gate (the title in the message's `titleTone`, `caption`: muted caption ink, findings as bullets, "Not blocking:" items, the tail in caption ink). Two Done gate prompts sent as one queued message stay two groups. A call refused because no turn was open (ruling 15) stays an ordinary call whose status word reads "refused" (`statusWord` on the card, `gate {state: "refused", word: "refused", caption}` on the entry), not "Failed", and isn't counted in "N failed"; a caption line in caption ink under it reads "‹repo› · ‹match› · refused at once: gates hold only calls made inside a turn" (`caption`; on the Mac a muted `text` right after the call's card), while "run it from the session's turn" stays in the agent's message only. The Mac composer reads "Held by a gate — messages queue" while a call is held (`landing.hint`); the phone keeps its placeholder and shows `landing.phoneHint` "Messages queue while it's held" above the composer in caption ink, its session header reads `landing.header` ("Held by a gate · ‹branch›", "Held by 2 gates · ‹branch›"; the branch is read when the hold starts and again when a stored hold resumes after a restart, so the sidebar build never runs git for it) with `landing.headerAccent` ("Held") in accent, and it draws no "Working…" under a held call while `callHeld` is true on the session. A held session's row (Mac tree and phone list) draws `landing.glyph` ("shield") in `landing.glyphTone` (accent) in place of the status glyph, for a held call and a held Done turn, not while the hold asks for you. The rounds card's Stop option carries `tone: "danger"`. The phone's SDUI transcript gets the same nodes. Its native transcript draws the block from the entry's `gate` (a `title` makes it a block) as an accent-bordered card in place of the call, never in an actions row: the shield and the title, the caption under it, the call (tool, input in mono, at most 3 lines; shown when either is set), one row per gate (the surface-2 row above: its text, its state in accent; a tap opens the reviewer's session while the line has `review`) and the note. A subagent's `heldCalls` are such cards right under the subagent's row. The refused block is the same card without the gate lines and the note, and `failed` on it or on a no-turn refusal doesn't put the danger colour on the call or its actions row; a no-turn refusal shows the entry's `word` ("refused") in place of the status glyph. A `gateMessage` entry whose `call` names a call in the transcript is drawn right after that call's block, wherever the plugin listed it; the phone draws no turn breaks. The message is one bubble on the right, a group per gate (its `title`, which the plugin adds to each of `gateMessage.groups` in `agents.transcript`, in the message's `titleTone` (`caption`), findings as "·" lines, "Not blocking:" items, the tail in caption ink). The composer keeps its placeholder and shows `landing.phoneHint` above it, and the session row's line is `landing.line`.

## Turn end line

Every ended turn closes with one muted line in the Mac transcript: "Turn N · checkpoint saved · K files changed". "checkpoint saved" shows when the turn has a checkpoint (`turns[].checkpointSha`), "K files changed" counts the distinct paths that the turn's completed edit calls wrote, and a stopped turn adds its stop note ("Turn 2 · checkpoint saved · Stopped"). `agents.transcript` marks such a turn with `turns[].ended`. The phone does not draw the line yet; backlog #1211 adds it there.

## Transcript cursor

`agents.transcript` returns a `cursor`; passed back as `since` it gets only the entries added or changed since then (`delta: true`). The cursor is the session log's `seq` (every log line carries one, kept across restarts), so it stays valid when the plugin restarts or unloads an idle transcript. Each entry carries the seq of the last log line that changed it: while the log is replayed, a line stamps the entries it touches with its own seq, and a streaming message not yet written (chunks are coalesced for 2 s) carries the seq its line will get. A second number orders changes in the same seq within one plugin run; a cursor from an earlier run is read with that number at 0, so the entries changed by its last line come back once more.

State the transcript derives without a log line moves an entry too, never leaving a cached copy stale:
- A tool call or compaction shown cancelled because the turn ended or the process is gone, open-item markers and numbers in agent messages: a change is stamped with the log's current seq when a read sees it. After a load (a restart, or an idle transcript read back) every entry whose look depends on that (an open tool call or a running compaction, an agent message in a turn with open items, an open items card) is stamped again, so a phone with a cursor from before gets it once.
- A thought's `open` flag changes when its segment is closed (a question arrives, the turn ends): the read that sees it stamps the entry with the log's current seq.
- Task links: the first backlog titles after a restart stamp every entry that names a task, and a load stamps every entry with task or doc links or images again (their titles and cards are read outside the log).
- The permission card and the AFK repo card are built per reply from the workspace's name, its folders, the session's task repos and the Mac's time zone; their hash is the first half of the cursor's last part, and when it differs from the one in `since`, every permission entry and repo notice comes back. The second half hashes the session's call holds and their states (it changes each minute while a review runs, for "reviewing · N min"); when only it differs, only the gate-marked calls and the agent entries with held calls come back.

A cursor the plugin can't read (an older format, or past the end of the log) gets the whole transcript without `delta`.

Every reply carries `headerRev`, a hash of the JSON of `modes`, `configOptions`, `commands` and `turns` (the same after an unload or a restart while they don't change). A `delta: true` reply to a call whose `headerRev` equals it leaves those four out and adds `kept: ["modes", "configOptions", "commands", "turns"]`; the caller keeps its own copy of each key named in `kept` and takes every other key from the reply (absent means gone). Any reply that isn't a delta (no `since`, a cursor the plugin can't read, `last`) sends all four. Only the phone sends `headerRev`; without it the reply is unchanged. An empty poll of a running session drops from about 6–18 KB to about 0.7 KB (the rest is `session` and `usage`).

`slim: true` leaves tool calls' `content` and `rawInput` out (`rawInput` keeps `command` and `description`) and adds `toolSummary {diffs: [{path, added, removed}], output}`: what the folded row shows, with the line counts of the phone's line diff. `agents.tool-call {session, tool}` returns one call's fields in full. The phone sends `slim` only to a Mac that lists `agents.tool-call` ([remote](remote.md)).

## Long transcripts

The Mac and the phone draw only the last 60 rows of a transcript, so a long session does not lay out a thousand rows on every update. A row is what the transcript shows as one line or card: a message, a plan, a card, and a run of adjacent tool calls (one "N actions" row); a window never starts inside a run, and on the phone never between a plan and the tool calls it pairs with. Above them a "Show 100 earlier · N left" row (the last page reads "Show N earlier") draws the next 100 in place and the transcript lands on the first row it added. Rows once drawn stay drawn while the session grows, until 100 more rows have come in below them: then the window goes back to the last 60 (on the phone only while you are at the bottom; the Mac's plugin cannot see the scroll position, so there it happens at the next render unless the transcript holds an `anchor`, i.e. until the next turn after a tap or a jump). A reload that returns fewer rows than were hidden shows the last 60. A new row scrolls the transcript to its end only while you are at the bottom, or when it is your own message; while you read earlier rows the view stays put, on the Mac and the phone.

The plugin decides the Mac's window and the phone's SDUI transcript once: the `transcript` node holds only the rows shown plus a `button` (`<session>/earlier`, action `showEarlier`) with the label, and the plugin keeps the first row shown per session by its entry, not by count. A tap moves it 100 rows earlier and sets `anchor` to the first row added; `agents.reveal` to a turn, the open items card and a jump back to a side thread's origin in its parent widen it to their row. The render is also capped below the node limit (see [SDUI protocol](sdui-protocol.md)): when the rows shown would exceed it the oldest are dropped and counted in "N left". The phone's native session screen applies the same rule to the rows of `agents.transcript` (`TranscriptWindow` in CompanionKit), keeping its window until you leave the session. It draws only the last 10 rows of the window when the session opens; when you scroll up to the first drawn row it draws 10 more above it and keeps the rows under your finger in place (it moves the view by the height added above that row, so a reply that streams below does not move it). A scroll that reaches the top without a drag (a tap on the status bar, VoiceOver) draws all the rows of the window. The "Show 100 earlier" row shows once all the rows of the window are drawn, so its count is the rows above the window; it draws all the rows it adds. A drawn edge whose first row changes its id (a row that joins a run of tool calls) stays at the same place. It parses each message's Markdown once (`MarkdownCache`).

The phone keeps the transcripts and loaded images of the last four sessions it left (a session that needs you keeps its transcript longer). Opened again, such a session shows the kept copy at once and asks only for what changed since; while that request takes longer than 0.3 s, a quiet "Updating…" line with a small spinner sits under the last row (not while the session runs: "Working…" is there), so a stale tail never looks final. The decision is the store's (`AgentsStore.refreshing`); the screen only draws it. A first open with nothing kept shows the centred spinner instead.

## Chat panel

The agents plugin claims `editor-nvim.chat` (Code) and `wiki.chat` (Wiki) and renders one tree for both; the two differ only in what the plugin knows about the view (`plugins/agents/chat_panel.go`). The design is ruled in `design/features/chat-panel/rules.md`.

**Context.** Code: the workspace's current worktree (`code:<root>`), the file open in Neovim (`editor-nvim.file`) and the review tab current in that worktree (`editor-nvim.review.current`, kept per worktree, so a tab change in another worktree does not drop it; with base, head, opener and Send recipient from `editor-nvim.review`). Wiki: the workspace (`wiki`) and the page shown (`wiki.page`). The plugin stores per context the session last picked and whether the picker shows, `{session, picking}` under `chat/<workspace>`, so both survive a plugin restart; the old Code binding `code/<workspace>` (worktree → session) is read once into it.

**Opening.** The client sends `{slot: "<section>.chat", nodeId: "panel", action: "open"}` when the user opens the panel (⌘B, the palette, the iPad Chat button); the plugin then shows the picker for that context. The Mac adds `value: {"focus": true}`, and the plugin then focuses the slot (`ui.focus`), so the picker list has focus also when ⌘B came from Neovim; the iPad sends no value and gets no focus, since a `ui.focus` would also show and focus the panel on the Mac. A slot that shows for any other reason (launch restore, a phone watching, a section switch) is not an open: it shows the stored state. The picker also shows while nothing is picked or the picked session is closed or gone, so closing the session you chat with brings the picker back, not an empty pane.

**Picker.** A `keys` scope `chat/picker` (`n` sends `chatNew`) around the picker header, a `list` `chat/sessions` (action `chatPick`, value the session id) and the foot. The header is a `bar` stack (`inset: bar`): `Chat` (strong), `· pick a session` (muted) and the hide button `chat/hide` (ghost, icon only, hint ⌘B, `clientAction: hideChat`: the client hides the panel; no event); no count. On the Mac the one-time Wiki hint shows under it: "⌘B opens chat here now. The page tree moved to ⌃⌘S." with a ghost Got it, the first 3 times ⌘B opens the panel in Wiki, gone for good on this Mac after Got it, the first ⌃⌘S in Wiki or the third showing (kept in the app's defaults, not per workspace); ↩, j/k and N still act on the list. The list holds every open session of the workspace except service sessions (restricted ones, graph steps and gate reviewers, which stay in Agents' Service group; one is listed only when it is the suggestion), newest activity first: a section row `Suggested` with the suggestion, then a section row `Open in ‹workspace›` with the count of the rest. A row is the status icon, the title and a caption: the worktree's folder name when it is not the panel's worktree (in Wiki: not the workspace's first git folder) · the age; on the suggestion the reason · the age, in the short form (`now`, `1m`, `2h`, `3d`, then the date). A session that needs you ([Needs you](#needs-you)) has the amber icon and a `needs you` badge.
