The list's `focus` is the suggestion (↩ takes it) and its `filter` is `Filter sessions` from 10 rows (`/` focuses it). Under the list, after a divider, the foot (`inset: foot`): the secondary button `chat/new` full width (`fill`, hint `n` as a key cap) and a muted caption of what the new session sees.

**Suggestion** (the plugin's `suggestChat`): in Code with a review tab current in the panel's worktree, the review's opener (`opened this review`), else its Send recipient (`gets this review's comments`); then the session last picked for this context (`last used here`); then the newest open session that is not a service session (`newest`). Closed sessions are never suggested or listed; they stay in Agents.

**New session** starts in the panel's worktree (Wiki: the workspace's first git folder, as `agents.new` does), becomes the context's pick and gets focus. The caption is one string the plugin builds, the same on the iPad: `Starts on ‹branch›` and one ending — in Code with a review current ` with this review` (the button reads `New session for this review`), with a file open ` and sees ‹file›` (the base name); in Wiki with a page ` and sees the page ‹title›`; else none. ‹branch› is the worktree's branch; no commit ids. With a review current, after the start the plugin runs `editor-nvim.review {base, head, cwd}` as the new session, which makes it the review's opener, so Send goes to it. When the review tab closes the button goes back to `New session`. Without open sessions the picker is the same header over an `empty` node: the icon and `No open sessions in ‹workspace›`, the sentence `A new session ` + the caption with a lower-case s + `.`, New session as its one primary button (label-sized, `N` as a key cap) and the `note` `Closed sessions stay in Agents` with `noteSection: agents`, so the client adds the key its rail gives Agents (`⌘3`).

**After the pick** the panel shows the session as in Agents, with a one-row `bar` header (the picker header's bar, so the hide button stays put): a ghost `chat/back` drawn `‹ ⌘[` (`iconOnly` + `keyCap`; `All sessions` is its tooltip and accessibility label; ⌘[, action `chatBack`, back to the picker), a 1 × 16 vertical rule, the status icon, the title (strong, one line, cut at the end, the whole title as tooltip) and, when the session is not in the panel's worktree, that worktree's folder name, then the session's buttons and the hide button last; there is no session select and no Context button at any time. A pick never moves the editor to the session's worktree. What the session sees shows as context chips first in the composer's `attachments` (`context: true`, `removable: true`, ids `ctx/file`, `ctx/review`, `ctx/page`): the file open in Neovim (its base name, the path as tooltip) and `Review ‹head branch›` (never a commit), only for a session in the panel's worktree; in Wiki `‹title› page`, always. The composer's `context` (`Context`, or `Transcript` while the detail shows) puts one ⌘I key cap at the end of the chip row on the Mac, or a dashed muted `Context ⌘I` chip when there are no chips; the iPad draws a dashed `Context`/`Transcript` chip at the row's end in both cases, without a key cap. ⌘I or a click on it toggles the context detail, so the detail keeps a way back also after × on the last chip. On the iPad the picker header is the panel's own "Chat" header with its close button, and the tree's hide button is not drawn. A click on a chip's label opens the detail at that chip: the detail's first group, `Next message`, opens and lists the chips with what goes with the message (the review's base, head and worktree; the page; the file's path); before Claude has written a log the detail shows that line over the group. × (`detach` with the chip id) leaves a chip out of the next message. On Send the chips go with the message: the file as a file attachment, the review and the page as a line of text, which the sent message shows again as its review or page chip. A session with no turns shows the starters; an unstarted graph step shows its step card with New chat (`chatNew`).

## Sending a message

A message you send shows in the transcript at once, dimmed (60 %, "Sending…" on hover) until the agent has it. On the Mac the composer empties on Return and the message is an echo at the end of the transcript until the plugin renders the session again after that send (the composer's node id ends in a counter the plugin bumps on each accepted send); a refused send drops the echo and puts the text back in the composer, with the error in the banner, and text typed meanwhile moves to the new composer. From then on the plugin draws it: while a turn is being started for it (the agent process attaching, the checkpoint, the hand-over), the prompt is the last `message` of the `transcript` node with `sending: true`, under the id the sent prompt gets (`<session>/msg/user/prompt-<turn>`), so only the flag goes when it lands (in tests `CLUBMATE_AGENTS_TAKE_HOLD` holds the plugin before it takes a composer send, `CLUBMATE_AGENTS_SEND_HOLD` before the prompt goes to the agent); a message sent while a turn runs on an agent without steering is a "Queued" row above the composer instead. A prompt that could not start goes back to that queue ("Queued, not sent"). Claude Code reads a slash command only from the last text block of a prompt, so a prompt whose first word is `/` and a name from the agent's `commands` (`/compact`, `/review main`) goes with its text as the last block: the finish mode, orchestrator and AFK rules, the attachments and the framing come before it, and a sender's "[From …]" line is a block of its own before it. Any other text that starts with `/` (a path) keeps the usual order. The phone keeps its own echo (see [Remote § Phone outbox](remote.md#phone-outbox)); `agents.transcript` lists only sent prompts.

## Jumping to a turn

`agents.reveal {session, turn}` opens the session in Agents and scrolls its transcript on the Mac so that turn's prompt is at the top (`anchor` on the `transcript` node, see [SDUI protocol](sdui-protocol.md)); it stays there until you scroll, another turn is revealed or the session starts a new turn; revealing the same turn again scrolls back to it. Every `agents.turn` event carries its `turn` number, so a caller can jump to the turn an event was about. The phone's native session screen ignores it; its SDUI transcript scrolls to `anchor` like the Mac.

## Docs in the transcript

A tool call is a doc when its status is `completed` and either its kind is `edit` (Write, Edit, MultiEdit) and one of its `locations` or `diff` paths ends in `.md`, `.markdown`, `.mmd`, `.html` or `.htm` (case-insensitive, `pkg/docext`), or it has a `resource_link` whose `file://` URI does. The agents plugin decides this once and sends the first such path, absolute against the session's worktree, as the entry's `doc` field in `agents.transcript`; a failed, cancelled or still running call, a delete or move, and a call on other files have none. The Mac card gets an Open action (before Open in nvim) and the phone's tool row an Open button; both show the doc in the [docs viewer](docs-viewer.md) — the Mac through `openDoc` on `agents.main`, which runs `docs.open {path, session}`, the phone with `docs.read`. Like the rest of a call, Open is inside the folded actions row until you open it. Neither client checks the path itself.

An agent message links the docs it names. When a turn ends, the plugin reads each of that turn's `agent` messages as Markdown and takes every link target that has no URL scheme (`http(s)`, `mailto`, `file:` … are skipped) and every inline code span without whitespace; fenced code is not read. A candidate's `#anchor` is set aside and the rest is percent-decoded, `~/` expanded and joined to the session's worktree when relative; it is kept when that is an existing regular file ending in a doc extension (`pkg/docext`). The entry gets `docLinks: {"<target or code span as written>": "<absolute path>[#anchor]"}` in `agents.transcript` and as the `message` node's `docLinks` prop in `agents.main` (omitted when empty); a message still streaming has none, a replayed transcript resolves them again on load, and thoughts and subagent messages have none. On the Mac a click sends `openDoc` with the value on the message node, which runs `docs.open {path, session}`; the phone's native transcript opens the doc sheet with `docs.read`, and its SDUI transcript sends `openDoc` like the Mac. The same pass gives the entry `docCards: [{path, kind, title, rel, presented?}]` (in `agents.transcript` and as the `message` node's prop, omitted when empty): one per distinct doc, the presented ones first, each group in order of first mention, at most 8, `path` absolute without the `#anchor` (two mentions that differ only in the anchor are one card), `kind` `markdown`, `mermaid` (`.mmd`) or `artifact` (`.html`, `.htm`), `title` the first `# ` heading of a Markdown file or the `<title>` of an artifact, read from the file's first 64 KiB (`pkg/docext`, the docs viewer's title too), else the file name without its extension, and `rel` the path relative to the session's worktree (absolute when outside it). Clicking or tapping a card opens its doc like a link. A doc is presented when the agent called `docs_present` or `docs_open` on it during that turn: the plugin listens to `docs.presented` and `docs.opened` (see [docs viewer](docs-viewer.md#bus)) and writes each as a `doc_presented {path, title?}` event in the session's log at the session's current turn (a doc a Done gate queued at the turn that queued it), so a replayed transcript keeps it; one that arrives after the turn ended links that turn again. A presented doc's card gets `presented: true` and the `title` passed to `docs_present` when there is one; a presented doc that none of the turn's messages names is added as a card to the turn's last agent message (a turn without an agent message shows none). Only presented cards are drawn open (§ Transcript folding). These links do not add the doc to `agents.docs`. Clubmate's agent instructions and the `docs_open`/`docs_present` tools ask agents to mention docs freely as a link or `path` and to present only the few the user should look at. Any other link in agent Markdown stays a link on the Mac and the phone only for `http`, `https` and `mailto` (`MarkdownLinkPolicy` in `ClubmateProtocol`, applied by `Markdown.inline`); other schemes (`shortcuts:`, `tel:`, app deep links, a hand-written `clubmate-doc:`) show as plain text, and the phone's URL elicitation Open opens only those schemes too.

An agent message shows the images it names. The same pass over a finished turn's `agent` messages keeps every candidate (link target or code span, resolved the same way) without an `#anchor` that is an existing regular file ending in `.png`, `.jpg`, `.jpeg`, `.gif` or `.webp` (case-insensitive) after symlinks are resolved, and gives the entry `images: [{path, rel, width, height}]` (in `agents.transcript` and as the `message` node's prop, omitted when empty): one per distinct path in order of first mention, at most 4, `path` absolute, `rel` as for `docCards`, `width`/`height` the pixel size when the file's header says it (png, jpeg, gif; omitted otherwise). An image is never a doc link or card; the entry's `imageLinks` maps each target written in the message that names one of its `images` (exactly as written) to that image's `path`, and the client draws it as a link in the accent colour, like a doc link. On the Mac and the phone the images are thumbnails under the message, side by side and wrapping (Mac 4 rows tall, phone 112 pt): the image cropped to the tile from its top, the tile as wide as the image's aspect wants between square and twice its height, a hairline border, a spinner while it loads, the file name under it (one line, middle-cut) and `rel` as its tooltip on the Mac. A click or tap on a thumbnail or on an image link opens the image in a viewer: on the Mac a sheet sized to the window with the file name and `rel`, zoom out / Fit / zoom in and Close (Esc), on the phone full screen (pinch, double tap to zoom, a close button). Nothing folds and nothing opens itself; the thumbnail is the folded state. The Mac reads the file and reads it again once it changed; the phone fetches it per message when the thumbnail scrolls into view (again after a failure, after the newest 48 images or 48 MiB pushed it out, or after the session left the four the phone keeps, see [Long transcripts](#long-transcripts); a thumbnail on screen is never pushed out), so a later message naming a rewritten file shows the new one, and asks for it with `agents.image {session, path, side?}` (not an agent tool, remote), which returns `{mimeType, data, scaled?}` (base64) only for a path an `images` entry of that session names, and fails for files over 8 MiB, for a path that no longer resolves to an image file name and for bytes that do not sniff as `image/*`. With `side` (pixels, clamped to 64–1024) the Mac sends a reduced copy and `scaled: true`: the image turned upright by its EXIF orientation, scaled so its shorter side is `side` and its longer side at most 4 × `side`, as JPEG, or as PNG when a pixel is not opaque (a gif gives its first frame); it keeps the last 64 copies by path, modification time, size and `side`, and makes one at a time. An image already that small, a webp, one over 64 Mpx or one that does not decode comes back as it is without `scaled`. The phone's thumbnail asks for `side` = 112 pt × screen scale and the viewer for the whole file, cached apart; a reply without `scaled` (an older Mac, a small image) is kept as the whole file too, and the viewer shows the thumbnail with a spinner until the whole file arrives. `images[].width`/`height` are the upright size (swapped for EXIF orientations 5–8). A file that is gone shows a photo glyph and "Image not found" in its thumbnail and in the viewer.

`agents.docs {session?}` (not an agent tool, remote) lists those docs as `{docs: [{path, session, at}]}`: one per path at its latest `at` (when that call began), newest first, from that session and its subagents' calls, or without `session` from every session of the workspace. A session whose transcript is loaded answers from it. Each session record keeps a doc index (`docIndex`: each doc's path and latest `at`, with the size of the session log and the worktree it was built from), rebuilt when the transcript loads and when it is unloaded as idle and saved without an `agents.changed`; an unloaded session answers from that index while the log size and worktree still match, without reading its log. A session with no index or a stale one (a record from before the index, a log written after the last unload, e.g. before a crash) is read once, indexed and unloaded again, so the reply is the same as reading every transcript. `agents.sessions` rows and `agents.transcript`'s `session` leave `docIndex` out. `docs.list` builds the docs lists on it (see [docs viewer](docs-viewer.md#docs-list)).

When an agent opens a doc with `docs_open`, the phone shows "Opened <file name>" with an Open button above the composer of that session's screen, for 8 s or until tapped; on any other screen it shows nothing.

### Task links

An agent message that writes `#601` links task 601 of the session's workspace backlog. At each turn end the plugin finds the `#N` tokens in the turn's `agent` messages (the rule of `docrender.TaskIDs`, the same one the doc viewer links tasks with: outside inline code, code blocks and links, `#` not after a letter, digit, `_`, `&`, `#` or `/`, 1–9 digits not followed by one), and when there are any it asks the backlog once with `backlog.list {since: "", slim: true}` (every status) outside the session lock, coalesced per workspace; the entry's `taskLinks` maps each id that is a task to its title (in `agents.transcript` and as the `message` node's prop, omitted when empty), so `PR #3` stays text when there is no task 3. When an entry's links change the session gets an `agents.changed` of kind `links`, which re-renders the Mac transcript and makes the phone refetch it. User messages, thoughts and tool output never get links. The links are not written to the log: loading a transcript (a restart, a session that was unloaded, an old session) runs the same lookup once, so old transcripts get links too, a renamed task shows its new title and a deleted one loses its link. A lookup that fails (the backlog plugin is not up yet) leaves the links as they were until the next turn end or load.

On the Mac a task link is drawn like a doc link with the title as its tooltip; a click sends `openDoc` with `/task/N`, and the plugin runs `backlog.reveal {id}`, which selects the task, makes it visible and focuses the Backlog section. A task deleted since answers "task not found" and the click does nothing. On the phone a tap opens that task's screen in the Backlog of the session's workspace, refetching the backlog first when the phone has no copy of that task; a task the refetched backlog does not have does nothing, and the task screen reads "Task Not Found" for an id the fetched backlog lacks.

## CLI

Inside a Clubmate terminal, `clubmate` is on `PATH` and `$CLUBMATE_WORKSPACE_ID` names the current workspace.

```sh
clubmate open src/main.go:42                       # open in the editor
clubmate command run terminal.new                  # any command or capability
clubmate command run session.read id=ses_123 bytes=4000
clubmate command run terminals.close --agent sessionId=ses_123
clubmate session send ses_123 "make test" --enter
clubmate session read ses_123
```

`key=value` arguments are typed by the command's schema (arrays are comma-separated); `--args '{"…": …}'` takes JSON instead. `--agent` runs the command as an agent would, with the same limits and approvals. The CLI never turns it on by itself.

`clubmate agent` wraps the agent-session commands and always runs them as an agent call, so a session started from an agent session's shell is its child:

```sh
clubmate agent spawn "fix the flaky test" --branch fix/flaky   # agents.new; without --branch it shares your worktree
clubmate agent tell agt_123 "also update the docs"             # agents.prompt
clubmate agent wait agt_123 agt_456 --timeout 300              # agents.wait
clubmate agent output agt_123 --from-turn 2                    # agents.transcript
clubmate agent sessions                                        # agents.sessions
clubmate agent cancel agt_123                                  # agents.cancel
clubmate agent blame src/main.go -L 10,40                       # agents.blame; or a commit: clubmate agent blame HEAD~2
clubmate agent trace main..HEAD                                # agents.trace; default HEAD
clubmate agent export agt_123                                  # agents.export into ./agt_123.clubmate-session
clubmate agent import agt_123.clubmate-session --branch more   # agents.import; without --branch it continues in your worktree
```

A reviewer is a child in your own worktree: `clubmate agent spawn "Review git diff main..HEAD read-only: report bugs, change nothing"` (or `agents_new` without `branch` over MCP), `wait` for it, `tell` it one round of follow-ups, then fix what it found yourself.

## History and attribution

A turn is the unit of history. Before each prompt Clubmate pins a checkpoint of the worktree at `refs/clubmate/cp/<session>/<turn>` (its parent is `HEAD` at turn start), and when the turn ends another one at `refs/clubmate/cp/<session>/<turn>.end`; both carry a `Clubmate-Worktree: <path>` trailer. When the turn ends, the commits it added to the current branch get a git note in `refs/notes/clubmate`:

```
Clubmate-Session: agt_4c67f4de54b9ae47
Clubmate-Turn: 3
Clubmate-Agent: claude
Clubmate-Title: Fix the flaky test
Clubmate-Prompt: <first line of the turn's prompt>
Clubmate-Checkpoint: <checkpoint sha>
```

The turn's commits are those reachable from `HEAD` but not from its start or from any other branch (local or remote), so rebasing onto `main`, merging `main` or fast-forwarding a child's branch claims nothing that is already elsewhere; a squash or merge commit belongs to the turn that made it. A commit keeps its first note. The transcript lists them per turn (`turns[].commits`).

Read them with `git log --notes=clubmate`, `agents.blame` or `clubmate agent blame <file|commit> [-L a,b]` (git blame of the file, only line ranges whose commit has a note).

Limits:
- `git merge --squash` into `main` makes a new commit, noted with the session that ran the squash (the orchestrator), not the child whose branch it was; the child's notes stay on its branch commits. For a commit `git_land` made, `agents.blame` also returns `builder`, the child whose branch landed, from the outcomes journal (see [Outcomes](#outcomes)).
- Commits the user makes in the same worktree during an agent's turn, or pulls into its branch from that branch's remote, are noted with that turn.
- Rebase and amend drop the notes unless the repo sets `git config notes.rewriteRef refs/notes/clubmate`; Clubmate doesn't set it.
- Notes are local; push `refs/notes/clubmate` to share them, but they carry each session's title and the first line of its prompts, so check them before pushing.

### Outcomes

Clubmate keeps the data to judge a child by **cost to landing**: the builder's cost, plus the cost of the land-gate reviewers of its landing, plus its fixers.

The outcomes journal is append-only JSON lines in the agents plugin's state dir, `outcomes/<YYYY-MM>.jsonl`; a month's file is deleted 90 days after the month ends. It holds:
- `verdict`: every land-gate verdict, as the gate's history has it (the gate's own history keeps only its last 100).
- `landing`: every `git_land` by an agent: `lander` (the caller), `builder` (the lander when it lands with mode index or works in the landed worktree itself, with no fixers; otherwise, of the sessions on the landed worktree, asides and gate nodes aside, the newest whose parent is not also there), `fixers` (the other such sessions on that worktree and the builder's own children), `squash`, `into`, `source` and each one's cost at that moment.
- `session`: a session's facts whenever it is closed or deleted, by the user or by Clubmate, written again only when they changed (the last entry wins), so they outlive its log (deleted after 30 days): title, parent, `kind`, `size`, `arm` and `armBy` (see below), model and effort (the session's current config value, so an inherited model shows too), created, last turn end, turns and cost.

A session's cost is its running `usage_update.cost` total in USD; a total that drops (a new agent process) adds on from zero.

The model experiment compares models by cost to landing, one factor at a time and assigned at random. `agents.new` takes `kind` (`feature`, `fix`, `refactor`, `docs`, `test`, `ui-check`, `review`, `spike`) and `size` (`S` up to 60 tool calls, `M` 60–150, `L` more) and `model: "auto"`: Clubmate then picks one of the arms (`agents.model-arms`, default `["opus", "sonnet"]`; only you set it, `arms` a list of model names) from a hash of the new session's id, so the arms split evenly within every kind × size, and starts the session on it as if the caller had named it. The session record keeps `exp {kind, size, arm, armBy}` (shown in `agents.sessions`): `armBy` is `auto` for a picked model and `caller` for one the caller named (which doesn't count in the comparison); a session started without them falls back to an `[exp kind=… size=… arm=…]` first line of its starting prompt, with no `armBy`.

`agents.outcomes {since?, workspace?}` (an agent caller sees only its own workspace and no night sessions) lists children of agent sessions and builders of landings, oldest first: the facts above, `wallSeconds` (created to last turn end), and for a builder its latest `landing` with `rounds` (reviewer verdicts; docs-only and user approvals are listed in `verdicts` but are no round), `changes`, `verdicts` and `reviewers` (the gate nodes of that lander, target and source started since the previous such landing), `reviewersCost`, `fixersCost` and `costToLanding`. A live session's numbers come from its log, a deleted one's from the journal. Beside them, `arms` sums up the listed sessions with `armBy: "auto"` per kind × size × arm (a missing kind or size is `unknown`): `n`, `landed`, `medianCostToLanding` and `meanCostToLanding` of the landed ones, `changesShare` (`changes` verdicts among all verdicts of their landings) and `medianWallSeconds`.

### Agent Trace

`agents.trace rev=<commit|a..b>` (`clubmate agent trace`) returns one [Agent Trace](https://agent-trace.dev/) record (version `0.1`; the spec's own example says `0.1.0`, its schema allows only `major.minor`) per non-merge commit, oldest first, at most 200. Each changed file lists its changed lines grouped by contributor:

- `ai`, with `url` `clubmate://session/<id>#turn=<n>`: written during that agent turn;
- `human`: changed while no agent turn ran in the worktree;
- `unknown`: Clubmate can't tell (a turn still running, or one from before end-of-turn checkpoints existed, two turns running at once, or a commit with no Clubmate history at all).

How: Clubmate replays the commit's parent, the checkpoints of the commit's worktree whose parent is that commit (plus the checkpoint named in the commit's note), and the commit itself as a throwaway chain of commits, then runs `git blame` on the changed lines. A line is credited to the step that introduced it: the step into `<turn>.end` is the turn, the step into a turn's start checkpoint happened between turns, and the last step belongs to the commit's noted turn (when the commit has no note it is `unknown` if the commit has no checkpoints at all, else human with no turn open, the turn when the one open turn has ended, and `unknown` otherwise). The record `id` is derived from the commit, so exporting twice gives the same record.

Limits: edits the user makes in nvim while a turn runs count as the turn's; two sessions working in one worktree at once make their overlap `unknown`; a squash commit is credited to the session that ran the squash; `model_id` and `content_hash` are not filled.

### Resuming elsewhere

Built (one machine): `agents.export session=… dir=…` (`clubmate agent export <session> [dir]`) writes a bundle directory: `session.json` (the record), `log.jsonl` (Clubmate's log) and `refs.bundle` (a git bundle of the session's checkpoint refs and the notes of the commits its turns made, without commits already on a branch, remote or tag; other sessions' notes stay out). It refuses a running session and an existing `dir`. `agents.import dir=… [branch=…] [note=…]` (`clubmate agent import <dir>`) fetches the checkpoint refs into the repository (never overwriting existing ones) and adds the bundle's notes that name the imported session and one of its checkpoints, on commits the repository has and that have no note yet, adds a closed copy of the session with the original transcript (`importedFrom`), and starts a new session in the caller's worktree (or a new `.worktrees/<branch>`) whose first prompt is the handoff brief of the copy. The copy and the new session link like a handoff. This works across worktrees, workspaces and clones on the same Mac; the bundle's checkpoints need their parent commits in the target repository (fetch the session's branch first).

Design (not built): continuing the agent's own conversation instead of a brief. Claude Code keeps it in `~/.claude/projects/<encoded cwd>/<agentSessionId>.jsonl`; the bundle would carry that file (found by globbing for the id), import would place it under the target cwd's project directory and `session/resume` with the old `agentSessionId`, falling back to the brief when resume fails. On another machine the same bundle works once the branch is pushed and fetched; a relay or shared store for bundles belongs with the mobile companion work.

## Home reads

An agent in Home can read other workspaces. It gets the workspace ids from `workspace_list` and passes one as `workspace` to a read-only tool: a command with `"readOnly": true` in its manifest, which changes nothing. Today these are `workspace.list`, `session.list`, `session.read`, `process.tree`, `terminals.busy`, `backlog.list`, `backlog.get`, `wiki.list`, `wiki.read`, `wiki.search`, `agents.sessions`, `agents.transcript` and `agents.search`.

- Any other tool with another workspace is refused: "workspace … is not this session's workspace (…); from Home only read tools reach other workspaces".
- The rule works only from Home outward. An agent in a project that passes Home's id, or the id of a third workspace, is refused as before.
- It applies to every caller in a Home terminal with `$CLUBMATE_WORKSPACE_ID` set, also a plain `claude`. The MCP server asks `workspace.list` once whether its workspace is Home and keeps the answer.
- The core checks it again: an agent session (`agt_…`) that is not in the target workspace passes only for a `readOnly` command and only when the agents plugin lists it in the `home: true` workspace. The same holds for `session.read` on a session of another workspace.

## MCP (Claude Code and other MCP clients)

`clubmate mcp` is a stdio MCP server over the same registry. Every call it makes is an agent call.

Its `initialize` instructions end with "Working in Clubmate" (`internal/mcp/clubmate.md`) and "Tasks across repos" (`internal/mcp/clubmate_repos.md`), both embedded in the binary: split a task, run independent parts as child sessions with `agents_new` and a branch, collect them with `agents_wait`, and use `cron_add` and `triggers_add` instead of polling. Every MCP client that connects gets it, in any project.

- Tools are named after the command or capability with `.` and `-` replaced by `_` (`editor_open`, `workspace_list`, `terminal_close`). A plugin command that provides a capability appears under the capability's name only.
- Every tool takes an optional `workspace` id. It defaults to `$CLUBMATE_WORKSPACE_ID`, then the most recently opened workspace. With `$CLUBMATE_WORKSPACE_ID` set, any other workspace is refused ("workspace … is not this session's workspace (…); agents act only in their own workspace"): an agent acts only in its own workspace. The one exception is an agent in Home, see [Home reads](#home-reads).
- If Clubmate isn't running, the tools still list the core primitives, and calls fail with "Clubmate isn't running". The server never starts Clubmate itself.

To connect Claude Code, use **Settings → Agents → Connect Claude Code**. It runs:

```sh
claude mcp add --scope user clubmate -- "/Applications/Clubmate.app/Contents/Resources/bin/clubmate" mcp
```

Development builds register the server as `clubmate-dev` instead, so both can be connected at once. The button is shown only when `claude` is on your login shell's `PATH`; Clubmate looks once per launch, so restart Clubmate after installing `claude`. It says "Connected" only when the registered server runs this copy of Clubmate; if it runs another one, it shows that command and offers "Reconnect" (remove, then add; if the remove fails, nothing is added). Clubmate never edits Claude's config files itself.
